3:T6f24d,<h2 class="heading_2">Part 1</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">DURING THE BREAKS and lunchtime, I meticulously used that time to speak individually with the anxious students of Class A, requesting a discussion after school and securing their permission.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to let them blow off some steam, even if just a little, so the students who&apos;d been complaining, led by Ike-kun and Shinohara-san, wouldn&apos;t still be agitated when it was time for the after-school discussion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun said it was an overreaction, but when I saw Chabashira-sensei, who was just as cold as she&apos;d been that morning, I became convinced that I&apos;d done the right thing by acting in advance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Horikita. As I told you during the lunch break, you have a matter in the Student Council room in one hour. I don&apos;t mind you having a discussion, but don&apos;t forget.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, I understand.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And just as I&apos;d expected, Sensei left the classroom after coming over to me and saying just that, without ever touching on the details of the special exam during the afternoon homeroom. After seeing her off, I made my move. I wanted to avoid a repeat of the morning&apos;s complaint session.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I quickly stepped up to the teacher&#x2019;s podium, another student began to move.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He was the one person I&apos;d identified as a potential problem but hadn&apos;t spoken to.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That guy...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun, who muttered quietly nearby, clicked his tongue and turned around.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The student who drew a conspicuous amount of attention, Koenji-kun, stood up from his seat alone and quickly left the classroom.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey, Koenji&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun immediately called out to him in a reprimanding tone, but he walked away without turning back or even stopping for a moment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was normal. He wouldn&apos;t interfere with the discussion, but at the same time, he wouldn&apos;t cooperate either.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I knew that, so I didn&apos;t even try to talk to him before school let out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I resigned myself to the fact that asking for his cooperation would be more trouble than it was worth and would likely just lead to a fight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Essentially, Sudou-kun, the only one who called out to him, must have felt the same way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if to prove it, he too lost interest in Koenji-kun&apos;s disappearing form.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was fine. We could just start the discussion as usual without him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The discussion would proceed more smoothly that way, and problems were less likely to arise.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if it was really alright to keep letting this slide, dismissing it as <em>normal</em>.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hirata-kun, I&apos;m sorry, but could you get started without me? I&apos;ll be right back.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, I understand.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I entrusted the room to Hirata-kun, who readily agreed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even though I knew it was pointless to plead with him, I hurried out into the hallway to chase after Koenji-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I followed him at a light jog down the hallway, where he was presumably heading for the shoe lockers.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And when I spotted the large back of the person I was after, I quickly closed the distance and fell in step beside him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Would you mind waiting a moment?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I called out, looking up at his tall figure from close by.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#xa0;&quot;Well, well, Horikita Girl. Is there something I can do for you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Koenji-kun glanced at me once, but he didn&apos;t so much as turn his head, his face remaining pointed straight toward his destination.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I struggled to match my steps to his long strides as I continued to follow.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just want you to at least hear me out.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t mind listening a bit, as long as we&apos;re walking.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not it. I want you to return to the classroom right now and listen to the discussion.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a modest request.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Koenji-kun didn&apos;t react extravagantly, just flashed a smile that showed his white teeth.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wouldn&apos;t that simply be pointless? My time is quite priceless, you see. Besides, you and I have an established contract. I&apos;m under no obligation to lend you a hand, am I?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, I am aware of that. But all I&apos;m asking is for you to listen to the discussion. I&apos;m not asking for your cooperation to win the special exam. I won&apos;t even ask you to say anything.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I were to ask him to use his potential to contribute to our victory, he would obviously refuse. I recognized the fact that he had every right to do so.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;All you have to do is sit there until we&apos;re dismissed. It&apos;s simple, isn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You seem to be making a passionate case, but I&apos;m afraid I don&apos;t follow. For what purpose, exactly?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s necessary for the class to unite once again. I don&apos;t want you to disrupt that harmony. If even one person breaks the rules, it affects the entire class&apos;s morale.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My justification and my appeal. Surely, at least some of it had to have gotten through to Koenji-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The next expression to appear on the profile I&apos;d been staring up at was a scornful laugh.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph. What a curious thing to say. If the class falls apart simply because I&apos;ve left, that just goes to show the limits of your own abilities, Horikita Girl.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This has nothing to do with my abilities. If there&apos;s even one student doing as they please, we can hardly be described as &apos;united,&apos; can we? If even a single piece is missing, the whole thing is incomplete.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see. It seems you&apos;re capable of a somewhat amusing answer. However, you said &apos;once again,&apos; but has the class, excluding myself, ever truly been united as one?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My expression stiffened for a moment at his merciless words, and I stopped in my tracks.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But I knew I couldn&apos;t let Koenji-kun walk away with a rebuttal like that, so I immediately resumed walking.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Saying we&apos;ve never been united is a bit of an overstatement, don&apos;t you think? Over the past two years, while not every time, there have been more than a few occasions when our class came together as one. That&apos;s why we&apos;re in Class A now.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We overcame hardships together. It might not have been something to boast about to others, but a fact was a fact.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You should add &apos;in your mind&apos; to that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And you should add, &apos;not in yours.&apos;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if it was just a tit-for-tat exchange, I couldn&apos;t back down, so I pressed on even if I had to force it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Being in Class A is proof of unity? The only reason the class was lucky enough to win was because Ayanokouji-boy was making his moves. Surely you understand that without me having to tell you, don&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...It&apos;s true that Ayanokouji-kun left our class. But we didn&apos;t win through his power alone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you truly believe that, then that&apos;s quite the comical tale. You talk of unity, of being as one, but aren&apos;t you just trying to convince yourself that you can win even without Ayanokouji-boy?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">His merciless words felt like a blade stabbing me in the back.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s no point in me returning to the classroom now. No matter how much you struggle, as long as you&apos;re the leader, a victory against the other classes is impossible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He pointed out my lack of ability without sugarcoating it in the slightest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m... not good enough?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ichinose-san, who earned enough trust to unite her class. Ryuuen-kun, who brought his class together through fear and violence. And Sakayanagi-san, who produced results and earned recognition with her exceptional abilities.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ayanokouji-kun, who&apos;d been accepted by the class in the blink of an eye with his overwhelming ability&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I couldn&apos;t deny that there was a difference when I compared them to myself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, that might be true. But that doesn&apos;t mean it&apos;s okay for me to just stand still here, does it? I have to start with what I can do. Precisely in order to strengthen our imperfect unity&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I won&apos;t deny that, in itself. It is your freedom to do as you please. And, as I have neither reason nor obligation to follow the class&apos;s instructions, I am also free to persist in my non-participation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re an excellent student; I know that. But with Ayanokouji-kun now on the enemy&apos;s side, our class&apos;s position as Class A will be threatened before long. You&apos;re fine with that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without his cooperation, we might not be able to graduate from Class A.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to see if Koenji-kun was prepared for that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">His behavior... So as not to miss any sign that he was acting differently than usual, I focused my gaze from his head to toe.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But even under my testing gaze, Koenji-kun&apos;s attitude didn&apos;t change at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In fact, he just laughed back at me, amused.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In the end, it seems you&apos;re the same.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without slowing his pace in the slightest, Koenji-kun continued to distance himself from the classroom.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...The same? What&apos;s the same?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I speak of this cheap ploy to stoke my sense of crisis and desperately try to make me move.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Does that mean someone besides me has already tried to do the same thing?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was there anyone in the class now who would blame Koenji-kun for his selfish behavior? Even Sudou-kun would only give him a light warning, and most students avoided even speaking to him. Hirata-kun might have tried to talk to him, but he would never resort to threats.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now, who&apos;s to say?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While I was trying to figure it out, I realized we were almost at the school entrance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Chasing after me any further is a waste of your time, so I suggest you stop.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I&apos;d like to take your advice, but I simply can&apos;t.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I would pursue him to the very end.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Until Koenji-kun admitted defeat and said he would return to the classroom.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I certainly felt a new resolve ignite within me, but that flame was extinguished in an instant.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Advice? What a misguided girl you are. Unfortunately, this isn&apos;t advice. It&apos;s a warning.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hk...!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I caught the look from Koenji-kun, and meeting his eyes, I gasped.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was an irritation from Koenji-kun that he rarely ever showed, tinged with a faint killing intent.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a particular glint in his eyes, different from what someone like Ryuuen-kun, who constantly relied on intimidation, would show.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you planning to make an enemy of me too, Horikita-girl?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He was not an ally, but he was neutral.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This was a warning that if I intended to make him abandon that stance, he would show me no mercy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I have no such intention.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My resolve vanished in an instant, and I stopped. I had no other choice.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Failing to gain his cooperation was one thing, but making an enemy of him was pointless under any circumstances.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He was, for better or for worse, a troublemaker. If he felt like it, he probably wouldn&apos;t think twice about letting the class fall apart.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Good. Well then, I shall be taking my leave.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I felt a gnawing frustration, but ultimately, I was still powerless to stop Koenji-kun from walking away.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 2</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WITH THE CLASS discussion at a standstill, I left things to Hirata-kun and the others, and hurried to the Student Council room to attend my duties.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d hoped to settle on a direction, at the very least, but that wish wasn&apos;t granted.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Truthfully, I wanted to discuss it until we were all satisfied...but it couldn&apos;t be helped. Having accepted the role of student council president, I had to tackle those responsibilities with the same diligence as the special exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I arrived in front of the Student Council room, Nanase-san was already there and bowed her head.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Good work, Student Council President Horikita.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re quite early.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While exchanging pleasantries, we entered the still-empty Student Council room together. Then, as I took my seat at the president&apos;s desk, Nanase-san stood beside me and showed me two printed sheets of paper.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just received these materials from Mr. Houjou, the homeroom teacher for Class 1-A.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Each paper contained information on a single student.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One was Kusanagi Minato-kun from Class 1-A. The other was Maki Yuuma-kun from Class 1-D.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And here is an overview of the incident.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After looking over their profiles, she handed me another sheet of paper.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t particularly complicated, and a quick glance was all I needed to grasp the contents.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;These two got into a big fight, is that it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The date was last Friday, and the location was behind the first-year dormitories.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Maki-kun from Class D called out Kusanagi-kun from Class A, and after a verbal dispute, it escalated into a fight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Both struck each other multiple times, including blows to the face. Maki-kun&apos;s injuries would take about three weeks to heal, Kusanagi-kun&apos;s about one. The fight came to light when a third party discovered them as they were returning from behind the dorms. Fortunately, they&apos;ve been able to attend school since Monday&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it was behind the dorms, then there&apos;s no footage of them fighting, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s right. It was in a blind spot for the security cameras. It seems a third party who was also returning to the dorms discovered the two injured students as they were about to head back.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Where the cameras are, and where they aren&apos;t.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seems even the first-years, who haven&apos;t been here long, are getting a grasp of such things.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems we should ask the school to install more security cameras.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I agree. I did try to briefly ask Houjou-sensei&#xa0;about it, but he told me to hear the details from the students and didn&apos;t elaborate.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To maintain fairness, I suppose. That&apos;s likely the reason they&apos;ve tossed the judgment of this case over to us in the student council.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san, who nodded firmly, seemed to have something on her mind, and began to speak.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Actually, last year, I was also called to the student council for a similar incident.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You, Nanase-san? Were you assaulted by someone?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, that&apos;s not it. It was a problem caused by a classmate&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After a slight, wry smile, Nanase-san explained the details.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Before last summer, when Housen Kazuomi-kun of Class D and Utomiya Riku-kun of Class C passed each other in the hallway, a trivial matter led to an argument, and a tense standoff ensued where they glared at each other from point-blank range. Just when it seemed a fight could break out at any moment, students from their respective classes who were nearby started to argue, and a Class C student, having reached their limit, struck a Class D student. Then, riding that momentum, they charged at Housen-kun and tried to punch him, only to be spectacularly countered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Just as Utomiya-kun was about to throw a punch, the teachers happened to pass by and the commotion was somehow settled, but afterward, the problem was brought before the student council.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The student Housen-kun had struck, suffered injuries that took about three weeks to heal. However, the Class C student threw the first punch and also hit another student. Deciding who was at fault and how to judge the matter dragged on, requiring numerous discussions and taking two weeks to resolve. Nanase-san shared all of the details with me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nagumo-senpai must have had a tough time.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I think so, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though exasperated, I couldn&apos;t help but laugh.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Was it that funny of a story?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, that&apos;s not it. It&apos;s just that the same kinds of trouble happen everywhere, and I had a similar experience in my first year.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is...that so?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It was similar to your situation. A classmate of mine got into a fight with one of Ryuuen-kun&apos;s classmates.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At that time, my brother was sitting in the very seat I occupied.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun struck a student in the special building and a discussion was held to determine responsibility, such as who threw the first punch. My brother didn&apos;t unilaterally take anyone&apos;s side, but fulfilled his duty as student council president with an impartial eye. I&apos;m sure Nagumo-senpai did the same last year.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s almost become an annual tradition for the new students to cause trouble at least once, and when I imagined it like that, I found it kind of amusing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hearing your story makes it feel more real. I can imagine something similar happening here again next year.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In that case, it will be your turn to pass judgment as student council president.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Eh, m-me?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If things proceed as they are, that&apos;s what will happen. Will you do it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I hope I&apos;m up to the task... Besides, they say all the past student council presidents have graduated from Class A... and I&apos;m currently in Class D.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san answered without much confidence, but she was without a doubt the leading candidate.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;d do best not to worry about precedents born from chance. Until now, the student council president could only come from Class A or B, yet here I am, serving in the position despite having started in Class D. And while this is a good example, there&apos;s no guarantee that I will even be in Class A at graduation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The current Class A was also a temporary position.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a position we wouldn&apos;t have reached without Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s help.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I believe you, at least, are more than qualified to become the student council president. Have some confidence.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Thank you very much. For now, I&apos;d like to focus on producing solid results as a secretary.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though hesitant, Nanase-san accepted my words earnestly, nodding and lowering her head.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The fact that their fight was brought to the student council is proof that this is a considerable dispute. I&apos;m going to share a few of my thoughts before they arrive. I want you to lend a hand depending on the situation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One of them was a Class A student. I had the impression that students prone to this kind of violence tended to be concentrated in Class D or Class C, but this time was different. Kusanagi-kun not only had high physical ability of B+, but his academic ability was also a B+.&#xa0;I wanted to consider the possibility that he was quick-witted and take the measures I could.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the time we had left, I verbally explained a few plans to Nanase-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Soon, when the scheduled time came, a dry knock sounded at the student council room door.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems they&apos;ve arrived. Please, come in.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At Nanase-san&apos;s reply, two male students appeared, glaring at each other.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Both had unique hairstyles and an appearance that would make anyone think of them as delinquents.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They showed no signs of remorse whatsoever and seemed to think the other was entirely responsible.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But what caught my eye the most was not their hairstyles or attitudes, but their faces.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Those are some... pretty bad injuries.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The injuries were so bad that even Nanase-san couldn&apos;t help but comment on them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The one standing to my left was the first-year Class D student, Maki-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The one standing to my right was the first-year Class A student, Kusanagi-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d heard that even minor injuries could be diagnosed as taking two or three weeks to fully heal, but they looked quite battered. They must have really gone at it, as both of their faces were swollen. Maki-kun&apos;s injuries were particularly bad, making it clear who got the upper hand in the fight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I assume you both know why you&apos;ve been summoned to the student council room?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san coaxed them, as if guiding them to explain voluntarily.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nah, I got no idea. Why the hell do I gotta get called in here? This guy&apos;s the one at fault.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kusanagi-kun immediately retorted, turning to Maki-kun and baring his teeth.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Don&apos;t fuck with me! You&apos;re the one who threw the first punch!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Bullshit! You&apos;re the one who came at me first. Well, you missed, though!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A loud shouting match began over who made the first move and who threw the first punch.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san and I exchanged a quick glance and listened to the boys&apos; argument for a while.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We judged that it would be easier later if they blew off some steam first.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At first, they just tried to pin the blame on each other for starting the fight, but it gradually devolved into unnecessary verbal abuse, and eventually escalated to irrelevant topics like height and appearance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;How childish.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Childish, indeed.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even though they were only just first-year high school students, they were starting to cross a line that couldn&apos;t be ignored. What&apos;s more, they showed no signs of calming down; on the contrary, they looked like they were about to start brawling right there in the student council room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s quite enough noise. I&apos;m going to have to ask you both to be quiet for a moment.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san, unable to watch any longer, warned them as such, but they merely glanced at her and didn&apos;t stop their tirade.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They were clearly underestimating her because she was a girl. That attitude was plain to see.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But as long as I knew Nanase-san&apos;s words reached them, that was enough.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Their complaining was simply a matter of mutual dissatisfaction.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In that case, all I had to do was prepare to hand down a ruling that would be most dissatisfying for them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I sent a look to Nanase-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Our eyes met, and she gave a small nod, having perfectly grasped my intentions.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you&apos;re going to continue arguing as you please, then this meeting is unnecessary. We may have to impose a heavy penalty on both of you. Is that acceptable?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As Nanase-san spoke with a cold, dismissive tone, I silently pulled back my chair and stood up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Hold on, there&apos;s no way I&apos;m gonna accept that!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This ain&apos;t got nothing to do with the damn student council, especially not some girl buttin&apos; in!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please use formal language when you speak.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They glared back at my warning, their gazes filled with discontent.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I suppose they think I&apos;ll back down because of that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This is ANHS, and the student council is vested with a certain amount of authority. This discussion is a precious opportunity to bring the truth to light. If you two insist on trampling all over it, then we have no intention of wasting any more time. Let us compile a report for the teachers, Student Council President.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t you dare end this on your own!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You two are quite welcome to leave. Or, if you wish to continue your argument, please do so in the hallway.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Since they only just enrolled, trying to convince them that this wasn&apos;t for the good of the class would have no effect.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For those two, the worst-case scenario was that neither of their claims would be accepted. I doubt their cheap pride would allow for that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But little by little, the two of them began to feel the strange atmosphere in the room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Since they entered the Student Council Office, I hadn&apos;t said a single word to them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Why is the Student Council President just staying quiet? Say something.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I guess because I gave a speech at the entrance ceremony, they do recognize that I&apos;m the student council president.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;ve been watching this whole time, so you get which one of us is in the wrong, don&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Each of them pointed at the other, continuing to insist that they weren&apos;t at fault, only the other was.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They&apos;d likely intended to continue until I gave my answer.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But I continued my silence.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I conveyed to them with my silence that the situation was not worth speaking about.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Eventually, my scheme began to bear fruit, and the noisy Student Council Office fell silent.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was proof they realized they were the only ones making a ruckus.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They still opened their mouths as if to protest, but their words were too quiet for me to hear, and eventually, their lips sealed shut. Both of them, at nearly the same time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What are we supposed to do...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, with a strained voice, he asked for my judgment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems they have the will to resolve this. What will you do, Student Council President?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I gave a quiet nod and signaled for Nanase-san to proceed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Alright then, first, Kusanagi-kun, would you please tell us your side of the story again? In the meantime, no matter how dissatisfied you may be, Maki-kun, I must ask that you absolutely refrain from speaking. I assure you that you will be given an equal opportunity to speak next. Is that understood?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Upon hearing Nanase-san&apos;s admonition, Maki-kun bit his lip hard and gave a slight nod.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This guy... Maki was givin&apos; me a nasty look. So when I asked him what his problem was, he just suddenly snapped. After that, he called me out to the back of the dorms. I wasn&apos;t gonna back down, so I went, and he immediately came at me swinging. Well, I had a hunch I was stronger, y&apos;know, so I dodged it easily and fed him a counter.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One side of the story. Of course, for Maki-kun, who had to listen, it probably wasn&apos;t a story he could accept.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He tried to speak, not hiding his irritation, but his eyes met mine and he stopped himself just in time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was a risk that if he interrupted, he would be unilaterally branded the bad guy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If he stayed quiet, he would be given a chance to explain himself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed he had instinctively sensed that this was how the proceedings worked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For a while after that, Kusanagi-kun continued his complaint that Maki-kun was entirely at fault, and when we determined that no further testimony would be forthcoming, we passed the turn to the long-suffering Maki-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;He can just run his mouth all he wants. This guy conveniently left out everything that makes him look bad. In the first place, it&apos;s &apos;cause that bastard Kusanagi called me &apos;defective&apos; or whatever! I just wanted to make him take it back! And a &apos;hunch&apos;? No way, I was clearly the stronger one! You think I didn&apos;t notice your legs trembling when you took that shot to the gut?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He yelled near Kusanagi-kun&apos;s ear, his voice carrying all the irritation he&apos;d been bottling up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Looking slightly uncomfortable at Maki-kun&apos;s words, he feigned ignorance, turning to look off in another direction to ignore him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>Defective goods</em>. Now that&apos;s a rather nostalgic phrase, isn&apos;t it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As first-years in Class D, we were called the exact same thing. I wonder if that part of it, like some vile tradition, had been passed down at the school this year too.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Putting their stories together without emotion&#x2014;-</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">First, Kusanagi-kun made a remark mocking Maki-kun, calling him defective. Hearing this, an enraged Maki-kun called him out, went behind the dorms and demanded he take it back. However, Kusanagi-kun refused, and Maki-kun, unable to stand his attitude, couldn&apos;t hold back and threw the first punch. The first blow didn&apos;t land; instead, Kusanagi-kun&apos;s counter hit its mark. From there, it became a fistfight of grappling and punching. As for the outcome, it seemed from the extent of his injuries that Maki-kun lost to Kusanagi-kun, but Maki-kun refused to admit it. He insisted that he&apos;d been the one with the upper hand. On the other hand, Kusanagi-kun said that he won and that he stopped fighting the moment the victor was decided&#x2014;or so he claimed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because their claims were complete opposites, it was impossible to judge either way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kusanagi-kun glared again at Maki-kun, who&apos;d just finished speaking, then his expression changed and he looked at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, how should I say this... Student Council President, you understand my side of things, right? I mean, we&apos;re both in Class A, after all. It was perfect self-defense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This was the first time he used proper polite language with me, but I wasn&apos;t so soft as to let my impression of him improve over something so simple.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The title of Class A is meaningless on its own. A fool in Class A will be punished, just as someone in Class D will be rewarded for good deeds.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-What? But&#x2014;!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if mentally slapping himself for trying to suck up to me, Kusanagi-kun&apos;s attitude instantly changed again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So even Class A has such violent-tempered students this year, huh.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It may be true that Maki-kun called you out, but that doesn&apos;t mean it was right for you to retaliate so thoughtlessly. Especially since you immediately intuited that you were the stronger one, correct?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That is...!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, for someone so convinced of his own strength, you seem to have sustained some rather nasty injuries yourself, haven&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if Kusanagi-kun had a slight edge, they looked to be on about the same level.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though he looked displeased, Kusanagi-kun averted his gaze at the remark.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Course you can&apos;t answer that. You didn&apos;t even win.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Shut the hell up, Maki. No matter how you slice it, I&apos;m the stronger one.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If they didn&apos;t apologize and reconcile as things stood, I would have to assign penalties based on the severity of their injuries&#x2014;a ratio of four for Maki-kun and six for Kusanagi-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was just as I&apos;d begun to think that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In the first place, I didn&apos;t even hit Maki this bad... Shit.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a slip of the tongue that escaped his lips unintentionally, rather than something he&apos;d meant for us to hear.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Normally, I would have taken it as an act just to save his own skin, but Maki-kun, standing beside him, didn&apos;t object to the remark at all; in fact, he made a gesture as if to ignore it. Given how things had gone up to this point, he absolutely should have argued back.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At that baffling behavior, Nanase-san and I exchanged a brief glance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you mean by that? When you say you &apos;didn&apos;t hit him this bad&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nah, it&apos;s nothin&apos;.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It can hardly be <em>nothing</em>. Violence is violence, but it&apos;s clear that Maki-kun has sustained more severe injuries. Combined with the school&apos;s judgment, you may be the one to receive a harsher punishment. If that&apos;s the case, shouldn&apos;t you explain yourself properly?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The two of them, who never made eye contact except to glare at each other, momentarily met gazes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It looked like the kind of eye contact Nanase-san and I occasionally exchanged.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s nothin&apos; more to say... What I just said is everything. Right, Maki?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah... Still, you&apos;re definitely the one who&apos;s worse, Kusanagi.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Are you seriously still saying that?!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I supposed this was what one would call being back to square one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If no new testimony is forthcoming, we will have to conclude matters here. Are you two really okay with this?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I also felt, from what I&apos;ve heard, that both of you are equally at fault.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I restated the student council&apos;s opinion, both showed their dissatisfaction and retorted, but they gradually fell silent. It seemed they understood that without new information, there would be no progress.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Fine, I get it. We just have to take the same punishment, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Tch... Then fine. This was a complete waste of time...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Following Maki-kun&apos;s lead, Kusanagi-kun also reached that conclusion, albeit with some reluctance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At the very least, when they first showed up in the Student Council Office, they&apos;d been trying to pin the blame on the other.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was clear that both sides hoped for a lighter punishment for themselves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had Nanase urge them once more to speak if they had anything to say, but their hardened attitudes didn&apos;t soften. As the student council couldn&apos;t hold them indefinitely, we dismissed them for the day, sent them home, and told them the results would be delivered later.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 3</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">IN THE NOW completely silent student council room, I sighed without reservation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If we wanted to, we could end this incident with a fair ruling, but...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;They don&apos;t seem to be denying that they hit each other, but it&apos;s clearly strange that they both started to accept punishment after that apparent slip of the tongue, wouldn&apos;t you agree?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kusanagi-kun said he had the upper hand on his opponent, Maki-kun, and admitted to hitting him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">On the other hand, Maki-kun also said that he had the upper hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, there was a considerable difference in the extent of their injuries, and from what I saw, Kusanagi-kun had the advantage.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But... regarding the damage to his face, which was the deciding factor, an unexpected statement slipped out&#x2014;that he hadn&apos;t attacked hard enough to cause such an injury.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Furthermore, even though it was the perfect opportunity for Maki-kun to claim&#x2014;even as a lie&#x2014;that Kusanagi-kun had attacked him excessively to lighten his own blame, he overlooked the slip of the tongue and pretended not to hear it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Student Council President, what was your take on it? To me, it looked like Maki-kun didn&apos;t want to admit defeat and prioritized his pride.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose so. That&apos;s a possibility, but... at first, it definitely looked like he wanted to pin the heavier blame on Kusanagi-kun. So it&apos;s strange that he didn&apos;t pursue that slip of the tongue. He could have claimed that he deliberately held back, but Kusanagi-kun didn&apos;t stop attacking. Couldn&apos;t he have protected his pride that way, too?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I answered, Nanase-san closed her eyes as if deep in thought.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or perhaps... I tried to think about it from another direction.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Maybe... there&apos;s someone else involved in this assault case.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Thinking that, as I recalled their testimonies, I felt as if I was beginning to see a glimpse of the truth.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But Kusanagi-kun was the one who won the fight, wasn&apos;t he? If this was revenge, wouldn&apos;t it only make sense if Kusanagi-kun was the one who got the worst of it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It doesn&apos;t necessarily have to be revenge, does it? It&apos;s possible a third party punished Maki-kun for starting a fight and losing. Not that such a thing should be condoned, of course.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I see. That&apos;s one way of looking at it. But then it doesn&apos;t explain why Kusanagi-kun wouldn&apos;t report it. Is it because he&apos;s afraid of becoming a target for the person who punished him... perhaps?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What I was just imagining is something similar to that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was understandable that if Maki-kun had been punished by someone like Ryuuen-kun or Housen-kun, even the winner, Kusanagi-kun, would lack the courage to speak up. But it was hard to imagine that they would go all the way to the first-year dorms just for a trivial squabble between freshmen. Besides, if there&apos;d been such a vicious intervention from an upperclassman, there was no way they would&apos;ve so brazenly tried to blame each other in the student council room; they would&apos;ve been instructed to act remorseful and get through the matter without further incident.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Those two showed no signs of anything like that, and what came across for certain was that they were both confident in their fighting skills and very proud. However, they were also unwilling to tell the whole story. I could surmise that much.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But Student Council President&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I know. As far as I&apos;m aware, there aren&apos;t any boys among this year&apos;s first-years who rule their surroundings with violence and fear like Ryuuen-kun or Housen-kun. That&apos;s what you were about to say, wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. I can&apos;t think of a single male student who stands out enough to be ruling his class through fear.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Were they just good at hiding, or was it something else...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I looked through the documents I received from Nanase-san again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Should I try to hear from the first witness who found the two injured...?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or should I call in Maki-kun and Kusanagi-kun again, after they&apos;ve had more time to cool off?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was also the option of speaking to them individually, but I had a feeling that carelessly wounding their pride wouldn&apos;t lead to a solution.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Say, Nanase-san. Boys can be a bit childish in the way they think, don&apos;t you agree?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hm, yes, I suppose so. I do think they have that side to them, to some extent. In fact, the two of them acted like children the entire time they were in the student council room.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. The problem is, both Kusanagi-kun and Maki-kun think they&apos;re strong. That&apos;s exactly why they were only concerned about who won or lost the fight, refusing to back down an inch and trying to pin all the blame on each other, even in a place where they could be punished by the student council.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What if someone took on both of them at the same time, two boys who are confident in their fighting skills?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You mean their pride was wounded because they lost a two-on-one fight? It&apos;s not out of the question, but would they really be that tight-lipped about it? Couldn&apos;t they have worked together to pin the blame on that single person?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But what if their opponent wasn&apos;t a boy... but a girl... and just one at that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san, who was listening beside me, looked at me with a startled expression.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That could be it. For two boys who are boasting and insisting they didn&apos;t lose the fight, I can see why they&apos;d be too embarrassed to admit they lost to a high school girl.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This matter could be closed, since the two boys were accepting of their punishment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, if a third party was involved in this fight, that was a clear act of assault.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Considering their future here at school, this was a problem I wanted to correct now.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Since neither of them is filing a complaint, we have no choice but to investigate. Nanase-san, if you&apos;re willing, could you help me uncover the truth?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, Student Council President. But being the president is a lot of work, isn&apos;t it? You&apos;re amazing, Horikita-senpai.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m nothing special. I was told I was exceptional quite often back in middle school, but... Ayanokouji-kun, my brother, Nagumo-senpai... no, Sakayanagi-san and Ichinose-san are the same. Watching them up close, I&apos;m always made keenly aware of it. To people like them, I&apos;m... undoubtedly just another mediocre person...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was pathetic self-deprecation, but I voiced what I honestly felt.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps it was because the way Koenji-kun so easily brushed me off after school was still weighing heavily on my mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For some reason, Nanase-san&apos;s eyes widened in slight surprise as she stared intently at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No&#x2014;mediocre. Even so, I think that&apos;s fine. A mediocre Horikita-senpai isn&apos;t bad at all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph... You know, hearing that repeatedly is starting to get on my nerves.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how I looked at it, it sounded as though she were making a fool of me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-Wha&#x2014;!? I-I&apos;m so sorry! I think Student Council President Horikita is very admirable!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nanase-san lost her composure, looking truly panicked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s too late to try and smooth things over now.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After pretending to be angry, I smiled and gently placed my hand on her shoulder.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll need you to lend me your strength for a while.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Y-Yes. Of course.&quot;</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 4</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I COULDN&apos;T IGNORE the student council&apos;s problems, but at the same time, I had duties to attend to as the class leader.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Despite feeling tired, I headed to Keyaki Mall for our meeting before 6:00 p.m.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was an important exam, no matter how small the reward or the change in Class Points.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And it was scheduled to take place either for a week starting tomorrow, or a week later.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In order not to lose, in order to win, I had to do what I could today.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I spotted a student standing at the entrance of the supermarket inside the mall and jogged over to her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Heya. The weather&apos;s been pretty awful lately, huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The person I met up with, Karuizawa-san, murmured.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s true. The rainy season isn&apos;t supposed to start for a little while yet, though.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It rained all day last Sunday, too.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The forecast for now called for a little rain late at night, after midnight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Thank you for coming shopping with me at this hour.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nah, not at all. I was planning to go to the supermarket myself, so don&apos;t worry about it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We both grabbed a basket and slowly walked from the fruit section near the entrance toward the vegetable section. As I picked up a golden kiwi that was on sale, I looked over at Karuizawa-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Did you have any particular thoughts while listening to the discussion after school today?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmm, I dunno. Seemed like business as usual to me. Totally disorganized, everyone just says whatever they want... I mean, why are you asking for my opinion?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She replied with a wry smile, saying that it really wasn&apos;t her kind of thing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was true that Karuizawa-san wasn&apos;t normally the proactive type in serious discussions. She left most of the talking during our meetings to me, Hirata-kun, and the other more outspoken students.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We can&apos;t keep doing the same things as always. That&apos;s why I want to take a step toward changing things.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So that&apos;s why you&apos;re asking me? Well, normally, people wouldn&apos;t bother asking me for advice on an exam, since I&apos;m useless for that, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No. It&apos;s true that I reached out to you because I want things to change. But I don&apos;t think you&apos;re useless, Karuizawa-san&#x2014;unlike before.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I spoke those words honestly, prepared for her to get angry.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After all, lying and saying she&apos;d always been someone I could rely on would have been far more disrespectful to her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re really merciless, Horikita-san. But, y&apos;know, I like that about you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without so much as a grimace, Karuizawa-san laughed and accepted my true feelings.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was far more clever of a person than I&apos;d given her credit for.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a great takeaway to have that perception corrected.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well then, how &apos;bout the &apos;super-helpful me&apos; lend an ear? So, Horikita-san, what do you think the main rule for this special exam is?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Before I could ask a question, Karuizawa-san posed one to me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I found that strangely funny and I let out a laugh myself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Although I was only able to join the after-school discussion for less than an hour, several potential ideas came from my classmates.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A standard written exam, a physical fitness test.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In addition, there were oral exams, presentations, a practical music test, and interviews. There was even talk of programming, art, and drawing exams.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In any case, the prevailing view was that some kind of test would be held in one week.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The reason so many people suggested an interview exam was likely due to the major influence of our approaching entrance exams and job searches as third-years.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Considering the one-week preparation time and the lack of an explanation of the rules, that possibility certainly couldn&apos;t be ruled out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, the opinion also came up that there was no need to hide the rules for that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That&apos;s because if it were an interview, it would be the kind of thing they should tell us about from the start.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In any case, there were suggestions ranging from plausible to unrealistic, but none of them felt definitive yet. Since some of the ideas overlapped, we could be more efficient if we narrowed them down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Especially for anything academic, the result would likely change drastically depending on whether it covered all subjects or just specific ones.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I told Karuizawa-san a few of my own thoughts.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She listened intently, which she should&apos;ve found boring, without showing any sign of displeasure.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And so, the conversation moved onto more sensitive topics, the kind I could only discuss with Karuizawa-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This is just my guess, but I think Class C and Class D are connected.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Karuizawa-san brought it up just as we were approaching the condiment aisle.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji-kun is lending Ichinose-san a hand... So that&apos;s what you&apos;re thinking, is it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. I&apos;m sure that&apos;s how it went last time, and I think it&apos;ll be something similar this time, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In a calm tone, Karuizawa-san answered while looking me straight in the eye.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was the first to suggest that possibility as one of the reasons for our defeat in the last special exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Of course, there was no proof of that for now. I&apos;d sensed something strange about Ayanokouji-kun and Ichinose-san growing closer right after his transfer, but I hadn&apos;t thought they&apos;d progressed so far as to share information with each other.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What about you, Horikita-san?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I don&apos;t think the possibility is low. From Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s perspective, it&apos;s only natural that he wouldn&apos;t want our class, Class A, to win. Since he can&apos;t fight us directly, it&apos;s easy to understand why he would give Ichinose-san information to let her win. He knows our class better than anyone else.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While I was unable to function, Hirata-kun and the others took the lead and selected the test members. I was sure that even if Ayanokouji-kun hadn&apos;t seen it happen directly, he could&apos;ve imagined it&#x2014;what discussions took place and what conclusions they&apos;d reach.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Of course, Hirata-kun and the others weren&apos;t at fault. I had a feeling that even if I&apos;d participated in the discussions, the outcome would&apos;ve been the same, although the process might have been different.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But this special exam isn&apos;t necessarily a one-on-one battle. It&apos;s possible Ayanokouji-kun could suffer a disadvantage by giving information to Ichinose-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That won&apos;t happen.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Because he thinks he won&apos;t lose even if he gives her information?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I asked that, Karuizawa-san gave a small laugh and denied it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If his goal in transferring to Class C was to raise it to Class A by himself, that&apos;s a bit half-assed, you know? He should&apos;ve just gone to the bottom-ranked Class D.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But isn&apos;t that because a leader like Ichinose-san is already there? Class C was in a situation where Sakayanagi-san had been expelled, leaving them without a new leader.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it were Ayanokouji-kun... he would&apos;ve gone to Class D, even if it meant getting Ichinose-san expelled.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s&#x2014;that&apos;s taking it too far. He might be able to target her if there&apos;s a risk of expulsion in a special exam, but... are you saying he was trying to get Ichinose-san expelled during the year-end special exam?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That special exam wasn&apos;t one where you could intentionally get someone from the opposing class expelled. Its rules were structured around whether or not someone from within your own class would be expelled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can&apos;t think of how he&apos;d do it, but getting someone expelled isn&apos;t just limited to special exams, is it? He might even be able to do it on an ordinary day like today.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps Karuizawa-san, who dated him, held Ayanokouji-kun in higher esteem than I did. Of course, I had no intention of arguing that she was overestimating him, but...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Assuming that&apos;s true... then why did he transfer to Class C, I wonder.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know. But Ayanokouji-kun seems to always be thinking of something unimaginable, and he doesn&apos;t seem to need Ichinose-san&apos;s help to get to Class A. And yet, if he helped her out, I just figured he&apos;d probably do it again this time.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was this a favor intended to make future exams easier by having the lower-ranked classes join forces?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As long as his objective and the answer remained unknown, speculation was dangerous, but I had to keep it in the back of my mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And if we were going to fight based on that assumption, I had to figure out the rules of this special exam, and its solution, as quickly as possible.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A late start the next day would truly be a fatal blow.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t think I would have a sharp flash of insight so easily just because I did something different from usual.&#xa0;But I was certain that taking on new challenges like this would surely be helpful in the future.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if some might say it was too little, too late, I wanted to cherish what I realized now.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 5</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">QUITE SOME TIME had passed since Ayanokouji-kun transferred classes, but the wound in my heart hadn&apos;t completely healed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The same must have been true for Karuizawa-san, whom I&apos;d gone shopping with.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But we&apos;d both become able to look forward and move on, more so than before.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I think I&apos;d recovered enough to at least feign composure whenever I saw Ayanokouji-kun at school, at Keyaki Mall, or in the dorms.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Karuizawa-san had been the catalyst for my recovery, but another one was right in front of me&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For a loser, your color&apos;s looking a little better, Horikita.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It was just past 6:30 PM, and I had just returned from the supermarket. I heard a rough knock, and when I unlocked the door to my room,&#xa0;Ibuki-san&apos;s opening words were dripping with sarcasm.&#xa0;It was hardly the attitude of someone about to come into another person&apos;s room and be served a meal.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s thanks to your wonderful flying kick. I&apos;m so very grateful.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In response to my sarcasm, Ibuki-san proudly puffed out her chest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it happens again, I&apos;ll be sure to kick you then, too, so be grateful.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if, in her head, she&apos;d conveniently interpreted that as praise...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was making me regret ever having said that she&apos;d helped, even a little, as a catalyst for my recovery.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No, more importantly, to prevent any future victims, it seemed better to explain that this was a real problem, rather than using sarcasm that wouldn&apos;t get through to her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kicking someone in the back from behind without warning is an extremely dangerous act, so don&apos;t do that again. At the very least, you should never do it to anyone but me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was fortunate that I, the one who was kicked, had some knowledge of martial arts and that by chance the damage was kept to a minimum, but if the point of impact had been worse, I could&apos;ve been seriously injured. I made sure to warn her firmly about that part.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, Ibuki-san didn&apos;t take my words at face value and instead laughed mockingly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So it&apos;s fine to do it to you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It is not fine. But I won&apos;t be kicked by you again. After all, your prerequisite for kicking me was, &apos;if it happens again,&apos; wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To think the precaution I&apos;d taken would pay off so quickly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph. So you&apos;re sayin&apos; you&apos;ve completely recovered, then.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can say it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if she found that completely unbelievable, Ibuki-san laughed as she kicked her shoes off messily and stepped inside. I picked up the disheveled shoes and rearranged them next to the two pairs that were already neatly lined up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One pair belonged to me, the owner of this room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And the other pair&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you think, Kushida? About what Horikita just said.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san called out, turning her gaze to the owner of the other pair of shoes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Unbelievable.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She answered instantly, without so much as a glance in my direction, her eyes fixed on her phone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s what she says.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Like you, she won&apos;t readily agree with anything I say, so I pay it no mind.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I let out a soft sigh and moved from the entryway to the front of the kitchen.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, while taking the groceries I bought at the Keyaki Mall supermarket out of my reusable bag, I turned back and called out to them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Just sit wherever and play on your phone like she is. I don&apos;t mind if you watch TV, either.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t have to tell me twice.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san plopped down in her usual spot and then glanced around the room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, who was watching Ibuki-san out of the corner of her eye, muttered quietly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s with that look on your face? It&apos;s disgusting.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Whose face did you say is disgusting?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s only one other person nearby, so there&apos;s no need to say a name, is there?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Could be Horikita. She&apos;s got a gross face.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, right. Yeah, that might be true. I guess that might&apos;ve been misleading.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if those two even realized whose room this was...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But I was talking about you, Ibuki-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? What, what? Why are you trying to pick a fight all of a sudden? You want me to throw a pillow at you?&quot; snapped the always-combative Ibuki-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s because you had this look on your face, like, &apos;I&apos;m so happy to be in Horikita-san&apos;s room after so long~&apos;.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wha&#x2014;? Wha&#x2014;? I&apos;m not happy! I just felt lucky I could get a free meal, that&apos;s all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Isn&apos;t that what you call being happy?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Absolutely not!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Casting a sideways glance at the living room, which had suddenly grown noisy with the addition of just one person, I began to cook with practiced hands. The ingredients I bought today cost twice my usual budget.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Still, compared to when I had given up on cooking for myself for some time in favor of simple meals from convenience stores, the expense was low.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The noisy sound of their two voices, something I&apos;d never found pleasant until just a little while ago.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But now, they reached my ears like strangely calming music.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A person&apos;s state of mind changed drastically with just a small shift in their perspective and way of thinking.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even as I chopped the vegetables with a kitchen knife, I reflected once again on the situation I found myself in.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A little while ago, I told Ibuki-san a small lie.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My answer regarding my recovery. My assertion that I could say so with certainty.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was a clear lie.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I do believe I&apos;d managed to crawl my way from the depths of despair to a much better place.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Nevertheless, my wounded heart is far from fully healed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A lie told to prevent another victim of Ibuki-san&apos;s kicks.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But the truth is, I wanted to lie to deceive myself into believing I was all right.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The reality that Ayanokouji-kun was gone was just that heavy&#x2014;</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 6</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">THE RICE COOKER, which I&apos;d set ahead of time, beeped, announcing that the rice was cooked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I immediately opened the lid, fluffed the rice with the paddle to release excess surface moisture, and evened it out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After that, I set the prepared meal down in front of Kushida-san and Ibuki-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A hamburger steak made with domestic kuroge wagyu. Not just black-haired cattle, mind you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;K-Kuroge wagyu?! Th-This is pretty extravagant, isn&apos;t it...!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though bewildered, Ibuki-san spread her hands and gave the food an approving look.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You were the one who hoped to eat something nice, weren&apos;t you? Also, your Japanese is a bit strange, so you should be more careful.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Pointing this out with exasperation, I quickly resumed my preparations.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you&apos;re gonna treat me this well, I wouldn&apos;t mind stopping by every day, y&apos;know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I will have to firmly decline. Today is an exception.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So stingy, Horikita.
