Oh well, I&apos;ll settle for a normal meal from tomorrow on.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t understand why she&apos;d already decided that I would be treating her from tomorrow onward, but I ignored her, as engaging would only encourage her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At the very least, she seemed to have taken it as a genuine token of my gratitude and welcomed this elaborate meal.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A spread on the dining table, a cut above the usual.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I noticed that Kushida-san, too, had stopped fiddling with her phone and was now staring at the food.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It certainly is special, perhaps&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In contrast to the delighted Ibuki-san, Kushida-san grew more guarded.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There might be a catch to this. If you eat it carelessly, you might regret it&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed she&apos;d tried to quickly warn Ibuki-san, but Ibuki-san was already in motion, lifting a side dish to her mouth with her fork.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So fast...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She ate with such vigor you could hear her wolfing it down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was proof of how delicious it was&#x2014;that once she started, she couldn&apos;t stop&#x2014;and a pleasant sight to behold. As the one who made it, it wasn&apos;t a bad feeling at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Savage.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Who&apos;re you calling a savage?!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though her mouth was still full, she immediately retorted to Kushida-san&apos;s jab.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I watched for a moment without touching my own food, a beaming Ibuki-san looked up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ve been flat broke lately, so a meal like this is an improvement.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was curious as to what kind of life she was leading, but I had more pressing matters at the moment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After all, Ibuki-san always left in a hurry as soon as she finished eating.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I want to hear both of your opinions.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;About the food?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No. Regarding Ayanokouji-kun, who has become quite the topic of conversation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The moment she heard that name, Ibuki-san showed an overt, unconcealed look of disgust.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, for her part, glanced at me once, her expression unchanging.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But her eyes seemed to say, &apos;I knew it,&apos; having already guessed the intent behind the special meal.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;About him transferring classes? If so, that&apos;s old news by now, don&apos;t you think?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As she spoke so nonchalantly, I wondered whether she felt no shock at Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s departure, even though the guarantee that we could safely maintain our position in Class A had clearly vanished.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji-kun is now our enemy... How can we defeat him?... Right now, I can&apos;t see a way. I need a hint that will lead to a breakthrough.&quot;</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/EGWpxXChgbpTWBkDPEcAf.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So it&apos;s &apos;three heads are better than one,&apos; huh? If that&apos;s the case, isn&apos;t Ibuki-san a little out of her league?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I am perfectly aware of that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I replied to Kushida-san, Ibuki-san, who was still gripping her fork, lightly slammed her fist on the table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I get that you&apos;re making fun of me. You wanna get kicked?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I may be making fun of you, but I&apos;m also trying to rely on you. With regard to this topic, at least, I&apos;m hoping you might come up with some unexpected advice. After all, you&apos;ve never seemed to like Ayanokouji-kun, have you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph. I get it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed she&apos;d accepted my convoluted phrasing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, you totally don&apos;t get it, Ibuki-san... Oh well, whatever.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san picked up her fork again, shoveled a large amount of rice into her mouth, and after chewing thoroughly and swallowing, she snorted once more. Then she nodded while spinning her fork around impolitely.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Beat Ayanokouji, huh? That&apos;s impossible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Giving up so quickly? That&apos;s rather unlike you. I do wish you&apos;d put a little more thought into your words.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I am<em>&#xa0;</em>thinking. He&apos;s as strong as a monster and surprise attacks don&apos;t work on him. Sure, if he were an opponent I felt I could almost beat, I&apos;d be fired up to get revenge next time, but he&apos;s not on that level, and I get that now. That&apos;s why I&apos;ve already given up.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For Ibuki-san, who loved to fight, to say something like that was just what one would expect from Ayanokouji-kun&#x2014;but that wasn&apos;t the issue.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems that when you think of &apos;defeating him,&apos; you only consider it in a physical sense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Is there any other way?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Normally, one would assume I meant in terms of intellect, but I decided to positively accept this as another one of those ideas that only she could come up with.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Perhaps it might not be a bad idea to start by considering things from that angle.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was precisely because Ibuki-san was here that we could even have this kind of discussion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But I gotta ask, don&apos;t you know the secret to his strength or something?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The secret to his strength...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Neither you nor I could even touch him. Even that Albert guy was like a kid in front of Ayanokouji. In terms of pure power, Ayanokouji shouldn&apos;t have stood a chance, but it wasn&apos;t even a contest.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The fork that&apos;d been spinning on her fingertips stopped as she gripped it tightly in her hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The secret to his strength that Ibuki-san mentioned. When and where had he acquired that abnormal strength?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No, it wasn&apos;t just his strength. I had a feeling his intelligence was also clearly different from that of an ordinary student.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Unfortunately, I don&apos;t know anything... Because he never told me anything.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>And I was the one closest to him</em>... I muttered such a self-deprecating thought to myself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re useless, aren&apos;t ya. And here I was, thinkin&apos; you and Ayanokouji were a package deal.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a painfully sharp remark that I couldn&apos;t refute in the slightest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I could, I would&apos;ve covered my ears with my hands to hide my pathetic self.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But even so, I was steeling myself for it. I was beginning to accept that it couldn&apos;t be helped that similar feelings would be reawakened every time the conversation turned to Ayanokouji-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t really get fighting and all that, but does that mean Ayanokouji-kun has something that makes him better than Yamada-kun, besides just muscle? &apos;Cause, like, no matter how you look at it, Yamada-kun is way stronger.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, if you put it like that, I guess so, but...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san trailed off and then looked at me. She was no doubt pawning off the tedious explanation onto me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In his case, it isn&apos;t a matter of training or martial arts styles; he exists outside of such things.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The path to becoming strong was fundamentally the same for everyone. In a specific field, be it karate or judo, one had no choice but to accumulate training and hone their skills. I explained this to Kushida-san.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If we could only understand the foundation of his polished technique...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even for a genius with exceptional talent, it took many years to grow to the level of a master.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if I hadn&apos;t seen as much as Ibuki-san, I had been shown a glimpse of his power when he confronted Housen-kun and during the training camp. It was precisely because I too had studied martial arts that my mind refused to comprehend it. I couldn&apos;t believe it was something a high school student could achieve.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder if he learned those skills after he came to this school, or maybe from before?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That guy was already a monster back in the winter of our first year. No way he got &apos;em after he enrolled here. There&apos;s no place to learn skills like that here, anyway. You&apos;re an idiot, Kushida.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Listen, my brain isn&apos;t made of muscle like you two, okay? It&apos;s totally normal not to know about all that barbaric stuff. Get it? So, doesn&apos;t that just mean he started taking lessons for that sort of thing before elementary school, or maybe around that time?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Martial arts are the same as academics, Kushida-san. Strength isn&apos;t something one acquires simply based on the time invested. No matter how much you study, it&apos;s difficult to come out on top of the pack, is it not? It isn&apos;t as if one can become stronger than anyone else just by training.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I studied diligently through elementary and middle school in order to take first place.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In fact, in middle school, I was proud to say that I was, to put it modestly, in the top three of my grade.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But after enrolling at the ANHS, I was unceremoniously knocked from that perch. Even though I could maintain a high rank, taking first place was no longer easy. When I went to university, I would likely experience something similar. Excellent students from all over the country would gather, and my ranking would naturally drop. It was a cycle. A cruel reality that could not be overcome by effort alone awaited.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well then, doesn&apos;t that mean there&apos;s really only one answer? He&apos;s the best at studying in this school, and he&apos;s the strongest in this school, right? It&apos;s just a simple case of Ayanokouji-kun having the natural talent of a genius, the kind that lets him be at the top.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yes. If one didn&apos;t think too deeply about it, that was, in a sense, an answer in itself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A person with genuine talent, different from the handful of hard workers and honor students in our grade.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One of the handful of talented individuals who could consistently take the top spot at Japan&apos;s most prestigious high schools and universities.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without a doubt, Ayanokouji-kun was a person who held that qualification.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It may be blunt to say, but that might be the case. However, it still doesn&apos;t feel real to me. I&apos;ve seen many people more capable than myself, but even among them, Ayanokouji-kun feels... anomalous... as if he alone is clearly standing on a different plane.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x2014;Somehow, I felt as though I was arbitrarily evaluating him on a level close to deification.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For now, I decided to assume he was just such an exaggerated existence. If it turned out that upon getting to know him, he was merely an honor student in the top ten or twenty percent, then my peace of mind would be preserved.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After taking a breath, I recalled a recent event.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wanting to know his roots, I actually went to the three-way conference hoping to meet his parents.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, who up to this point seemed almost completely uninterested in our discussion of martial arts, opened her mouth eagerly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s? What were they like?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m curious about that, too. Did he look strong, like a gorilla?&quot; As if she couldn&apos;t wait to hear the rest, Ibuki-san leaned forward.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You might be disappointed to hear this. It was his father who came for the interview, and while he had a somewhat stern look, he was a calm, ordinary person. He also said he hadn&apos;t done anything particularly special regarding his education.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No way, that&apos;s gotta be a lie. It&apos;s the only way to explain it, short of him being turned into a cyborg.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She said something outrageous while slurping her miso soup.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;He didn&apos;t seem like he was lying, though.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But we&apos;re talking about Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s father, right? It&apos;d be kinda weird to think he&apos;d tell the whole truth.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Yes, that&apos;s true.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It certainly wasn&apos;t something that could be acquired through a normal education.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even with a considerably elite education, it still seemed a distant realm.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was it simply a lie to avoid actions that would be considered bragging about his son?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Speaking of parents... I wonder if there are any students at this school from the same middle school as Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder about that. When I tried to get similar information out of him a few times before, he skillfully evaded my questions.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Usually, someone would tell you what school they went to, right? But the fact he won&apos;t... maybe that means he really does have a little secret.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Coming from Kushida-san, who desperately wanted to keep her own middle school a secret, it was quite persuasive.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was certain that it wasn&apos;t something one would hide unless there was an inconvenient reason.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So is Ayanokouji a beast at sports other than fighting, too? I mean, he was fast on his feet.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m not so sure; I don&apos;t really know about that. Early on, I didn&apos;t question it much, but considering he deliberately avoided high scores on the written exams, it&apos;s quite plausible he was intentionally holding back in sports he excels at.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So even if we found out he was in a club in middle school, he might&apos;ve been holding back then, too, huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...That&apos;s true. If he possessed outstanding talent in a particular sport, someone would&apos;ve likely heard rumors, whether inside or outside the prefecture...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just tried searching Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s name, but nothing came up. I thought something would pop up since Ayanokouji is a rare last name, but I can&apos;t find any trace of him setting records in elementary or middle school. The only other results are for celebrities or politicians who don&apos;t seem related.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san explained this to us while scrolling on her phone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Until he enrolled in high school, he wasn&apos;t the kind of student who, for better or worse, would appear online.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That in itself was a clue, but we could hardly say we&apos;d made any progress.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Someone with a clue that connected to Ayanokouji-kun...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That guy seemed like he knew something, didn&apos;t he?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san muttered quietly, as if recalling something.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At almost the same moment, I recalled the person she had called <em>that guy </em>as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What was his name again... uh, the one in the year below us...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yagami. Yagami Takuya-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, yeah, Yagami. He was a pretty dangerous dude, went on a real rampage. Looked like he beat the crap outta Komiya and his friends on the deserted island, and wasn&apos;t he screaming something about Ayanokouji?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As Ibuki-san and I reminisced about Yagami-kun, Kushida-san, who&apos;d been listening quietly, tensed up at the mention of his name. Cornered in the student council room by Nagumo-senpai, Ryuuen-kun, and the teachers, Yagami-kun was expelled for instigating the assault on the deserted island.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, at the time, I had a fleeting thought that someone else might have been pulling the strings. A suspicion that Ayanokouji-kun had orchestrated the entire thing. But with no evidence, it was nothing more than a hunch. Now, however, that same suspicion was beginning to resurface.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Amasawa showed up at the end, too. And a bunch of weird adults stormed in.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s right... I believe they said they were mutual associates of Yagami-kun and Amasawa-san. My own memory has faded quite a bit, but I&apos;m certain I heard that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yagami-kun and the others had been trying to get Ayanokouji-kun expelled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Ayanokouji-kun had summoned that very same Yagami-kun by setting a trap in a letter.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The connection between Yagami-kun and Amasawa-san was the only solid fact.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But any connection to Ayanokouji-kun remained unconfirmed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yagami, Amasawa, and Ayanokouji... maybe they were from the same middle school, or they&apos;ve known each other for a long time? The possibility is definitely there, right? Why don&apos;t you try searching for Yagami and Amasawa, too?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No luck, I&apos;m afraid. I just tried.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Kushida-san had already begun her search, as she turned her screen toward us to show the results.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At that moment, I remembered something I had forgotten until now.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wait. Now that I think about it, Yagami-kun... he went to the same middle school as me&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? Seriously? Then doesn&apos;t that mean he has nothing to do with Ayanokouji? How could you not remember something like that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It couldn&apos;t be helped. I never knew him back in middle school. But you&apos;re a different story, Kushida-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, who&apos;d been listening in silence up to this point, quietly placed her phone on the table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kushida-san, you&apos;re from the same middle school as me, and you also knew Yagami-kun. Is that right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then it&apos;s your fault, you idiot Kushida. Hurry up and say it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yagami... I see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After murmuring quietly, Kushida-san continued.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, Ayanokouji-kun has transferred classes and there&apos;s no point in hiding it here anymore, so I&apos;ll tell you. Yagami-kun and I aren&apos;t from the same middle school.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;Huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? What&apos;s that? What do you mean? I&apos;m getting totally lost here.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ibuki-san was confused. At the same time, I also felt a sense of confusion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Right after last year&apos;s first-years enrolled. I remembered that when Yagami-kun visited the second-year classrooms, they certainly had a conversation stating they were from the same middle school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was something I believed unconditionally, without a shred of doubt. That fundamental premise was suddenly shattered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, Kushida-san told us the reason why.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The students from her middle school days who knew her true nature. Although she had no memory of a kouhai named Yagami Takuya, he clearly acted as though he knew her. She was worried that if she acted like she didn&apos;t know him, it would lead to a difficult situation where he might carelessly expose her past, so she impulsively lied that they were from the same middle school to go along with his story. She did it to probe and see for herself just how much Yagami-kun knew. And the result had been the worst possible one. Despite not being from the same middle school, Yagami-kun knew the details of Kushida-san&apos;s past, and in exchange for not revealing it, he demanded various forms of cooperation from her. It seemed Amasawa-san had a hand in that affair as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-Wait a minute. That&apos;s so much bombshell information, my head can&apos;t keep up. You knew that Yagami-kun was smart, too... Was he really not your kouhai?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I only remembered a little because it was right after I saw the new students&apos; OAA. He had an A in Academic Ability, so it stuck in a corner of my mind. It&apos;d be a pain if a kouhai from my middle school showed up, like you did in my case, Horikita-san, right? That&apos;s why I checked the faces and names of all the first-years the moment we got access.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She confessed that she&apos;d skillfully used that &apos;A in Academic Ability&apos; part to pretend they were acquaintances.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know how sure you guys were, Horikita-san, but the one who got Yagami-kun expelled was definitely Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san cut into the one area where Ibuki-san and I had been unable to find a definitive answer.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I asked Ayanokouji-kun directly after Yagami-kun was expelled. He said he &apos;took care of&apos; Yagami-kun to save me from being used. It seems Yagami-kun and Ayanokouji-kun went to different schools, but he said they were acquaintances. Same for Amasawa-san; he said she lived nearby.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You should&apos;ve said that sooner. Really, you idiot Kushida.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s my sealed-away past, you know. This is a story I really wouldn&apos;t want anyone to hear.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Jeez, to think there was someone who knew what I wanted to know this close by.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Indeed... I sympathize with your circumstances, but this is a perfect example of it being darkest under the lighthouse.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It felt like the building blocks I&apos;d painstakingly stacked on my own had been forcefully knocked over by a hand from the side.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji-kun said he earned their resentment a long time ago. Did Amasawa-san forgive him? Was the misunderstanding cleared up? It seemed like something like that, but he said Yagami-kun wouldn&apos;t forgive him. The story was that he probably dug up my past in order to get revenge on Ayanokouji-kun... I think.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san spoke, recalling that conversation from what was likely last year as best as she could.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I thought I&apos;d never tell this story to anyone else, but you never know, I guess.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, laughing self-deprecatingly, took a sip of water.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps her throat was dry from having spoken the truth she had hidden until now.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I may have made you recall unpleasant things, but this is a huge piece of information. Though we&apos;ll need to reorganize everything from the beginning... you know.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The person known as Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, whose background was a mystery.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But it was a fact that even a person like Ayanokouji-kun had mutual acquaintances in Amasawa-san and Yagami-kun. Furthermore, both of them also possessed extraordinary abilities. What could be deduced from this was that their relationship was likely more than just being mere acquaintances.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He earned the resentment of his two kouhai somewhere&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Were they in the same environment since middle school, or perhaps even earlier, since childhood&#x2014;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And there was some trouble... In my mind, I began to reconstruct everything from scratch.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know anything about martial arts, but there are probably famous karate or judo dojos known nationwide, so isn&apos;t it possible they attended a place like that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Idiot Kushida. Their strength isn&apos;t something you can get that easily.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I did preface my statement by saying &apos;I don&apos;t know anything about martial arts,&apos; didn&apos;t I? Just because my idea is different from your perception, Ibuki-san, could you stop trying to attack people for it? You&apos;re just embarrassing yourself. Also, could you stop calling me an idiot over and over? With our grades, it wouldn&apos;t even be a contest between you and me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san added with exasperation that she&apos;d merely offered one opinion from a layman&apos;s point of view.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph. Knowing about martial arts is just common sense, isn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t have that kind of common sense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t fight. But it&apos;s true that you can&apos;t easily acquire skills like Ayanokouji-kun and the others just by attending a famous dojo... Conversely, yes, perhaps as a result of their skills, they were invited to a famous dojo or something, and that&apos;s how they met... That line of thought might be possible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The adults who came to pick up Yagami-kun when he went on a rampage in the student council room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If they were also people related to his origins, then the connections would gradually start to become visible.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder if I should try asking Amasawa-san directly soon?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You think that little devil would tell you anything?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That may be true... What will you two do?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll pass.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If I see Amasawa&apos;s face, I&apos;ll want to punch her, so I&apos;m not going.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The two of them showed their refusal almost simultaneously.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But it was true that making careless contact would likely yield no progress.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It might be better to start by just observing the situation.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 7</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">IBUKI-SAN, HAVING EATEN her fill, left first. About two minutes later, Kushida-san went to the entryway and put on her shoes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I thought she would leave the room without so much as a glance back&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Thank you for the meal today. I appreciate it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...You&apos;re not so bad, I suppose. Unlike a certain someone, you can actually say thank you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not just about you, Horikita-san. I don&apos;t really want to thank anyone else either, but I guess it&apos;s just a habit. I make a point of putting it into words, so it just comes out unconsciously.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san nonchalantly&#xa0;stated that her previous remark was also an unconscious one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... Well, regardless of your true feelings, words are important, aren&apos;t they?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In truth, there was a certain satisfaction to be had even from those words of gratitude, however superficial.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I thought she would leave, she immediately turned back toward me for some reason.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;By the way, how are things with the special exam? Have you been able to predict what kind of rules it&apos;ll have?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m narrowing down the possibilities. I intend to report a few potential strategies to the class tomorrow.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This sort of discussion was impossible with Ibuki-san around.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if that was why Kushida-san had deliberately chosen to leave a little later.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So you&apos;re even wary of her, of Ibuki-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Honestly, she&apos;s not really at a level where I need to be cautious, you know. But just in case, I was on my guard, thinking she might try to probe me about our thoughts on the special exam rules during the meal. But there was no sign of that, or rather, I guess the thought never even crossed her mind.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no doubt she&apos;d stopped by the room purely for the meal.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Still, Kushida-san avoided the topic just in case.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To be honest, it was a helpful consideration.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s a special exam and we don&apos;t know the rules, but do you still plan on winning?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What a thing to ask. Of course, I do. The conditions are the same for every other class, you know.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But Class C has Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A sharp gaze and words that struck at the core without any reservation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was as if she were testing my mental state all over again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s strange, isn&apos;t it? When we first enrolled, I never would have imagined it, but now, just hearing his name makes my body instinctively tense up and feel intimidated, and I can no longer see him as an opponent I can defeat.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I acknowledged the undeniable fact and sighed at my own weakness.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even so, I&apos;ll fight with the belief that I can win. Isn&apos;t that the bare minimum attitude to have?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Perhaps,&quot; Kushida-san answered, averting her gaze from me for a moment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I thought I&apos;d get to bask in a little bit of bliss once we made it to Class A, but now we&apos;re in a position where we have to run for our lives. We&apos;re like rabbits being chased by a lion, you know? It&apos;d be pretty uncool if we keep losing like this, I think.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With her great pride, Kushida-san continued to play the part of an honor student and was extremely concerned with appearances. Though she never put it into words, there was no doubt she&apos;d been deeply irritated about being placed in Class D and having to content herself with being in a lower-ranked class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, during the Unanimous Vote Special Exam, her true nature was exposed to the entire class, and her circumstances changed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She now had to interact with people she despised, like me, and was forced to keep fighting, even as she was being wounded.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The status of Class A, which she finally, painstakingly obtained.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The fact that it was now facing the threat of being toppled in an instant must have been causing her considerable mental anguish.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Either way, it doesn&apos;t look like you can do it by yourself, Horikita-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Even if I wanted to, I can&apos;t deny it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For now, if you figure out anything that could be a hint for the special exam, let me know. I&apos;ll try thinking about it my own way from there.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, of course. Even so...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san, who showed her willingness to cooperate even as she unreservedly voiced her complaints.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I never thought I&apos;d hear you declare your support. You do have a genuinely kind side to you, don&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I praised her frankly. In response, Kushida-san scoffed, then looked at me with a fixed smile, her eyes unmoving.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just love the cute me who pretends to be kind. It&apos;s because I&apos;m aware I have an awful personality that I can afford to treat even you with an angelic attitude, Horikita-san. So then, I expect your strength isn&apos;t just for show.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With that, Kushida-san gave a small, mocking laugh, opened the door, and left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... I guess that means I&apos;m still a poor judge of character.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Far from being exasperated, I found myself inadvertently impressed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d been shown Kushida-san&apos;s way of life&#x2014;one that was endlessly concerned only with how she presented herself to others.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her final words echoed in the back of my mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&apos;<em>I expect your strength isn&apos;t just for show</em>.&apos;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x201c;&#x2014;I am expecting that of myself, as well.&#x201d;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After the door closed, I let out a breath and returned to the living room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A new special exam would begin the next day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But I had things to do, so it wasn&apos;t yet time to rest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x201c;Well then&#x2026; I suppose I should clean up.&#x201d;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Suppressing my slight reluctance, I turned my gaze to the table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kushida-san always ate gracefully, but Ibuki-san was the surprising one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While it wasn&apos;t uncommon for people to leave rice grains in their bowls, she&apos;d eaten every last one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x201c;I can praise her for that, at least, but everything else is as hopeless as ever.&#x201d;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Spilled miso soup and bits of side dishes weren&apos;t only on the table, but also where she&apos;d been sitting.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her uniform skirt must have been stained as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x201c;I wish she&apos;d have at least some awareness that her daily conduct and manners are being observed&#x2014;&#x201d;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I was about to reach out, I stopped.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A thought suddenly occurred to me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... That sort of thing is certainly plausible, isn&apos;t it...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The school imposed a week-long special exam with no explanation of the rules.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Did it refer to this one week, or to the time after this one week?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The answer hadn&apos;t been obtained.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Therefore, every class must have been racking their brains.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;The possibility is by no means low.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Chabashira-sensei&apos;s words, actions, and attitude.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">What if they were, in a way, the best hint she could give us?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She wanted us to make her think that we were different from when we first enrolled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Such cold words reflected the true feelings of our teacher, who&apos;d been watching our immature class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If the idea that surfaced in my mind was correct, I had to take action immediately, within the day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This was because whether or not I could control that person by tomorrow was directly linked to victory or defeat.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 8</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I RUSHED OUT of my room just after 8:00 p.m. and first headed downstairs in the elevator.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, standing in front of Sudou-kun&apos;s room, I suppressed my impatience and knocked with some reservation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After hearing footsteps pattering toward the door, it opened a little roughly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Who the hell is it at this hour&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun appeared, still in his school uniform with only his blazer removed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The sight of him wearing glasses, which I rarely saw, felt a little refreshing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I apologize for visiting without notice. I have something I&apos;d like to talk about.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;S-S-S-Suzune!? Wh-what are you doing here? I mean, uh, it&apos;s not a problem or anything!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A mechanical pencil was clutched in the hand of the flustered, gesticulating Sudou-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh? Horikita-san?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">From behind the bewildered Sudou-kun, Onodera-san poked her head out as she spoke.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I glanced at the entryway and saw two pairs of shoes. Even I had my suspicions, however slight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Was I interrupting something?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;N-No, you&apos;re not interrupting anything at all! We were just studying together, that&apos;s it!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Studying?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Indeed, there were Sudou-kun&apos;s glasses and the mechanical pencil in his hand. More importantly, both of them were still in their school uniforms.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As the student council president, there seemed to be no problems to point out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Well, y&apos;see, about this special exam. They said we don&apos;t gotta study yet, but it&apos;d be bad if there was a written test, right? So I figured we should prepare, even just a little.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even though I hadn&apos;t yet told them our plan for tomorrow, he&apos;d already begun to prepare countermeasures. I doubt anyone could have predicted seeing him like this back when we first enrolled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s admirable, but make sure you get Onodera-san back to her room safely before curfew, all right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;O-Obviously! So what do you want, showing up without any notice?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He tried to shoo Onodera-san toward the living room with his hand, but I stopped him. Since there was a guest, it would be faster to explain it to both of them at once.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;May I come in for just a moment? This is something I&apos;d rather people from other classes not overhear.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah... of course you can.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I stepped into the entryway and closed the door.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then I beckoned Onodera-san, who was watching me with curiosity, over to the entryway.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The special exam that starts tomorrow. I can&apos;t say for certain, but I have an idea about the kind of rules it might have.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I lowered my voice slightly and explained it to the two of them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Seriously? What kinda rules d&apos;you think they are?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;When Chabashira-sensei announced the special exam, her attitude was colder than usual, wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kanji and the others were complaining about it, yeah. Sounded like they were trying to figure out why.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not that she was being cold because she couldn&apos;t afford to be otherwise; I believe she was actually being curt on purpose.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? On purpose? What&apos;s the point of doing that? She&apos;ll just make people hate her, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I interpreted that attitude as the only hint the teacher could give us... I think she wanted to make us remember the time shortly after we enrolled.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You mean back in our first year, right? Yeah, she was pretty damn cold back when we enrolled.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun glanced back to meet Onodera-san&apos;s eyes, recalling the past, and they both nodded in understanding.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do you remember the first special exam we took as first-year Class D?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmm? The uninhabited island exam... wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Onodera-san, who&apos;d taken a few more steps forward to stand beside Sudou-kun, murmured as she tried to recall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Your guess isn&apos;t technically wrong, Onodera-san. However, right after we enrolled, we were put through a certain month-long test without any explanation of the rules&#x2014;the root cause of all our struggles for the past two years.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As soon as I said that, Sudou-kun immediately remembered, and Onodera-san followed suit.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, right. They were checkin&apos; stuff like tardiness, absences, and our attitude in class. That&apos;s how our Class Points went to zero, wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A surprise from the school that gave the children who&apos;d just become first-year high schoolers points equivalent to 100,000 yen every month. Repeated tardiness and private conversations. The teacher simply continued to watch over us quietly, without a word of warning.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Class D, who enjoyed and reveled in it all without a single thought for what was behind it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The price for that was steep, and it led to the two years of hardship I just mentioned.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So it&apos;s that exam again this time?... Come to think of it, she did say somethin&apos; like, &apos;show me you&apos;ve grown.&apos; Guess she meant for us not to make the same mistake again.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun, who&apos;d been properly listening to Chabashira-sensei&apos;s words, picked up the pieces of her statement himself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s how I interpreted it. That&apos;s why I thought it might be a special exam with the same rules.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That seems totally plausible. Actually, now that you mention it, it almost feels like that&apos;s the only possibility.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Onodera-san heard my idea and I could tell from her expression that it clicked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, I can&apos;t completely rule out the possibility of an exam with some kind of unrelated rules. But our classmates studied intensively for the last special exam, and a fair amount of frustration has built up. Continuing this way doesn&apos;t guarantee we&apos;ll get good results.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There couldn&apos;t have been many students who could maintain such high motivation, like these two.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This whole &apos;prepare for anything&apos; approach, honestly, it&apos;s not a good fit for our class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s certainly easier just focusing on one thing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, it will be a gamble. If it&apos;s announced on the day that it&apos;s a written exam after all, our chances of winning might disappear. However...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know what you think, Sudou-kun, but I&apos;m on board with Horikita-san&apos;s plan. Studying isn&apos;t a bad thing, but you get less efficient if you overdo it. As a strategy for the class to win, I think a simple one is more effective and not a bad idea.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. I want to share this with the entire class today and have them lead a thoroughly disciplined life starting tomorrow. A single case of tardiness could be the difference between winning and losing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ayanokouji-kun&apos;s class and Ichinose-san&apos;s class had many honor students to begin with.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They could maintain a high standard for their usual perfect attendance and general conduct without even trying.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A mistake on the first step meant we had absolutely no chance of winning.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then we gotta make damn sure no one finds out about this. If Kanji and his crew go blabbing to other classes, we&apos;re screwed.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re absolutely right. Whether or not we can get a head start on the first day is a crucial factor.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, putting the other classes aside, there was no way Ayanokouji-kun couldn&apos;t have figured out something of this level. Class C must have already factored this possibility into their plans. And that meant... just as Karuizawa-san feared, the information might be passed to and shared with Class D...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Ayanokouji&apos;s probably figured it out.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even without me voicing my thoughts, it seemed Sudou-kun had gotten the picture.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was proof that he now fully understood that Ayanokouji-kun was a formidable opponent.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even so, the other classes may not know. Will you cooperate?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. I&apos;ve even got an idea how to make those guys stick to the rules and keep their mouths shut.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He thumped his chest with his fist and puffed it out. He looked very dependable.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But just keeping Ike-kun and the others in line won&apos;t be enough. Our class has one major obstacle.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;An obstacle?... Ah, I see. Is that why you came here, Horikita-san?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">An image of the problem child who never listened to anyone came to both Onodera-san&apos;s and Sudou-kun&apos;s minds.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;He isn&apos;t a student who is normally late or absent without reason, but his classroom attitude and private life are highly problematic. If he alone were to drag us down, our class would have absolutely no chance of winning.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To win the special exam, getting his cooperation became our most important task.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But that&apos;s impossible, ain&apos;t it? Gettin&apos; that guy to do what he&apos;s told.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Simply asking him will likely be impossible. However, I have an idea. To that end, I am thinking of visiting Koenji-kun&apos;s room, but I&apos;ve never been there before. Therefore, I would like a classmate to accompany me, just in case.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Koenji&apos;s room? Right now? Yeah, that does sound a little dicey. If that guy goes feral, even I wouldn&apos;t be able to stop him easily. Nah, you made the right call not goin&apos; alone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That wasn&apos;t the sort of thing I was worried about, but...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t feel like pressing the issue, so I let it slide.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So, that&apos;s the situation. Would you mind if I borrowed Sudou-kun for a bit? I believe I can have him back in ten or twenty minutes, at the earliest.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If that&apos;s the case, be my guest. Well then, I&apos;ll just hang around and wait.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun readily agreed on the spot, and after he quickly put on his shoes, the two of us stepped out into the hallway.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 9</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WHEN WE ARRIVED at Koenji-kun&apos;s room, Sudou-kun stepped forward and took the lead for me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no response from inside the room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder if he hasn&apos;t returned yet...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I thought he would be in his room at this hour, but maybe that was wishful thinking...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Who knows? That guy seems like he&apos;d just ignore anything he finds annoying, so I can&apos;t say.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With that, he knocked repeatedly on the door with brute force. It was very helpful that he could do something I would hesitate to do without a second thought.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After the sound echoed in the surrounding area for a while, the door, which had long been silent, finally opened.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yo, Koenji. So you were in there after all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh my, oh my. To think the fools at my door were none other than my own classmates.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I apologize for showing up unannounced so late at night. I have a simple request for you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you wished to make an appointment with me, I should think you would have used your cell phone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Using a cell phone with no guarantee of reaching you would be a problem if this waited until tomorrow. Besides, I determined that you wouldn&apos;t listen to my request unless I came to speak with you in person. It&apos;s about the special exam.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wondered what this request could be, but are you still not giving up, Horikita-girl? I was under the impression you&apos;d seen the light during our discussion this afternoon.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You don&apos;t have to participate in our discussions. And you don&apos;t need to shoulder anything for the class by yourself. But that&apos;s not it... If this were a request that would benefit you, wouldn&apos;t you have room to consider it? If you hear me out, I&apos;m sure it won&apos;t be a bad deal for you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t want to overuse the tactic of dangling a carrot in front of an unmoving horse.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But it was also true that I couldn&apos;t afford to say such things when dealing with Koenji-kun.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmm. In that case, I suppose I shall hear you out.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As he said that, the door swung wide open before we could say anything else.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It would be difficult to talk with our voices echoing outside, wouldn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps having realized something when he heard the words <em>special exam</em>, he welcomed us into his entryway.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Never thought I&apos;d see the day I&apos;d be stepping into your room.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Fufufu. You should be purely delighted, red-hair-kun. You are the first man to have ever set foot in my room.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Not happy about it at all...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After making a gagging gesture, Sudou-kun crossed his arms and stood a half-step in front of me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now then, shall you let me hear this story of yours? Something about it being beneficial to me?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. Starting tomorrow, I want you to simply come to school as usual, without being late or absent, and to refrain from idle chatter during class. On top of that, for one week only, I want you to correct all the behaviors that are considered problematic: your posture and habits in class, and any actions that disrupt discipline at Keyaki Mall and other places.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Up to this point, it was merely a request from our side. It was obvious that Koenji-kun would not nod his head in agreement for that alone. So I immediately got to the part that would benefit him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you exhibit no problematic behavior whatsoever, just like the other classmates, I will pay you 200,000 Private Points.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Essentially 200,000 yen just for being mindful of the rules for one week. This was undeniably an exceptional reward.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun gave me a look that said I was paying too much. But without this level of impact, Koenji-kun wouldn&apos;t budge. Besides, I was prepared to pay up to 300,000 points in the unlikely event he complained it was not enough.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So, you believe that following the rules is the basis of this exam, do you, Horikita-girl?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Coincidentally, that is precisely what I was thinking, myself.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That is... a coincidence. I would very much like you to tell me how you arrived at that conclusion.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Most likely the same way you did. From our teacher&apos;s attitude.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Unlike me, who&apos;d just had a flash of insight, Koenji-kun may have realized it at a much earlier stage. I wanted to ask for details, but I decided against it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;However, the world is full of various happenings. As it stands, the probability of this being an exam that requires discipline is about 70%. In the event that it is an entirely unrelated exam, or if we lose due to factors other than myself, I will still expect to be paid the full amount in Private Points. Is that acceptable?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey, that ain&apos;t fair!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There is nothing more expensive than something free, you know. If you cannot accept this, then I can offer no cooperation whatsoever.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I expected you would say that. I don&apos;t mind accepting those terms, but you will have to avoid any action that could result in even a single point deduction.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Fufufu. Then our negotiations are complete. For one week, starting tomorrow&#x2014;I shall sell you my discipline. For the exceptional price of 500,000 Private Points.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...You&apos;re asking for quite a lot, aren&apos;t you? I can hardly believe that&apos;s an amount you&apos;re willing to negotiate.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even this is me showing considerable kindness, you know? Not being able to check my bangs with a hand mirror during class when I am so concerned with them is painful for one such as myself who pursues beauty. Besides, I am the type who prefers to conclude negotiations in one fell swoop rather than probing each other&apos;s true intentions.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That last remark was his way of saying he&apos;d seen through the fact that I hadn&apos;t set 200,000 Private Points as my upper limit. He hit me where it hurt, just like he did after school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s way too much. Just because you&apos;re takin&apos; advantage of our weakness, you think you can get away with&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I stopped Sudou-kun, who was on the verge of grabbing him, cutting him off.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Forcing your own arbitrary sense of what&apos;s normal is also out of the question, I trust you understand that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course. It will be a sad time from tomorrow, not being able to take in the sight of my beautiful, long legs upon my desk, but I suppose it can&apos;t be helped.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Are you sure about this, Suzune? Five hundred thousand Private Points is just&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The deal is done. And don&apos;t speak of this contract to anyone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a gamble. Just as Koenji-kun said, there was no guarantee that obeying school regulations was part of the special exam rules, and a 70% chance would&apos;ve been more than enough. But for now, I had to act with the belief that I could seize that probability; I wouldn&apos;t be able to defeat the other classes without shouldering that much risk.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Above all else, Koenji-kun would never act in an ordinary way. Had my request been something no one else could imitate, such as asking him to get a high score on an exam or take the top spot in sports, there would&apos;ve been no room for negotiation at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But this time, it was the opposite kind of request: for him to simply do nothing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Although it was a large sum, he accepted my proposal of Private Points.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well then, it is about time you took your leave. Today will be another long night for me. Or perhaps, Horikita Girl, would you care to accompany me for this long night? I promise you won&apos;t regret it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Like hell! We&apos;re leaving, Suzune.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun took my arm and, as if he didn&apos;t want me to linger for a second longer, opened the front door and stepped out into the hallway.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Damn that guy... Spouting such nonsense...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As we were leaving, Sudou-kun quietly cursed in front of the elevator.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It was a foolish joke, so pay it no mind. Besides, the negotiations were concluded successfully.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But still, isn&apos;t five hundred thousand just for doing nothing way too steep?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If the other students heard about this, they&apos;d no doubt complain. But rest assured. I have no intention of burdening anyone else in the class with the cost.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not what I mean... Before we even get to whether you&apos;re paying for it yourself, don&apos;t you think there&apos;ll be people who won&apos;t be convinced if they find out about this proposal?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s right. That&apos;s why I asked you to accompany me. You won&apos;t tell anyone else about this negotiation, will you, Sudou-kun?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a request to keep it &apos;confidential&apos; while avoiding a direct statement.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun looked a little bewildered, but he nodded firmly as he reached for the elevator&apos;s up button.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-well... If you&apos;re asking me to keep my mouth shut, then I won&apos;t tell anyone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Thank you. Oh, the down button is fine.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I reached past Sudou-kun&apos;s slowly extending arm and pressed the down button.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m going to take the stairs back. Well then, you were a great help today for agreeing to my sudden request. Good night.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After thanking him, I turned toward the emergency exit door.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Suzune!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A voice called out, stopping me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What is it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&#x2014;I, um...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if he hadn&apos;t decided what to say, or if he had, but the words wouldn&apos;t come out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seeing him so flustered, his voice catching in his throat, I tilted my head slightly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you have something you want to say, you can tell me. Did you disapprove of my methods?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not it. It&apos;s not like that... I... I might not be a replacement for Ayanokouji, but...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ayanokouji. At his name, I found my eyes widening slightly in surprise.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m not strong like him, and I&apos;m not smart either... But still, I&apos;ll always be on your side, Suzune. So&#x2014;whenever you&apos;re in trouble, make sure you rely on me like you did today.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sudou-kun told me so, his eyes, filled with strong resolve, fixed on me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You held the class back quite a bit during our first year, so for this next year, I&apos;ll need you to do your best until the very end.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ugh&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In response to my merciless words, Sudou-kun gave a wry smile, as if he&apos;d broken out in a cold sweat.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The elevator is here. Say hello to Onodera-san for me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, uh, yeah. Well... see you tomorrow...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, see you tomorrow.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I watched Sudou-kun step into the elevator, then headed for the emergency stairwell.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Thank you, Sudou-kun. Your words made me very happy.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The reason I couldn&apos;t thank him honestly was because I couldn&apos;t help but feel a warmth spreading through my chest. I had allies. I had reliable classmates. I couldn&apos;t remain in despair forever just because Ayanokouji-kun had left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I knew I had to live up to the expectations of those who supported me, no matter what.</p><h1 class="heading_1"><a class="heading-link" href="/read/ss-y3-v2/2">Chapter 4:<br/>The Quest for Knowledge</a></h1><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>IT WAS THE morning after the exam was announced.</em>&#xa0;This day marked the start of the week-long exam period&#x2014;or rather, the preparation period. But I did nothing out of the ordinary. I just woke up in the morning, went to school, paid attention in class, spent my free time after school and prepared for the next day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d given my classmates one instruction, but otherwise, they were free.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They could carry on as usual, or they could study more. I left it all up to their own discretion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was still quite early for school, but with nothing else to do, I decided to keep up my early departure routine for a little while longer. I turned off the TV, put on my shoes, and stepped out into the hall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When the elevator arrived and its doors opened, five girls and one boy were already inside.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This wasn&apos;t an unusual sight, as the elevators were often crowded on mornings before school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Considering the capacity, I could&apos;ve squeezed in, but the three Class A girls who happened to be riding&#x2014;Shinohara, Matsushita, and Inogashira&#x2014;were staring at me with somewhat cold eyes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no point in forcing my way on, so I decided to let it go and wait for the next one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The stairs were an option, but I chose to wait for the next elevator.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This one also had three girls on it, but there was still some room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, my feet felt a little heavy, and for a moment, I hesitated to get on. But there was clearly space, and it would&apos;ve been unnatural not to get on. With that thought, I stepped forward. I turned around inside the elevator&#xa0;to face the doors, and it soon arrived at the first floor.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As the last one to get on, I was naturally the first to step out into the lobby. I took a few steps and stopped, and the two girls behind me immediately walked past.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Good morning.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One girl remained. She spoke to me from behind in a small voice, careful not to startle me. It was Hiyori, who&apos;d been in the elevator with me. I&apos;d seen her nearby or passed her in the halls several times like this, but we&apos;d never had a proper conversation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Good morning.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Have you been...well?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Same as always.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a brief exchange. For some reason, I struggled, unable to keep the conversation going.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d envisioned going to the library, first apologizing about my class transfer, and then maybe talking about books... I wondered if it was because we were in such an unexpected place.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No, that was just a flimsy excuse. In this small community, I never knew when or where a coincidence like this would leave us alone together. It could&apos;ve happened yesterday or the day before. It was something I should&apos;ve been able to anticipate from the start.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&#x2014;And yet, I couldn&apos;t find the words to say. That never happened before.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Feeling a strange sensation, we started to walk together.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was only a short, few-minute walk to school, but I was sure a conversation would arise naturally as we went.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or so I hoped.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, at times like this, unexpected events tended to pile up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as we stepped outside, looking for the right moment to speak, I noticed a trio standing a short distance from the dorms, watching the lobby: Hashimoto, Morishita, and Shiraishi. It seemed I was their target, as Hashimoto approached me alone, acting as their representative.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Yo, good mornin&apos;. And mornin&apos; to you too, Shiina.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto offered his morning greeting with his usual casual demeanor.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Wanted to check on somethin&apos; related to our class before we get to school, y&apos;see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I always welcomed contact from Class C students, but the timing of this couldn&apos;t have been worse. But Hiyori, either reading the mood or wanting to get away quickly, greeted the three of them and started walking to school by herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Whoops, was I interruptin&apos; somethin&apos;...? My bad.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He apologized for form&apos;s sake, but considering he was using it as a convenient way to drive away an outsider who wasn&apos;t his classmate, it was clear his choice of words was deliberate.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s fine.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I wanted to have a proper talk with Hiyori, the issue could be resolved simply by heading to the library as I&apos;d originally planned.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d known that for a long time. I kept procrastinating, but considering what happened today, I knew I had to go and properly clear the air soon.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, you certainly don&apos;t discriminate, do you? Are you trying to make a move on Shiina Hiyori, too?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why would you think that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s what you say, but I wonder what the truth of the matter is.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s wrong with that? It doesn&apos;t matter how many cute girls he gets close to. Besides, setting your sights on Shiina-san... I&apos;d say you have excellent taste. I think it shows a wonderful eye for beauty!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I could see Shiraishi&apos;s eyes sparkle as she watched Hiyori walk away.
