It brought back memories of my recent conversation with Shimazaki and Yoshida.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In any case, there was no point in continuing this pointless conversation, so I prompted Hashimoto to move things along.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Bout that special exam plan you sent out yesterday. I wanted to confirm it with ya.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I was curious about that as well. Since Hashimoto-kun appeared to be waiting for Ayanokouji-kun, I decided to take the opportunity to join you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And Morishita, you too? You seem surprisingly interested&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I am merely an onlooker.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The onlooker declared curtly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You laid out the instructions for the special exam in the group chat last night, right? When I saw that, I was impressed, man. Thought you were finally gettin&apos; serious about leadin&apos; us.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I posted in the group chat that I once again believed it was highly likely the school was making our daily conduct part of the exam. I conveyed some finer points, such as recommending that students who weren&apos;t confident in their actions shouldn&apos;t leave the dorms, as weekends couldn&apos;t be taken lightly in addition to our weekday behavior.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I bet you&apos;ll manage the class more stably than we could ever imagine. Things were a real pain in other ways back when it was Sakayanagi. You think so too, don&apos;t you, Shiraishi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto tossed the topic to Shiraishi, seeking her agreement, but he received an unexpected reply.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, I hold our former leader, Sakayanagi-san, in high regard. It&apos;s true that she had a habit of making decisions on her own as she pleased, but she maintained our position as Class A for two years without ever once letting it fall.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In reality, if an irregular existence like mine hadn&apos;t been there, she likely would have maintained control of the class for three years without issue and led them to graduate as Class A.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I, too, agree with that statement. Only Hashimoto Masayoshi seems oddly negative, despite having been by her side.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s with that suspicious look...? I&apos;m just sayin&apos; it was a huge pain precisely because I was her aide, you know? Besides, in the end, it was Sakayanagi&apos;s fault we dropped to Class C as a result of her doin&apos; whatever she wanted.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto spoke with an attitude that suggested he was completely without fault.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh? I don&apos;t believe so. Shall I specifically elaborate on the reason for you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Nah, I&apos;ll pass. Just listenin&apos; to your crazy talk is exhausting.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That would be wise, traitorous Hashimoto THE <em>Righteous</em>.&quot;<sup id="fnref-1-eea6e4d7-8987-50ea-8261-1c9f1ec8ffb4"><a class="footnote-ref" href="#fn-1-eea6e4d7-8987-50ea-8261-1c9f1ec8ffb4" data-ref="1" epub:type="noteref">[1]</a></sup></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s with &apos;THE&apos;? What do you mean, &apos;THE&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To this incomprehensible nickname, Hashimoto retorted like a comedian.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh my, what do you mean by &apos;traitorous&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t mind it at all. That&apos;s just Morishita sayin&apos; whatever comes to mind, like always. More importantly, let me get us back on topic.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as Hashimoto got the conversation back on track and was about to return to the topic of the exam, Kondou and Komiya from Ryuuen&apos;s class emerged from the lobby and approached us. Their timing was so perfect it seemed planned.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The new class leader and his cronies, huh? Scheming somethin&apos; evil this early in the morning?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kondou circled around us, glancing at everyone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Long time no see, Kondou. Looks like you&apos;ve been keepin&apos; your distance from our class lately.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, things are different now. We&apos;re Class B, and you guys are Class C. We&apos;re only ever on guard against the classes above us.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even as he said we weren&apos;t worth his time, his words were contradicted by his attempt to make contact, and above all, a faint, unconcealable tension leaked from Kondou&apos;s voice.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then you could&apos;ve just ignored us today, too, you know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just thought you might get lonely if I didn&apos;t mess with you a little.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It&apos;d been the same the other day when Ryuuen came into our classroom, but the usually fussy Morishita became abnormally quiet, like a mouse. At that moment, she was staring at the trees with a feigned air of ignorance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Besides, I figured if you were havin&apos; an interesting chat, you could let us in on it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Unfortunately, we were just lamentin&apos; the start of another boring day.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was almost certain that Ryuuen had ordered Kondou and his group to decipher the exam rules. Perhaps they were just trying to get any hint they could. Or perhaps, like us, they&apos;d already pinpointed the exam&apos;s rules and sent Kondou and the others as agents.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the end, Kondou and his group, who stuck to our side, continued to apply silent pressure without saying or doing a thing, right up until we entered the school building and went our separate ways in the classroom.</p><hr class="footnote-rule"/><ol class="footnotes"><li data-id="eea6e4d7-8987-50ea-8261-1c9f1ec8ffb4" data-ref="1" class="footnote first-footnote" id="fn-1-eea6e4d7-8987-50ea-8261-1c9f1ec8ffb4"><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><a class="footnote-backref" href="#fnref-1-eea6e4d7-8987-50ea-8261-1c9f1ec8ffb4">[1]: </a>Hashimoto&apos;s given name Masayoshi (&#x6b63;&#x7fa9;) means justice/righteousness, Morishita is jokingly saying &quot;the&quot; in english here to emphasize how his last name means righteous even though he&apos;s a &quot;traitor.&quot;</p></li></ol><h2 class="heading_2">Part 1</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WHEN SCHOOL LET out, Hashimoto immediately approached and spoke to me before I could make a move.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Can I assume you&apos;re free today? Let me continue what we were talking about this morning.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m sorry, but I have plans, so I must decline. Please try not to cry, even if you&apos;re hurt.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita, who sat behind me, rejected Hashimoto&apos;s invitation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m not asking you... I&apos;m talking to Ayanokouji, Ayanokouji.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I expected this would happen ever since our conversation was interrupted this morning, but at this rate, the opportunity to head to the library was slipping further away.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Should I turn him down once and prioritize meeting Hiyori?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No... we already entered the exam period.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If that was the case, for the sake of sound class management, I should probably put off showing my face at the completely private library. There was a chance I could still meet her after this discussion was over.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Shall we change locations?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That sounds like a good idea. Seems like a troublesome phantom&apos;s latched onto your back, Ayanokouji.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I followed Hashimoto, who urged me to get up from my seat, and we headed for the hallway.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shiraishi, who&apos;d been there in the morning, was now talking with a girl named Nakajima, perhaps planning to spend time with her after school. She seemed to notice us, but right after, the two of them left the classroom side-by-side.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The caf&#xe9; at Keyaki Mall is good, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had no particular reason to refuse, so just as I was about to give a brief reply&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That will be on Hashimoto Masayoshi&apos;s treat, correct?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not that I wouldn&apos;t treat you if you&apos;re telling me to... but hey, why are you coming along?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Since you called me a phantom, I thought I would make good on your words for the sake of consistency. For the honor of phantoms.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wasn&apos;t sure if it was necessary to be consistent or protect the honor of spiritual beings, but she probably didn&apos;t approve of Hashimoto and I talking about various things by ourselves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Jeez. Do you intend to monitor me so thoroughly?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s a path that cannot be avoided for the sake of sound class management. Well, I won&apos;t be asking for anything too expensive, so please rest assured. I&apos;m a woman who is kind to wallets, you know.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I said I&apos;m not treating you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;ll treat Ayanokouji Kiyotaka but not me? Is this... gender discrimination...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I couldn&apos;t tell if her face was one of astonishment, but Morishita&apos;s eyes widened as she muttered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I shall post on social media and request an all-out assault from my comrades.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She promptly took out her phone and began to tap her fingertips on it over and over.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, geez, whenever you show up, the conversation never moves forward.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you wish for the conversation to proceed, why not graciously pay the price for some tea? It&apos;s a small price to pay for things to go smoothly.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Agh, damn it, fine. I&apos;ll treat you, so at least be a little quiet while we&apos;re having a serious discussion. That&apos;s an order, got it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps those words were the deciding factor, as Morishita gave a small nod and immediately closed her mouth. Then, she pressed her left thumb and index finger together and made a small sliding motion from right to left across her lips. I supposed it meant she was zipping her mouth shut.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After confirming that she&apos;d actually gone quiet, we started walking again, and Hashimoto looked at me and began to speak.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I spent all day yesterday looking into the other classes.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Did you get any results?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Unfortunately, there wasn&apos;t anything you could call a real find. I guess it&apos;s no surprise that it&apos;s hard to make a move in a special exam with no rules explained. All three classes were quiet. Or rather, I should say they&apos;re no different than usual.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This probably wasn&apos;t the main topic; Hashimoto continued while lightly reporting on it and moving on.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Those instructions you gave us last night, or rather, your idea... did you tell Ichinose about it too?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course. As allies, we need to share information.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Is that so? Well, it&apos;s more convenient for us if Class D ranks high right now anyway. If we take first place and Ichinose takes second, then there&apos;s nothing to complain about.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t that he was completely without complaints, but for now, it was probably just a confirmation to be safe.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If the exam is just like you predicted, Ayanokouji, then Ryuuen&apos;s class is very likely to lose again. If Horikita&apos;s class is even one step too late in noticing these rules, it&apos;ll be irreversible. And even if there&apos;s an unexpected exam waiting for us, we won&apos;t lose if it&apos;s a battle of academics. I think we&apos;ve got this one in the bag.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The moment you let your guard down is when the rug gets pulled out from under you. Besides, Horikita&apos;s no fool. She already realized as of yesterday the possibility that &apos;school life attitude&apos; is being watched, just as I told you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I told him that with conviction, he stopped walking for a moment and looked back at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Huh? Is that right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, after sensing from my demeanor that my statement was true, he started walking again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;How can you say for sure that she&apos;s figured it out?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It just means that getting a class to unite isn&apos;t that simple.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I told him only that, Hashimoto must have understood something, as he let out a short whistle.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You must have a skilled scout, or maybe you&apos;ve got a spy planted in there&#x2014;either way, you&apos;re skillfully extracting the situation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Normally, the inquisitive Hashimoto would probably want to pursue the matter further, including whether I was already using Yamamura, but he didn&apos;t. He understood instinctively that it wasn&apos;t a wise move to carelessly dig too deep here and worsen my impression of him.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 2</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">AS THE THREE of us headed toward a cafe in Keyaki Mall, a female student standing by the entrance spotted us and gave a light wave. After Hashimoto and I exchanged a glance, we approached her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I thought I would join you all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The one who answered was Shiraishi, who I thought had left the classroom earlier with Nakajima.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Where&apos;s Nakajima?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We were just chatting for a bit, then we parted ways. You&apos;re well aware that I was with Nakajima-san, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka is always ogling Shiraishi Asuka from afar, so it&apos;s a piece of cake for him. Right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita patted my shoulder and gave me a firm thumbs-up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Could you not make me out to be a pervert?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you afraid of being disliked? Even though you&apos;re being hated by everyone in your previous class?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whoa, whoa, just &apos;cause it&apos;s true doesn&apos;t mean you have to say it out loud. Besides, he&apos;s got us now. I mean, as a guy, I totally get wanting to stare at Shiraishi,&quot; he said, patting my other shoulder and giving a firm thumbs-up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;He may be like this, but please try not to hate him.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how I looked at it, Morishita seemed to be leading the effort to make me disliked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I were to say, at that moment, that I was just trying to keep track of the entire class, it would only be taken as an excuse.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh my, I&apos;m actually very happy. It&apos;s quite the honor to have someone you&apos;re interested in keep their eyes on you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For real? Good for you, Ayanokouji. Sounds like you&apos;ve got a shot.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This probably wasn&apos;t the first time he&apos;d heard that kind of reply from Shiraishi. Hashimoto also played along, knowing it was half a joke.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As for me, I was at a loss for how to respond either way, so I just let it slide.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You get it, right, Morishita?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll be quiet for the discussion, so rest assured. Just make sure you treat me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Now a group of four, we each placed our orders and took an empty table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was what you&apos;d call &apos;our usual spot&apos;.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a strange thing, but people have that sort of natural habituation, like a routine.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They feel a sense of security in places they&apos;ve been before, and they subconsciously choose seats that meet detailed criteria, such as the view from the seat, the way the light comes in, or the distance to the next table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was likely influenced not only by physical aspects, but also by the consolidation of memory.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Positive factors, such as having a pleasant time with the people you were with, an enjoyable conversation, or coming up with a good idea, couldn&apos;t be ignored. Conversely, if one had bitter memories associated with that seat, there would be a tendency to avoid it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A rational psychological mechanism. I could guess that Hashimoto and Morishita had a similar feeling.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shiraishi joined us, but I couldn&apos;t see any change in the atmosphere, for better or for worse.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shiraishi was good at blending into the atmosphere; that was the impression she gave.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Jeez, you really made me buy you an expensive drink. You know my financial situation, yet you still ordered the most insane thing without a second thought...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As payment for her silence, Hashimoto treated her to a new frapp&#xe9; made with a generous amount of strawberries. Just at a glance, it looked like it contained about five whole strawberries. While most cafe drinks were between 500 and, at most, 800 Private Points, this strawberry frapp&#xe9; was 1,300 Private Points.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;My mother taught me to go all out when someone else is paying. I was simply following her teachings faithfully.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She answered nonchalantly with a statement that was exquisitely hard to judge, though I figured it was probably a lie.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To be honest, my instincts were telling me I just wanted a regular caff&#xe8; latte, but... <em>sigh</em>.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She added those words with a look of distaste and unenthusiastically plunged her straw into the strawberry frapp&#xe9;.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then you should&apos;ve just ordered the caff&#xe8; latte...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While watching their exchange out of the corner of my eye, I had a sip of my coffee and took a breath.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I moistened my throat in anticipation of the upcoming discussion with Hashimoto.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Alright, time for a serious discussion.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Upon hearing that, Morishita mimed zipping her lips from right to left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was probably a signal to reassure us that her lips were sealed tight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You seem fired up, but to be honest, I don&apos;t intend to be particularly concerned with winning or losing this exam,&quot; I stated quietly, as all three of them turned their gazes toward me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you mean by that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto&#x2014;no, almost every student&#x2014;intended to win every single exam for the coming year.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was why his question, laced with suspicion, was a normal reaction to hearing I wasn&apos;t concerned with the outcome.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This was true for the last special exam as well, but the changes in Class Points weren&apos;t exactly astounding, and they didn&apos;t have a major impact. As for this exam, the reward is only about half of the previous one.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, yeah, it&apos;s true that even if we were to lose this one by some chance, the damage might not be that bad. But what do you mean you&apos;re not concerned with winning or losing?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He greedily aimed for even a single rank higher.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto looked disgusted by my statement, which went against that stance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, I had no intention of taking a harsh stance on this exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To be precise, it may be more accurate to say that it&apos;s not that I don&apos;t care about winning or losing, but rather that I can&apos;t.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps finding some sense in that simplified explanation, Shiraishi smiled and nodded.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I believe I have some understanding of what you wish to say. You think that the information you shared with us, Ayanokouji-kun, is what&apos;s most likely the real focus of the exam, correct?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The details of the exam were kept hidden. Therefore, all the third-year students had to spend the week considering several possibilities for the exam&apos;s content. Some of those possibilities, such as a written test, were directly tied to one&apos;s abilities, so normally, one should&apos;&apos;ve adopted Hashimoto&apos;s mindset of wanting to win and taking action to increase their odds. Simply studying for a week would have brought one closer to victory.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But the exam I considered most likely was based on &apos;everyday conduct&apos;.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You can avoid obvious demerits, but you can&apos;t earn merits. The only thing we can do is wait for the other classes to make a mistake. That&apos;s why I think it&apos;s pointless to get all worked up.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So you don&apos;t think this&apos;ll be a battle of academics or athletics, huh? But if that&apos;s the case, wouldn&apos;t it be better to let everyone know that from the start? There are plenty of guys gettin&apos; all worked up so they&apos;ll be ready for whatever exam comes, y&apos;know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s fine to let them put in the effort. Since the rules are unclear, it&apos;s not like they can overdo it by focusing on just one thing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto&apos;s expression remained somewhat stiff, as if he was still unconvinced. Morishita was silent as usual, so I thought our conversation about the current exam was coming to an end, but Shiraishi unexpectedly took an interest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;May I ask a small question? You&apos;ve concluded that the exam is based on our everyday conduct, Ayanokouji-kun, but what would you say the probability of that is?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Over 90%. At least, that&apos;s my assessment.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Damn, that&apos;s crazy high. Guess I should be more on board with this, then. The day this turns out to be right is the day my faith in Ayanokouji goes through the roof.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What led you to determine that probability? It&apos;s impossible to clearly tell if this week is a preparation period or the exam itself, which is exactly why everyone is speculating on all fronts. It seems you based your judgment on Mashima-sensei&apos;s comment to &apos;try to act in a way that befits a student,&apos; Ayanokouji-kun, but I feel that alone is insufficient grounds for such a conclusion.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Shiraishi was demanding to know the basis for my assertion of over 90%, given the few hints we had.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it was only Mashima-sensei&apos;s comment, the probability wouldn&apos;t even have reached 50%.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But were there any other remarks that caught your attention? We didn&apos;t miss anything, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto sought confirmation from Shiraishi and Morishita as he recalled his exchange with Mashima-sensei from the previous day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can&apos;t think of anything.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As Shiraishi denied it, Morishita also nodded, her lips sealed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In reality, no other hints were given. Not from Class C, that is.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whaddya mean by that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For this exam, what some students latched onto was Mashima-sensei&apos;s phrasing, &apos;try to act in a way that befits a student.&apos;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, that stuck out to me too. But we can&apos;t be sure if it&apos;s actually related to the exam, can we?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it&apos;s ambiguous and unclear, then we should verify it. What I focused on was the next step: whether or not those words themselves held any meaning. And if they did, the next thing to investigate is whether that statement was common to all classes. If the school instructed them to convey the message without changing a single word, then you can be confident that the words themselves are meaningful.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You mean what the teachers of the other three classes said&#x2014;is that it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s right. We have no information on Sakagami-sensei, but Hoshinomiya-sensei apparently said, &apos;You&apos;ll be fine as your usual selves.&apos; Chabashira-sensei supposedly said, &apos;I want you to show me that you&apos;re different from when you enrolled.&apos;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If one added Mashima-sensei&apos;s statement to those, the rule being used for the exam became clear. The three others present would likely be able to figure it out as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A single statement shouldn&apos;t have been enough to narrow it down, but adding the information from the other classes feels like it&apos;s removing all the static.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, you&apos;re dead on... At this point, I can&apos;t even imagine the rules being about anything other than our conduct.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto let out a breath with a wry smile, making no attempt to hide his admiration.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Horikita&apos;s class, which lost all its Class Points at the start of the year due to their poor conduct.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ichinose&apos;s class, which, despite receiving a certain number of demerits, had steadily produced results.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And Sakayanagi&apos;s class, which had been more stable than any other.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One could interpret them as pieces of advice from the teachers that corresponded to their respective results.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;By the way, how I procured information from the two classes is a trade secret.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto and Morishita could probably guess that the information about Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s statement came from Ichinose, but because Shiraishi was also here and because it also included information about Horikita&apos;s class, I kept the sources hidden.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As for the alliance, I could just announce it when the time was right.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Everyday conduct is something you practice all the time; it can&apos;t decide the outcome. So that&apos;s why you&apos;re not hung up on it this time&#x2014;it&apos;s all startin&apos; to make sense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m glad you seem to understand. More than that, I want the three of you to focus, not on this current exam, but on the <em>next</em>&#xa0;special exam.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The next one? You think the next special exam is likely to be a huge battle?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. Considering the last exam and this one, it wouldn&apos;t be strange for a high-stakes one to come next.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you considering the possibility that someone might be expelled?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can&apos;t say for certain, but I wouldn&apos;t be surprised if that was included in the rules with a fairly high probability.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sure, not every exam is gonna be risk-free, but aren&apos;t the high-stakes ones supposed to be rare?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto posed the question, wondering if it was really something to be so cautious about.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, I wouldn&apos;t say that&apos;s necessarily the case. It was clear from observing the upperclassmen over the last two years that the third-years&apos; risk of expulsion is higher compared to the other grades.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The one who offered a reply was Morishita, who&apos;d properly remained silent up to that point.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her tone suggested she understood what happened with Horikita Manabu&apos;s and Nagumo Miyabi&apos;s generations.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Come to think of it, last year, didn&apos;t a pretty big number of students get expelled by the time the second term started?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s correct. About fifteen students were expelled. Nagumo Miyabi did as he pleased, you see. Even if that was an extreme case, it&apos;s unlikely that this year alone will see everyone pass through unscathed.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Getting expelled during a time like this would seriously suck, but damn, you&apos;ve got a good memory.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This is merely common sense. Common sense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto kept several sensitive antennae tuned to information about his own grade, but it seemed he paid little attention to the other years.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey, I thought you were just gonna listen quietly? What happened to zippin&apos; it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh? When did I ever say such a thing? At what hour, what minute, what second, and after how many of the Earth&apos;s rotations?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? What the hell is that supposed to mean? The Earth&apos;s what-now...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Good grief. Do the youths of today truly not know the statements conceived by the great figures of the past?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was confident that my knowledge of great historical figures and their statements was fairly comprehensive, but I had no memory of that one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The Earth took about twenty-four hours to make one rotation. More precisely, the twenty-four-hour day was defined based on the time it took to make one rotation. With that as a premise, the Earth was about 4.5 billion years old. If I were to start calculating that out, with 365 days in a year plus leap years and so on&#x2014;no, this was a completely useless calculation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you&apos;re truly that dissatisfied, I shall return your drink.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Return it, you say?... It&apos;s empty!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was because while she wasn&apos;t talking, she&apos;d been silently and diligently drinking her strawberry frapp&#xe9; by herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if she chugged it all just so she could start talking.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 3</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">AFTER THAT, I focused on Hashimoto and Shiraishi to get information about Class C.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I asked about personal matters that couldn&apos;t be understood from the OAA or written exams alone, like who was on good terms with whom, and who wasn&apos;t. Although Hashimoto knew a lot about the class, there seemed to be many things that only girls could see, and I was able to hear a few useful stories that made me nod in agreement.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">About an hour passed since we started chatting, and the caf&#xe9; gradually grew more crowded as evening approached.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I started to consider wrapping things up&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Crap... Ayanokouji again...?!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A voice exclaimed, and the pair who arrived at the next table turned to us with a look of dismay.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was Ike and Hondou, whom I ran into at the school cafeteria yesterday afternoon.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We&apos;re fated to meet.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At my reply, he narrowed his eyes, his displeasure obvious.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you mean, &apos;fated to meet&apos;?! Don&apos;t act so familiar, you traitor.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Disgust oozed from his expression. I thought they would immediately move to another table, like at the cafeteria, but with few empty seats available, the two of them sat down reluctantly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seeing the Class A students, Hashimoto deliberately chose to act casual.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We&apos;re in different classes, but we&apos;re still students at the same school, so let&apos;s get along, eh? Or are you planning to complain every time you see his face?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not like I mean to... but you can see why I&apos;m pissed, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ike and his friend, not wanting to direct their frustration at Hashimoto, glanced over and glared at me again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why&apos;re you so bent out of shape about it? It&apos;s not like Ayanokouji transferred from C to A. Just changing classes had no effect on your Class Points. It&apos;s not like it cost you anything out of your own pockets. And besides, you guys are the almighty Class A, aren&apos;t ya?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though it was a struggle, he tried to flatter them to somehow reduce their pointless frustration.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, we&apos;re Class A, but Ayanokouji hid how smart he was. That&apos;s pretty much like betraying the class from the start, ain&apos;t it? And then the second he transferred, bam, a perfect score. I&apos;ve never seen such a nasty move.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I produced proper results in the latter half of my time in Horikita&apos;s class, but perhaps for Ike and the others, that was also part of the problem.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And besides&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seemingly with more to complain about, Ike averted his gaze from Hashimoto and looked at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m hearing all sorts of nasty rumors, y&apos;know. That you intentionally set up both Sakura and Maezono to get expelled from the very beginning. Don&apos;t tell me... You didn&apos;t get Haruki expelled too, did you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A former friend of ours. The name of a student who&apos;d been especially close to Ike and Sudou came up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Haruki? Who&apos;s that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if the name didn&apos;t ring a bell, Hashimoto looked blankly and tilted his head a little to the left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yamauchi Haruki!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He slammed his hand on the table and shot to his feet. The cup shook from the impact, but thankfully, it didn&apos;t tip over.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ike had been complaining but remained calm until now, but it seemed that was an unexpected landmine.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yamauchi... Oh, ahh, right, there was a student with that name. My bad, my bad. He was in another class, so I totally forgot about him. But I remember now. Anyway, Sakayanagi was the one who did that, right? Ayanokouji had nothin&apos; to do with it, man. It&apos;s way outta line to blame him for every little thing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Apologizing for having genuinely forgotten, Hashimoto hastily tried to calm him down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll never let a guy like you get me expelled, you hear me!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey, I said calm down, Ike. No need to go raising such a huge death flag for yourself. Nobody wins if we start trading barbs here.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seeing Ike&apos;s increasingly heated and uncontrollable attitude, even Hashimoto started to panic. He probably couldn&apos;t care less about what Ike and Hondou were feeling, but he knew this commotion wasn&apos;t good for his class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He had to have heard about one of the possible rules for this special exam from Horikita, but I wondered if he really understood it. On the other hand, if this was all an act&#x2014;a strategy to drag down Class C&#x2014;then it might be worthy of praise, but it wasn&apos;t a clever method, as it also increased the risk of their own defeat. If he raised his voice any further and continued his belligerent attitude, he would inevitably be noticed and without a doubt be subject to a penalty.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Okay... Maybe I was overreacting a bit, but we in Class A have the right to complain this much. Just how much did they pay you to betray our class?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Their frustration built up considerably, partly due to their defeat in the last special exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That wasn&apos;t a problem that could be easily resolved by trying to smooth it over like Hashimoto did.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed it would be better if I just took the brunt of it and let them vent their anger.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Who can say. It might be twenty million, it might be thirty million. Unfortunately, I have no intention of revealing the exact figure to you here.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was important for me to maintain a cold and detached response.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">By doing so, Ike and the other guy could direct their hatred toward me without any hesitation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And by bringing that back to their class, they could rally together, firmly united against a common enemy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Above all, if I got worked up here, Class C would only be at a disadvantage. Even if the school was monitoring the situation with cameras or external observers, I wanted to paint a picture where Class A was the aggressor, and Class C was trying to settle things peacefully.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was unclear if the school was monitoring us that closely, but even if our daily behavior was unrelated to the exam, it was important to act with discipline like a proper student, as it had a small, yet not insignificant, effect on our monthly Class Points.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The teachers&apos; words to each class would surely be a good opportunity to make our classmates re-evaluate the importance of sticking to the fundamentals.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t you come crying to us later, regretting your decision and wanting to come back to our class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After voicing his complaints one last time, the pair slightly distanced themselves as they returned to their seats.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They just sat down. I didn&apos;t want to make their caf&#xe9; time uncomfortable.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Shouldn&apos;t we head back soon?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I called out to the three of them, picked up my nearly empty cup, and stood up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, good call. We&apos;ve said what we needed to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto replied in agreement, and Morishita and Shiraishi followed suit.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, after we left the caf&#xe9;, Hashimoto turned and glanced back toward Ike&apos;s group.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;All that complaining... Ike&apos;s completely getting worked up over nothing. The way he blames every little thing on Ayanokouji tells me the atmosphere in their class is still pretty damn heavy. They need to get their heads on straight already.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Logically speaking, that may be so, but I believe it would be better to cut them some slack. After all, if I were a student in Class A, I&apos;m sure I would be on edge in their current situation as well.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Maybe so, but still. In the first place, Ayanokouji, was it really okay not to deny taking the money? That guy&apos;s gonna go around spreading all kinds of rumors, true or not.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It doesn&apos;t matter. It&apos;s already being whispered about as a rumor, anyway. The situation won&apos;t change just because it seems a little more plausible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So you don&apos;t care what other people think of you, huh? Still, connecting it to the Yamauchi incident... that&apos;s a bit of a leap, to say the least.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As Hashimoto looked on, exasperated, Morishita turned a suspicious eye toward me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You never know. With a man like Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, you can never tell what he&apos;s doing behind the scenes. It&apos;s a long shot, I know, but he might have had a hand in it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nah, that&apos;s going too far. But if, on the off chance, it were true, that&apos;d make him one hell of a reliable ally. The stronger your allies are, the better, after all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how things turned out, Hashimoto was determined to see it in a positive light.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you overestimate me, you&apos;ll only be more disappointed later.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then I&apos;ll gladly overestimate you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to hedge my bets just in case, but it seemed he wasn&apos;t going to let me.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 4</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>BY THE TIME I left Keyaki Mall, it was past five in the afternoon.</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The sky was still blue, but the evening sun would soon begin to peek through.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sorry, but I&apos;m gonna take a little detour on my way back.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A detour? Where to? I was thinkin&apos; we&apos;d all walk back together.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto asked, looking disappointed, as if he had assumed we would all return to the dorms together.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To school. I was thinking I&apos;d stop by the library.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, Shiina, huh? Now that ya mention it, it seems like I interrupted you two talkin&apos; this mornin&apos;.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Having prioritized his own plans, Hashimoto wore an expression that said he couldn&apos;t argue with that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If that&apos;s the case, I&apos;ll gracefully see ya off today. Let&apos;s head back, Morishita, Shiraishi.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;d rather not. Why should I have to walk home holding hands with Hashimoto Masayoshi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No one said anythin&apos; about holdin&apos; hands. What kinda leap in logic is that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even if we weren&apos;t holding hands, I still wouldn&apos;t walk back with you. I&apos;m going to browse around Keyaki Mall for a bit before I head back. So, if you&apos;ll excuse me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With a graceful turn, Morishita went back inside the mall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems Morishita-san dislikes you more than you imagined, Hashimoto-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t care if she hates me. It doesn&apos;t bother me one bit if she does. Well then, Shiraishi, shall we go together&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m also going to take a small detour on my way back. Let&apos;s walk home together some other time, Hashimoto-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Tch... I see. That&apos;s a shame.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He seemed genuinely unconcerned when Morishita turned him down, but being rejected by Shiraishi as well must have been unexpected. Hashimoto&apos;s shoulders slumped in disappointment as he walked back alone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Would you mind if I walked with you part of the way?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do you have some business at the school?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No. I just thought I&apos;d like to chat with you a little longer, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;With me? I&apos;m not as interesting as Hashimoto, you know. Are you sure?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s just you being modest. You&apos;re a very interesting person, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was hard to feel like I was being complimented, but even if it was just flattery, it didn&apos;t feel bad to hear her say it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shiraishi fell into step beside me and we began to walk slowly toward the school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At our pace, it would only be a few minutes before we reached the entrance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Come to think of it, this is only the second time we&apos;ve been alone like this.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The first time was the day after I transferred to Class C. It&#x2019;s been since that day that I came to school early in order to observe my classmates.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Fufu, that&apos;s right. It hasn&apos;t been that long since then, though.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I suppose you&apos;re right.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I made it a point of coming to school early ever since that day, but Shiraishi was no longer the first to arrive in the classroom. Now, my impression was that she tended to come in later. She&apos;d mentioned she woke up unusually early that one time, so her habits were probably different from the start.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What kind of impression do you have of me, Shiraishi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh my, that&apos;s quite direct of you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Eh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I thought you were a more reserved person. I must say, that was a bold question.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No... Ah, I see, it could be taken that way. My apologies.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It might have sounded like a question related to romance, like whether she liked or disliked me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What I meant to ask was about me transferring into the class. I could tell you were welcoming from early on, but wouldn&apos;t people normally be more suspicious?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why do you think that is?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She turned the question back on me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know... I&apos;d never even spoken to you during our first two years. I assumed that students who only saw me from the outside would have a rather negative impression.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After successfully navigating the first special exam, I managed to gain acceptance from my classmates, but Shiraishi seemed to place her trust in me even before that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no sign that Hashimoto or Morishita gave her a special explanation early on.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re the person who transferred to Class C after Sakayanagi-san left, aren&apos;t you? Moreover, Hashimoto-kun was certain you would be a great asset. While I&apos;m not surprised some people were doubtful, I think there were actually quite a few who, like me, wanted to hold onto hope and believe in you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Rather than give up on Class A, they would bet on that last chance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t difficult to say in words, but convincing one&#x2019;s own heart was a different matter.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the future, for the sake of Yoshida and Shimazaki, I wanted to avoid creating opportunities to be alone with Shiraishi as much as possible. That was precisely why it might be okay to press a little further.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It may be conceited of me to say, but wasn&apos;t Shiraishi watching me, to some extent, before I transferred classes?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was the same kind of observational gaze she&apos;d shown in the karaoke room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed a bit excessive, going too far for someone she practically just met.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was also somehow different from the sense of distance she kept with Yoshida and the other boys.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her lips parted slightly, and an ambiguous sound, like a breathy word or a sigh, escaped them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then Shiraishi, who stopped walking, looked directly at me from a few steps ahead and met my gaze.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ahh... that just won&apos;t do.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;Won&apos;t do&apos;? What&apos;s wrong?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I asked quietly, but Shiraishi only continued to smile without answering.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After a few seconds of silence, in which it felt as if time had stopped&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m Shiraishi Asuka,&quot; she said.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We already introduced ourselves long ago, and even spent time together during lunch breaks and on days off.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And yet, for some reason, Shiraishi now introduced herself all over again and held out her hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And you&#x2014;who are you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered what the intention behind her words was.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or perhaps, was she simply teasing me?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m Ayanokouji Kiyotaka.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Prepared to be laughed at, I accepted her words and her outstretched hand, grasping it in return.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I felt the touch of her slender fingertips, which were a little cold, but certainly held a gentle warmth.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For some reason, you using your full name reminds me of Morishita-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;True...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Shiraishi laughed, not because I was teasing her, but because of the resemblance to Morishita.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Comparing her to Hashimoto and Morishita, or Yoshida and Shimazaki, made it clear just how little my understanding of Shiraishi had progressed.</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/gdFtxkCshMjyAgqlHOGXR.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The Shiraishi that Yoshida saw and the Shiraishi that Shimazaki saw.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">By combining both of their impressions with my own, as her neighbor, I made some slight progress.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I thought I would be able to get a better grasp of her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In reality, however, the mystery only deepened.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her mysterious eyes couldn&apos;t be confined to the simple label of &apos;classmate and neighbor.&apos;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or perhaps this was simply her mysterious personality and atmosphere.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well then, I&apos;ll be going. Please give my regards to Shiina-san.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the end, Shiraishi never answered my question.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was there no reason, or was there one she simply didn&apos;t want to give?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Either way, she didn&apos;t seem harmful for the time being.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">From there, I wanted to delve a little deeper to understand what kind of person she was.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After that, I made my way through the school and arrived at the library.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, that was just how things went; when the timing was off, it was completely off.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hiyori was nowhere to be seen in the spacious library and when I asked the familiar librarian, I was told she left before five o&apos;clock.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Since it was a Friday, it wouldn&apos;t have been unusual for Hiyori to have a plan or two.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And so, day by day, my visit to Hiyori kept being pushed back&#x2014;</p><h1 class="heading_1"><a class="heading-link" href="/read/ss-y3-v2/2">Chapter 5:<br/>Yamamura&apos;s Courage</a></h1><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>AFTER SCHOOL ON Monday</em>, it&apos;d been four days since the exam was announced over the weekend.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t know how many times I hesitated, but the days truly started to feel like they were passing quickly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was still bright outside the window, but the world would soon be dyed the color of sunset.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if it was what one would call a sentimental mood&#x2014;it wasn&apos;t bad.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji Kiyotaka. I must ask you to lend me your face.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The moment Mashima-sensei left the classroom, that listless voice came from the seat behind me, pulling me back to reality. Yes, just as if my hair were being forcefully pulled from behind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Tug, tug...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Tug, tug...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But, it wasn&apos;t &apos;as if,&apos; it was actually being pulled with considerable force.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are your ears working properly?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can hear you, so stop pulling my hair. And your choice of words is questionable.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Reluctantly, I turned around and asked what she wanted.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It appears Yamamura Miki wishes to see you now.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yamamura?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">School had only just ended, but while I was gazing outside, Yamamura vanished.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She must have slipped out into the hallway in an instant, leaving behind neither sound nor presence.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then delivered a message to Morishita.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose she could have just told you herself, but for her to approach the current Ayanokouji Kiyotaka directly would make her stand out in a bad way, even with her presence being as faint as shrink wrap. Yes, I admit my magnificent metaphor may have missed the mark a little. Do you have a problem with that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I didn&apos;t say anything, though...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She played the fool, acted as her own straight man, then quietly got defensive all by herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I intended to go to the library today for sure, but I could probably make time to see Yamamura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">More than anything, if I missed this chance, I didn&apos;t know when she would reach out again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What should I do?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You would do well to follow me. I shall guide you. And you&apos;ll owe me for this, you know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She stood up smoothly and struck a pose with a thumbs-up, like a sidekick.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wouldn&apos;t have minded going by myself if she&apos;d just told me the location, but she unilaterally put me in her debt... Oh well, refusing would be a hassle, so I figured it was fine.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It might have been that Morishita wanted to come along as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I glanced around the classroom to see who was still there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shiraishi and Nishikawa were chatting with a group of a few other girls, while Hashimoto and Kitou were already gone from the classroom.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 1</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">FOR THE MOST part, when I met up with students, it was inside the Keyaki Mall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This was no exception for Yamamura, who did her best to conceal her presence, but the designated location wasn&apos;t a caf&#xe9; or the like, but rather a bit unusual.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;My? It appears she isn&apos;t here.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was brought to a rest area on the second floor with a row of vending machines, but Yamamura was nowhere to be seen; instead, several first-year students were gathered and chatting on a bench.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how faint Yamamura&apos;s presence was, if there were several people gathered nearby, and she were hiding next to a vending machine, she would surely attract an unusual amount of attention if discovered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It would appear she saw the group of first-years and fled.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Seems like it. But if she&apos;s not here, where could she be...? Did you try her phone?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without answering my question, Morishita motioned with her chin for me to follow, crossing her arms and heading toward the side of the vending machines. Then she placed the palm of her right hand on the floor.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had no idea what she was doing, but I watched to see what would happen.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmm... She must have been sitting here until just a moment ago. It&apos;s still faintly warm.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is that so?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if she&apos;d been sitting on the floor, it seemed like it would have returned to its normal temperature in a matter of seconds, or a few dozen at most, after she stood up&#x2026;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you doubt me, you should try touching it. You&apos;ll be able to indirectly feel the warmth of Yamamura Miki&apos;s butt.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a rather unpleasant way to put it, but I was curious if it was actually warm, so I touched it with the palm of my hand. Instead of warmth, a chilly sensation came through.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ugh, you actually touched it. What an outrageous pervert.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But you&apos;re the one who told me to touch it, Morishita...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, if someone told you to grope them, would you just obediently do it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No... I wouldn&apos;t...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s right. You mustn&apos;t be so easily swayed and deceived by the words of others. From now on, let&apos;s learn to judge for ourselves what&apos;s right and what&apos;s wrong. Is that clear?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was admitting that she deceived me herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to point that out, but the bigger problem was the first-years watching me with curiosity from a distance as I was getting scolded by a petite girl.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And they probably also heard words like &apos;butt,&apos; &apos;warmth,&apos; and &apos;pervert.&apos;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a misunderstanding from beginning to end, but I probably wouldn&apos;t be given an opportunity to explain myself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In any case, we&apos;re moving. I&apos;m told Yamamura Miki moved to just outside the south entrance.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With that, she turned her phone screen toward me, showing me a message she received from Yamamura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>[&apos;I&apos;m sorry, but please allow me to change the meeting place to just outside the south entrance. I&apos;m truly sorry.&apos;]</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It certainly seemed she received such a message, but the timestamp indicated it arrived five minutes ago.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I want to ask, just to be sure, but when did you notice this message?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I noticed it as soon as it arrived, but what of it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Five minutes ago would&apos;ve been right around when we were approaching the Keyaki Mall entrance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She indeed made a gesture as if to take out her phone... so she really did receive a message then.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So, the fact that Yamamura isn&apos;t here, and that the floor is already cold...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, of course. I was aware of all of it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a completely pointless trip&#x2014;or rather, a pointless act.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now, let&apos;s hurry to the south entrance. If we keep Yamamura Miki waiting too long, her existence might just vanish from the world, you see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Despite being the one to cause the grand delay, she turned back the way we came without showing the slightest bit of remorse.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 2</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WE BOTH LOOKED around at the same time after exiting the south entrance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh? She isn&apos;t here either.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Looks like it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Since the dorms were a fair distance away and the area wasn&apos;t very crowded to begin with, I thought we would find her right away, but we didn&apos;t. I glanced toward the roadside trees, but there was still no sign of her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;She&apos;s quite a handful, isn&apos;t she? Leading people around like this.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Aren&apos;t you one to talk?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We looked around here and there, but we couldn&apos;t find a trace of Yamamura, let alone Yamamura herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This is like a real-life <em>Where&apos;s Waldo</em>. What a waste of time.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you one to talk&#x2014;never mind.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how many times I interjected, it was probably meaningless by now.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And yet, you&apos;ll still interject next time. That&apos;s what it means to be the straight man in a comedy duo.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t go making us a comedy duo without my permission.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If we headed straight for the south entrance, we would&apos;ve arrived a good ten minutes earlier. Then Yamamura would&apos;ve been standing there quietly, waiting for us&#x2014;or maybe not.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the hushed space, the cell phone Morishita held in her hand vibrated faintly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&apos;If you can find me, then come and try&apos;? How provocative of her!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yamamura sent a message like that? Thinking so, I peeked at her phone screen from the side, but it seemed she only received a notification for new economic news.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please don&apos;t peek at my phone without permission, you rude person.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My impulsive action might have indeed been a bit rude.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose there&apos;s no other choice. Shall we try contacting her?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not that there&apos;s no other choice. Shouldn&apos;t we have done that from the start?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Radio waves are bad for the brain. I try to avoid using my phone as much as possible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had no idea what she was talking about, but for some reason, I felt it was better not to press the issue.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as Morishita put her phone to her ear and started to make a call, I felt a faint breeze from behind me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, um...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whoa, you appeared so suddenly, Yamamura Miki. Please don&apos;t startle me like that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;S-Sorry.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yamamura, who had appeared without a sound, bowed her head slightly in apology for being scolded.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This probably included an apology for changing the meeting place as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Morishita told me you had something to talk to me about. Is that true?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was sad that I had to start by questioning that part, but it was unavoidable, thanks to the wonderful relationship of trust I built up with Morishita.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Y... Yes. Since you transferred classes, Ayanokouji-kun, we still... haven&apos;t said a single word to each other... so...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">About a month already passed, and if you included greetings on the way to and from school as conversation, it wouldn&apos;t be an exaggeration to say she was the only student in the class I hadn&apos;t spoken to.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Right. I was being considerate because you dislike standing out. Were you unhappy about it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;N-Not at all. I mean, I was grateful... for it...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The way Yamamura stammered out her words, she might have seemed even more fidgety than when we first met. To the untrained eye, it might look like she&apos;d regressed, but in reality, that wasn&apos;t the case.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Up until now, Yamamura had quietly erased her own presence and made herself inconspicuous.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I took that image of her desperately trying to speak as a sign of evolution, a phenomenon seen in the process of breaking away from her past self.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, given Yamamura&apos;s life so far, it was a difficult task to ask a person who lived 99% of their life passively to suddenly become proactive.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She tried to somehow advance the conversation on her own but couldn&apos;t make any headway.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita probably didn&apos;t know this flustered side of Yamamura either. A passive person has a passive way of speaking, and until now, she likely engaged in dialogue with that stance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Um... I-I, therefore...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So I would just watch over her for now. If I anticipated her and said, &apos;<em>Is this what you want to do?&apos;</em>&#xa0;or <em>&apos;Is that what you want me to do?&apos;</em>&#xa0;it would only encourage her passivity.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, and... so...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yes... watching over her was important&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And so, what felt like a minute passed with her fidgeting, unable to continue her sentence.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Should I prompt her a little to make it easier for her to talk?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No, for her own growth, I supposed I should wait as long as it took for Yamamura to speak of her own accord.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So... what I want to say is...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The conversation wasn&apos;t progressing at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There were quite a few students who were bad at communicating and I couldn&apos;t deny that I was one of them, but I was struck by how many different types there were.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There were people like me, who couldn&apos;t think of what to say in the first place, and then there were people like Yamamura, who had things they wanted to say but had difficulty voicing them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While I was prepared to wait patiently, Morishita, next to me, apparently lost her patience and went to stand beside Yamamura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For a moment, I thought she was going to subtly give her some support, but&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Fwooh!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, quite forcefully, she puckered her lips and blew into Yamamura&apos;s ear.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wha-a-a-aah!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yamamura, who&apos;d been looking in my direction, but not actually at me, hadn&apos;t noticed Morishita approaching her face. A scream, louder than any she&apos;d ever let out before, echoed all around.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wh-wh-wh-what are you doing...!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hmph. That&apos;s the <em>Ear Gun</em>.
