All those who&apos;re caught unaware are startled, you see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You probably based that on &apos;air gun,&apos; but isn&#x2019;t that... still just an air gun?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now that you mention it, that is true indeed. I shall praise you, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That might have been the moment in my life I was most praised, yet felt the least happy about it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I worried that Yamamura&apos;s tension might have broken, just as she&apos;d been working up her resolve.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita, showing no signs of remorse, wore a smug look on her face and lightly pinched Yamamura&apos;s cheek.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wh-What!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;How about it, Yamamura Miki? Don&apos;t you feel a little less tense now?&quot;</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/UyCcIzEuCmoMIOGduwfNa.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Eh? Ah... I suppose... that might be so.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In all things, tension and relief are essential. That&apos;s the secret bestowed upon the masters of comedy.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;C-Comedy...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, that is of no consequence, so you would do well to tell Ayanokouji Kiyotaka at once.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her expression, which had been rigid, certainly relaxed and the redness in her cheeks subsided a little.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Although it was rough and nonsensical, Morishita&apos;s actions seemed to have had a positive effect on Yamamura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her gaze, which had been fixed around my nose, finally met my eyes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But perhaps that hurdle had been too high, as her gaze immediately darted back down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Today... I called you here because there&apos;s something I wanted to tell you...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was able to speak much more clearly than before and without faltering.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I want to change. I want to have more self-confidence, to be able to smile in front of others. I really, truly think so.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yamamura composed her breathing and did her best to string her words together to convey her feelings.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered how much courage she must have mustered to expose her innermost thoughts&#x2014;the pathetic, embarrassing parts.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She couldn&apos;t maintain eye contact, but it was a commendable effort.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The desire to change sprouted within her, and she approached me with resolve.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please let me... help with getting to Class A, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You have good grades, Yamamura and you&apos;re already helpful to the class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I want to do more... much more. Because I believe... there&apos;s something... that only I can do.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You understand, do you not, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka? It should be your role here to meet Yamamura Miki&apos;s expectations.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose so. Then there&apos;s something I&apos;d like you to do for me right away.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Y-Yes!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">First was a task suitable as a starting point to see just how useful Yamamura could be.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I want you to find out about Shiraishi Asuka.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sh-... Shiraishi-san, you mean...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was probably prepared to conduct any kind of espionage, but she would&apos;ve assumed it would be against the other classes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She surely hadn&apos;t expected to be told to investigate her own class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So the man who came to save the class was just a common lecher... I see. What has the world come to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s quite the imaginative interpretation, as always.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Am I wrong? Shiraishi Asuka is the class&apos;s secret idol. Is it possible that you, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, have also fallen into her trap? Why don&apos;t you let your heart be as honest as your body? Well? May I have your answer?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Saying that, she made a fist and pressed it insistently against my cheek.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Apparently, she was pretending her hand was a microphone, but it hurt nonetheless.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m currently struggling to raise my understanding of the class. In the process, I feel I&apos;m making progress understanding people like Yoshida and Shimazaki, but with Shiraishi, it feels like I&apos;m getting nowhere. I can&apos;t spend forever on my neighbor, which is precisely why I want to ask for your cooperation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You certainly have a convincing way of putting things. However&#x2014;I suppose I can feel a bit of sympathy for the part about you struggling to understand her.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is... that so?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose you wouldn&apos;t understand, Yamamura Miki. You always use your faint presence to investigate students from other classes, or untrustworthy students like Hashimoto Masayoshi; you wouldn&apos;t watch a student like Shiraishi Asuka, who seems harmless on the surface.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes... If you&apos;re okay with it, Ayanokouji-kun, I think I can... try to investigate Shiraishi-san in my own way... I can&apos;t, um, promise at all that I&apos;ll find any useful information, though...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, I&apos;m aware of that. Whether or not she has a side she doesn&apos;t show to me or our classmates&#x2014;anything you learn, no matter how trivial, will be fine. Look into it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Investigating students from other classes came with some trouble, but since this involved a classmate, the possibility of it developing into a major problem was likely low.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Of course, if Shiraishi recognized Yamamura&apos;s presence, it could become a source of conflict, but any dissatisfaction or anger would be directed not at Yamamura, but at the person presumed to be behind her. If she was the kind of student who would instead assume Yamamura was acting alone, then that was the extent of her abilities. Even if parts of her personality remained unclear, it was safe to end the analysis there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I hadn&apos;t had this much contact with Yamamura since transferring. Strictly within our class, our relationship wasn&apos;t apparent to any student besides Morishita.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 3</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">IT WAS RIGHT after I received Yamamura&apos;s agreement and finished telling her about Shiraishi.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A single student popped up at the south entrance, where we hadn&apos;t seen anyone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He then spotted us, shook his head as if to say <em>&apos;good grief&apos;</em>, and approached with a smirk.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kinda cold, isn&apos;t it? Not inviting me to what looks like a pretty important chat.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;How did you know we were here, Hashimoto Masayoshi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita, not even trying to hide her wariness, bluntly asked him for his reasoning.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Process of elimination. I had to run frantically around campus and Keyaki Mall. I was wonderin&apos; where you and Ayanokouji were going and well, I imagined you two were in a relationship you couldn&apos;t speak of.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please limit your jokes to your face.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now that&apos;s just hurtful. I&apos;m actually pretty confident about my looks, for your information.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was almost impressive that he hadn&apos;t followed us immediately after we left the classroom.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He&apos;d properly taken into account the risk of us spotting his tail.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m on your side, you know? You don&apos;t have to be so guarded.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto said that as he closed the distance with Morishita, who frequently and openly avoided him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you so much as lay a finger on me, I&apos;ll let out a maidenly scream that could tear silk, you brute.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had a feeling that &apos;tearing silk&apos; and &apos;maidenly&apos; didn&apos;t really go together.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, since I couldn&apos;t imagine what kind of scream Morishita would let out, my interest was piqued just a little.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, I guess I can kinda see why you wouldn&apos;t want me involved this time, though.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Saying that, he shifted his gaze from Morishita, with whom he was making no progress, to Yamamura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yamamura, now the subject of his gaze, looked uncomfortable, took a step back, and averted her eyes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you making Yamamura your aide, Ayanokouji?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not what this is about. For me, this is still Class C, which I&apos;ve only just entered. There aren&apos;t many people I can rely on. However, Yamamura and I were together for the school trip and the training camp, and I believe we&apos;ve formed something of a bond. And I&apos;ve judged her to be a trustworthy person. It&apos;s not a matter of me <em>adding </em>Yamamura; I&apos;m hoping she&apos;ll <em>join </em>me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This reversal of roles was an extremely important distinction.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, well, looks like you&apos;ve really earned Ayanokouji&apos;s trust, eh, Miki-chan?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Uh... Mi-Miki...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Calling a girl by her first name without permission is rather inappropriate, wouldn&apos;t you say, Hashimoto Masayoshi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey, you&apos;re one to talk, calling people by their full name. And while we&apos;re at it, why don&apos;t you try getting along with me too, Ai-chan?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I absolutely refuse.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Harsh. What&apos;ll it take for you to trust me?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Trust isn&apos;t built in a day. No, in your case, perhaps I should say: it takes years to build trust, but only a moment to lose it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Morishita sharply thrust her index finger right in front of Hashimoto&apos;s face.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even if Ayanokouji won&apos;t back me up, at least Miki-chan might&#x2014;huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the instant his gaze was stolen by Morishita, Yamamura once again silently departed from the scene.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;As expected of a ninja&apos;s descendant. It seems the talent is passed down through the ages.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was pretty sure she didn&apos;t have any ancestors like that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I will be leaving as well. Please don&apos;t follow me again.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t worry, I won&apos;t chase after anyone but Ayanokouji.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;My condolences,&quot; she said to me as a parting shot and without heading back to Keyaki Mall, Morishita seemed to be walking home from outside.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Once it was just the two of us, Hashimoto&apos;s shoulders slumped in an exaggerated fashion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Am I really that untrustworthy?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do you think you are?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I don&apos;t.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then you&apos;ll have to be prepared for people to keep secrets from you or avoid you to some extent.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The truth hurts.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto wasn&apos;t an idiot. He understood and accepted that his past actions made his classmates give him the cold shoulder. And yet, he&apos;d gone out of his way to chase me down. It seemed like an action that could only bring him harm, not benefit.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it strange? That I&apos;d show up even when I&apos;m being told to stay away.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;d be a lie to say I wasn&apos;t wondering about it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hah... Of course I want to get to Class A. I&apos;m the guy who was willing to betray his own class to make that happen. I tried every trick in the book to cozy up to the other classes. But now, I&apos;ve decided this class can win. The fact that I threw away my Private Points is proof of that, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He&apos;d actually lost a large sum of Private Points, making it nearly impossible to buy a ticket to escape should his class sink. For Hashimoto, that was proof of his resolve to go down with his current ship, even if it meant being swallowed by the raging waves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re an amazing guy, you know. I feel like you&apos;ll win to some extent even without my help. I know it&apos;d be a smarter move not to show up like this and make myself disliked. And yet, the reason I can&apos;t help but stick my nose in... is because I want to do everything I can to help get us to Class A.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After he said that with a serious face, he immediately reverted to a joking expression.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Not that I expect you to believe me, though. But I&apos;m gonna keep pestering you anyway.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed he intended to stick to his guns, fully aware of the criticism he would receive for being unable to suppress his true nature.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do as you like. Though I think you&apos;ll be hearing endless complaints from Morishita...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s my one and only headache. She&apos;s so loud. So? You heading somewhere now?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;d like to, but I&apos;m stopping by the school.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The school? The library? Didn&apos;t you get to see Shiina on Friday?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, she&apos;d already left for the day, so I couldn&apos;t meet her. So you could say this is me trying again.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I see?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Although he let me go without any issue on Friday, Hashimoto&apos;s expression turned slightly suspicious, perhaps because he thought I was using the same line again as an excuse to turn him down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Listen, you&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto started to speak, but then he let out a short laugh, exhaled, and shook his head.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s nothing. If you want to see Shiina, I&apos;ll graciously step aside today, too. Well then, I&apos;m off.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He turned his back and walked into Keyaki Mall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was a little concerned about how Hashimoto was acting, but it was getting late again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I decided I should hurry to the library.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 4</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">AFTER PARTING WITH Hashimoto, I returned to the school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The time was just past five o&apos;clock. Most students had already gone home and the majority of those remaining were in the middle of their club activities.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Shouts could be heard from the fields and the sound of squeaking shoes from the gymnasium.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was heading to the library to meet Hiyori, something I&apos;d been putting off for a while.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We exchanged greetings on Friday, but didn&#x2019;t get to talk beyond that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The invitation to transfer to Class B.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I refused it and, for my own purposes, transferred to the unrelated Class C.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t that I regretted it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But the reason I still felt a lingering pull was probably because I, too, had wanted to transfer to Class B, where Hiyori was waiting.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As an ordinary male student of the Advanced Nurturing High School.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A year spent without being tied down by things like being a leader or a strategist.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A future that might have been.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A future I could&apos;ve chosen.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I recalled the end of our second year, when Ishizaki led me to hold hands with Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Am I regretting this?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seeing my own faint reflection in the windowpane, I stopped walking.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My feet, which stayed away from the library under the pretext of personal matters.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was true that many things often overlapped at inconvenient times.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But it wasn&apos;t as if I couldn&apos;t go to the library at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I should&apos;ve been able to find a gap in my schedule and make enough time to at least show my face.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Does that mean I was unconsciously avoiding going there?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Why?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I probably already had the answer to that question.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because I didn&apos;t want to see the sadness on Hiyori&apos;s face, if at all possible.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because, even though I knew it was wrong, I betrayed the smile of the girl who invited me with all her heart.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But that wasn&apos;t rational.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The more time that passed, the more the relationship deteriorated and the sadness grew. If I was the one at fault, I should&apos;ve gone to see her immediately, apologized, and repaired the relationship.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Knowing it was pointless, I posed the question to myself once more.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No answer comes back. Of course not. The answer was already in my head. The act of asking myself questions while pretending not to know... it felt unsettling somehow.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I decided to move to Class C and rejected Hiyori&apos;s invitation. Therefore, I would apologize for it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Why couldn&apos;t I just get on with such a simple process?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I could also tell her via a call or a message, so why hadn&apos;t I even considered that as an option?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the first place, Hiyori wasn&apos;t the only one saddened by my transfer.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My classmates, like Horikita, who were aiming to graduate from Class A, were the prime victims of that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So why am I&#x2014;so concerned only about Hiyori...?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I averted my gaze from the windowpane and started my halted feet moving toward the library once more.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had no basis for it, but I felt like I&apos;d understand if I went and saw her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was the feeling I had.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I steeled my resolve and started moving again, Hasebe appeared from the hallway that led to my destination. There didn&apos;t seem to be anyone else around right now, but I wondered what she was doing all by herself at this hour.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was a little curious, but we weren&apos;t on the kind of terms that we could casually call out to one another. So, I figured we would just pass each other by and that would be the end of it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Indeed, Hasebe seemed to notice me right away, but she averted her gaze, her eyes downcast.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And so, we passed each other without exchanging a word.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hey... hey...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a faint, small voice, so much so that I thought I might have imagined it for a moment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But it definitely came from Hasebe.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I stopped and turned around, Hasebe did the same, though she kept her gaze averted.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I... I wanted to talk for a bit, if that&apos;s... okay?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It would&apos;ve been easy to refuse, but I could see a hint of resolve in Hasebe.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She&apos;d approached me in the same way at the end of the school year, but Amasawa&apos;s interference at the time meant I was ultimately unable to hear what she had to say.</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/fXQWGKCSlvjlEkSueEGIQ.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I brushed her off again here, she might never approach me again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That was before I changed classes. And now it was after I&apos;d changed classes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">What she wanted to talk about might have changed, but it seemed I needed to hear her out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. I couldn&apos;t hear you out when you came to talk to me before.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You remembered.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It was just the other day.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s true... In the meantime, you changed classes, Ayanokouji-kun... but... could that be my fault? Because I came to talk to you&#x2014;and you got sick of it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s completely unrelated, so you can rest easy. I&apos;d been getting offers from students in the current Class C since before the end-of-year special exam. Sakayanagi&apos;s expulsion just made it a reality.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a lie I made up on the spot, but there was no problem with it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It didn&apos;t matter whether there was one reason or a hundred for my transfer, for my betrayal.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh... I see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Whether she believed my words or not, she let out a breath as if relieved, or rather, as if she were a little happy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I would&apos;ve thought resentment would come first.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Resentment...? It&apos;s not like I have the right to feel something like that... It&apos;s like, conversely, with you changing classes, Ayanokouji-kun, I feel like we&apos;ve found a more appropriate distance between us. Of course, I&apos;m going to be in my current class forever, so I think I understand that part.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A distance that was uncomfortable as an ally. A distance that was comfortable as an enemy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So that was how Hasebe was taking it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So, if it&apos;s not about resentment, what&apos;d you want to talk to me about?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, yeah, well, actually&#x2014;it&apos;s...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While answering my question, Hasebe hurriedly searched for something on the phone she was holding.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I just really wanted you, Ayanokouji-kun, to see how hard she&apos;s been working&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">From Hasebe&apos;s flustered phone-fiddling and fragmented words, I figured out what she wanted to say.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The one who left the school. The student named Sakura Airi and what happened to her afterward. Hasebe must have some information about that. I&apos;d briefly heard that she&apos;d passed an audition, but that was just a piece of information I&apos;d acquired. Until a short while ago, I wouldn&apos;t have had any interest in it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, through my contact with Tsubaki, I had developed a slight curiosity about what happened to those who dropped out.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Still, that was only in comparison to before.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It certainly wasn&apos;t something worth stopping and turning back for.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hasebe&apos;s anxious eyes, staring at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">All I would gain by refusing was the small amount of time that wouldn&apos;t be wasted afterward.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it... not okay?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I didn&apos;t answer, Hasebe whispered, clutching her phone anxiously.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not true. I&apos;d like to hear it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Eh&#x2014;r-really?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. I didn&apos;t have the courage to look into it myself, but to be honest, I&apos;ve been wondering about her all this time.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The students of Horikita&apos;s class were becoming more and more wary of me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Although there were still some cracks, it would probably become difficult to extract information from them from now on.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In that case, I just needed to try a new angle of attack.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Precisely because I put myself in a difficult position by abandoning everything and betraying my class, I couldn&apos;t pass up an opportunity to test how to win them over, or how to create a breakthrough.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hasebe, her eyes sparkling, held out her phone to me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;She&apos;s started to appear on TV bit by bit, though it&apos;s in a late-night slot.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;On TV? That&apos;s amazing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Sakura was expelled at the beginning of the second term last year. The fact that she made it onto TV in less than a year since then was genuinely surprising.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I imagined her simply starting a new school life as a student at some other school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed she had actually wanted to invite Miyake and Yukimura to talk about Sakura at the end of the school year, and I confirmed that what she wanted to tell them was exactly the same.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Our standing conversation went on longer than expected and the scenery outside began to take on a reddish hue.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh...! Sorry, Ayanokouji-kun. I got carried away talking... Didn&apos;t you have somewhere to be?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s fine. I enjoyed talking with you, Hasebe, it&apos;s been a while. And I was happy to learn more about Sakura.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;R-Really? To be honest, I could talk about Airi forever... but I&apos;m glad.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you don&apos;t mind, let&apos;s make some time to talk again somewhere. Or, if you&apos;re worried about drawing resentment from Class A, we could talk on the phone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course! I&apos;ll make sure you become Airi&apos;s number two fan, Kiyopon.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed she didn&apos;t realize it herself, but she unconsciously called me by my old nickname.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After that, I parted ways with Hasebe and arrived at the library before six o&apos;clock, but Hiyori was already gone; the same librarian from the other day told me that she left about ten minutes earlier.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There were two main routes from the library to the entrance, and it seemed I picked the wrong one, a fifty-percent chance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The librarian asked if I wanted them to pass on a message, but I declined the offer.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll try again another day.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no need to rush; the time to know the answer would surely come soon enough.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 5</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>AT 7:40 P.M. on Monday</em>, Kaneda, having finished dinner in his dorm room, arrived in front of the karaoke parlor where they were to meet at 8:00, looking restless.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He occasionally looked around, let out small sighs and paced about the area.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That strange behavior came to an abrupt halt with the appearance of a certain person.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;T-Thank you for coming today, Shiina-shi.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With a nervous expression, Kaneda bowed his head, quickly adjusted his crooked glasses and offered a stiff smile. Then, he let out a long breath as he looked at Shiina in her casual clothes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The pleasure is all mine, Kaneda-kun. This feels somewhat refreshing, doesn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, it really is. I wonder what Ryuuen-shi&#xa0;is thinking.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Together, the two of them looked up at the karaoke parlor, which was brilliantly beckoning customers.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But Kaneda, out of the corner of his eye, burned the sight of Shiina into his memory.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He arrived much earlier than the meeting time because he anticipated that Shiina, who now stood beside him, might also arrive early. And that hunch was correct.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That meant they would have nearly twenty minutes alone together at most.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We still have a little time, what should we do? Personally, I think it would be best if we went inside to wait&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as he was about to suggest that, a loud voice echoed through the mall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What the heck?! I thought I&apos;d be the first one here, but you two already beat me to it!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With an exaggerated reaction to something not particularly surprising, Ishizaki arrived.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Hello, Ishizaki-shi.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Whether he was put off by his attitude or for some other reason, Kaneda greeted him with a somewhat displeased look.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re here early. I had the impression you were the type to be late.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You look pretty bummed, Kaneda. Don&apos;t tell me you&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if he&apos;d an inkling about Kaneda&apos;s suspicious behavior, Ishizaki grinned slyly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-What is it...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s my job to greet Ryuuen-san, got it? I&apos;m not gonna let you look cool!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... So that&apos;s why you arrived early, Ishizaki-shi.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The three of us got here at pretty much the same time. There&apos;s no first or last place here, so remember that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Rest assured, I don&apos;t place any importance on such things.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Man, I wonder what Ryuuen-san&apos;s thinking. He said he&apos;s not just callin&apos; you, Kaneda, but that Tokitou guy too. They ain&apos;t got the rank to be called to a meeting, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know about Tokitou-shi, but I have regular meetings with Ryuuen-shi, so I would appreciate it if you didn&apos;t lump us together.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda pointed it out sharply, pushing up the bridge of his glasses with his middle finger.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In reality, Kaneda did have regular conversations with Ryuuen while the exams were underway, but a large portion of those cases involved them speaking one-on-one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The strategy for the special exam&apos;s already been decided, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. We haven&apos;t received any additional orders as of now. Or, it&apos;s also conceivable that this is a meeting to report on some other possibility he&apos;s discovered...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Or maybe he needs our power. Like, we all get together and come up with some amazing ideas.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I understand what you&apos;re trying to say, but that doesn&apos;t mean just any talent will do... Don&apos;t you agree, Shiina-shi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was just a proverb, after all. Kaneda added that simply having more people wasn&apos;t enough and was about to ask for Shiina&apos;s opinion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At that moment, however, Ryuuen appeared and the atmosphere instantly grew heavy. Ishizaki also put on a tense expression, bowed his head deeply in greeting and immediately began to move inside the karaoke parlor.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The four of them were led to a room in the back that&apos;d been reserved in advance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen slid a menu across the table and had the other three order food and drinks.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems Katsuragi-shi and Tokitou-shi haven&apos;t arrived yet. Is that all right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;They let me know they&apos;d be late. More importantly, Kaneda, have you heard anything from that Hashimoto guy?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without even looking at him, Ryuuen asked the question, and Kaneda slowly shook his head from side to side.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, I have not. We were in frequent contact until just before we became third-years, but since Ayanokouji-shi&apos;s class transfer was finalized, he&apos;s been ignoring my messages.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Upon hearing that report, Ryuuen closed his eyes and the left corner of his mouth curled up slightly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So he&apos;s made up his mind, huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems he holds Ayanokouji-shi&apos;s abilities in very high regard. Of course, I&apos;ve also been observing the recent developments objectively and have had to revise my own evaluation of him two or three times&#x2014;but even so, I thought Hashimoto-shi would leave open the possibility of transferring to another class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, Sakayanagi probably would&apos;ve found Hashimoto&apos;s two-timing diplomacy amusing, but with Ayanokouji, you never know how things will turn out. If he makes a clumsy move to secure his own escape route, he&apos;ll be cut off without mercy. Even he doesn&apos;t have the guts to cross that bridge.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Either risk it all and keep his ties to other classes, or commit fully and go down with Ayanokouji. Ryuuen answered that it was the decision he made after weighing those options.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...You truly do hold him in high esteem.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda acknowledged his ability, but he still didn&apos;t rate him as highly as Ryuuen did.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because he&apos;d only ever seemed like an ordinary student, Kaneda perceived him as having some kind of weakness and failed to sense the sharpness&#x2014;the kind that made one feel they would get hurt just by touching him, like with Sakayanagi or Ryuuen.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen turned his gaze to Shiina, who hadn&apos;t said a word since entering the room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s your read on Ayanokouji right now?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Me?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;His actions since transferring classes. Including what the hell he&apos;s aiming for.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A little surprised, Shiina recalled how he&apos;d been when she ran into him in the elevator the other day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder... I can&apos;t really give a good answer.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s impossible, Ryuuen-san. It&apos;s &apos;cause Shiina sees Ayanokouji through a filter.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A filter? What do you mean by that, Ishizaki-shi?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda retorted to the strange expression.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s a special filter, man. A special one. The kind ya call the Ayanokouji Filter.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At Ishizaki&apos;s answer, Kaneda&apos;s facial muscles tensed slightly and his expression grew grim.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even Shiina, who was dense about such things, understood what Ishizaki was trying to say and looked down awkwardly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen, on the other hand, didn&apos;t change the way he looked at Shiina in the slightest.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t give a damn what kind of feelings you have for Ayanokouji. But as long as you&apos;re a member of my class, you need to work to make us win. Got it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, I understand.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Anyway, you managed to talk to Ayanokouji after he transferred, right? Tell us about that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No... Actually, I haven&apos;t been able to speak with him properly yet.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? How come? I definitely told him to go see you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As Ishizaki tilted his head in confusion, Shiina&apos;s spirits sank at the mention of Ayanokouji.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll get in touch with Ayanokouji right now and&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s enough out of you. Just sing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if to say there was no need for any more pointless talk, Ryuuen gripped the microphone and tossed it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-Whoa, r-roger that!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ishizaki, who caught the mic to keep it from falling, seemed to think he&apos;d been appointed to liven things up, grinning determinedly as he psyched himself up. Then, after he selected a song, the introduction began to play.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as he began to sing, the door to the room opened, and Katsuragi and Tokitou appeared in the karaoke room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sorry for the wait.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Immediately after Katsuragi spoke, Tokitou cut in and confronted Ryuuen.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you think you&apos;re doing, calling even me out here?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I figured I&apos;d listen to a useless guy&apos;s opinion for once. It&apos;s just a whim.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While Ishizaki belted out his song, sparks already flew between Ryuuen and Tokitou.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now, calm down. First, why don&apos;t you tell us why you&apos;ve gathered us here today? I was under the impression our policy for the current special exam had already been decided.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Tearing down Ayanokouji and the next special exam. Figured I&apos;d touch on that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Hoh. That sounds like a rather interesting topic.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Got any new information on Ayanokouji?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, I don&apos;t. I haven&apos;t really been able to speak with him lately. There&apos;s always someone from Class C by his side, starting with Hashimoto. I&apos;ve been avoiding any careless attempts at contact.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Beside Katsuragi as he answered, Tokitou also averted his gaze, responding with silence.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Um, Ryuuen-shi. If you would permit me, would you allow me to look into Ayanokouji-shi? Since I haven&apos;t tried to get close to him before, I might be able to learn something new.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do as you like.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen replied without expectation, and then, the moment Ishizaki finished his song, the conversation shifted to the next special exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In my opinion, the results of the current special exam aren&apos;t that big of a deal. However, judging by the lackluster rewards, it wouldn&apos;t be surprising if the next special exam causes a major shift in Class Points. We absolutely cannot afford to fail that one.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if agreeing with Kaneda&apos;s assessment, Katsuragi nodded firmly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;d say so. Even though we&apos;re Class B and Horikita&apos;s is Class A, that doesn&apos;t mean we&apos;re in a safe zone. Our last loss narrowed the gap and now we&apos;re under heavy pressure from the classes below. If we suffer two or three consecutive losses, we&apos;ll inevitably be neck-and-neck with them. And if that happens, many of our classmates will see it not as being on equal footing, but as falling into a disadvantage.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The outcome of that special exam is our class&apos;s final line of defense, isn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was still only May. Assuming the next major special exam would be held in either June or July, the worst-case scenario might await them before they even reached the halfway point of the year.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If there is any silver lining, I suppose it&apos;s that we can assume the probability of an academics-centered special exam is not very high. While studying is an inseparable part of being a student, if we&apos;re constantly forced to compete in that area, our class has no chance of victory from the outset. I&apos;m sure the school wouldn&apos;t be so consistently unfair.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At the time of class assignments upon enrollment, many honor students were placed in Class A.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While one could accept that as a large head start, if that alone were to decide everything, there would&apos;ve been no need to make them compete from the very beginning, as it would&apos;ve been entirely superfluous.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If the rewards are worth it, we should use anything at our disposal, including the Private Points we saved last time. I don&apos;t want to recommend it, but that includes acts that border on breaking the rules.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So you&apos;re finally starting to get it, Katsuragi. But I have no intention of fighting within the rules. Fouls, whatever, I&apos;ll use any means necessary to win.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I understand. But I will stop you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Katsuragi didn&apos;t change his stance of strictly adhering to the rules.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They continued their fruitless discussion, and a silence fell between them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a disconnected moment, especially as other students were getting rowdy in the next room.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ryuuen-kun... you&apos;re not trying to force me to talk about Ayanokouji-kun, it seems...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s just that I don&apos;t think I&apos;d get any useful information out of you anyway.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Is that really the only reason?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Huh? What do you want me to say? Do you want me to order you to &apos;find his weakness by any means necessary&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;That&apos;s...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen already had the answer to that question.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">What Shiina felt for Ayanokouji went beyond mere friendship.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll make damn sure you&apos;re useful. Not for Ayanokouji, but for this class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even if it meant exploiting those feelings, Ryuuen envisioned a future where he defeated Ayanokouji.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. That resolve&#x2014;I intend to form it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ever since the transfer, she hadn&apos;t been able to have a proper conversation with Ayanokouji.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because of that, Shiina was trying to come to a certain resolution.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To Ayanokouji, she was just a friend, or perhaps even less than a friend.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If that were the case, even her faint feelings would become heavy shackles.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda quietly continued to watch Shiina, whose sorrowful profile looked as if she might vanish.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was the first such experience for a man who&apos;d lived his life devoid of any fighting spirit.</p><h1 class="heading_1"><a class="heading-link" href="/read/ss-y3-v2/2">Chapter 6:<br/>Fortune and Misfortune Are Intertwined</a></h1><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">THE DAY STEADILY drew to a close and before I knew it, school was over. It was almost the end of the fifth day since the exam was announced, but not a single major change had occurred. As far as my brief investigation showed, every class maintained a perfect record of no tardiness or absences and the student body was leading a quiet, more serious school life than usual. I also heard no rumors of any trouble over the weekend, a time when things tended to get lax. It seemed the students hadn&apos;t forgotten to maintain a sense of tension.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As was evident from their diligence in their studies and sports, their avoidance of tardiness and absences, and their strict adherence to order and politeness, all four classes were acting with the consideration that their daily conduct would be key to the exam. It seemed Ryuuen&apos;s class hadn&apos;t been able to grasp that on the first day, but Kondou and the others&apos; actions, while probing, had clearly taken daily conduct into account from the very beginning. I didn&apos;t know which student in Ryuuen&apos;s class proposed the idea, but in that class, the leader&apos;s decision was everything.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The fact that he adopted it meant that, at the very least, Ryuuen himself came to the same conclusion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>Thwip, thwip-thwip!</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I was about to get up from my seat, a sharp pain shot through the back of my neck again and again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ow, ow, that hurts. What the...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Turning around in response to the unfamiliar sensation, I saw Morishita holding what looked like... a gun, made from disposable chopsticks.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To think you&apos;re still alive. You&apos;re quite something.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Never mind &apos;quite something&apos;... What is that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It is a gun. A gun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>Thwip!</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While I was turned around, she fired a rubber band into the palm of my hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The gun was formed from multiple layers of rubber bands wrapped around disposable chopsticks and it seemed to be designed so that pulling the part that resembled a trigger would launch a rubber band.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That hurts, you know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s because I&apos;m shooting you with the intent to cause pain.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why&apos;d I have to be the one in pain...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Still, it was well made... It wasn&apos;t just haphazardly assembled into the shape of a gun; it was designed to shoot rubber bands.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;When&apos;d you make something like that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s a secret weapon I diligently crafted during break time. It&apos;s a wonderfully lethal weapon, capable of slipping past even the metal detector gates.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I decided to consider it a small mercy that she hadn&apos;t been working on it during class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Let&apos;s take a drive to the ends of hell together. <em>Bang.</em>&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I absolutely don&apos;t want to do that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As we were having this pointless exchange, Satonaka&#xa0;approached us hesitantly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do you have a moment?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. What&apos;s up?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While surprised to be addressed by someone so unexpected, part of me thought it would be funny if Morishita shot Satonaka with a rubber band.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I glanced over at Morishita, at some point she&apos;d put the gun on her desk and was staring out the window, looking bored. Apparently, I was the only one she wanted to take a drive with to the ends of hell.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I was wondering if you&apos;d like to hang out with our group this Sunday.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As he said that, Satonaka looked back and a group of a few boys and girls waved in our direction.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There were three of them in total: Yanagibashi and Tsukasaki, with whom I&apos;d had almost no interaction, and a girl named Hoashi.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I won&apos;t force you, but what do you think?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t have any particular plans, so if you guys don&apos;t mind, I&apos;d like to join.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I answered, Satonaka smiled happily. I wondered if the reason I felt inexplicably happy just seeing that was because of his handsome features, which even a guy could admire.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you&apos;d like, how about you, Morishita-san?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll pass.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without so much as a glance at Satonaka, she gave her blunt refusal while continuing to stare out the window.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After that, Satonaka said he&apos;d message me the details later and went back to his group.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why&apos;d you refuse?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For no reason other than I&apos;m simply not interested.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At some point, she shifted her gaze from the window back to me and was now unabashedly gripping the gun in her right hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Don&apos;t tell me, Morishita, you have feelings for Satonaka&#x2014;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>Thwip!</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A rubber band shot mercilessly into my left cheek.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Too bad for you, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka. Did you think you would get to see me blush and become flustered?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The thought crossed my mind, but it seems I was wrong... Besides, that hurt, and it&apos;s dangerous.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m simply not interested in him. Besides, faces that are too androgynous&#xa0;are not to my liking. A face as hideously distorted as yours, Ayanokouji Kiyotaka, is better&#x2014;at least it warrants pity. Are you happy to know that I&apos;m fond of you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s no doubt that sadness far outweighs happiness. Sorry, but I&apos;m heading home.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I hurried out into the hallway, escaping from Morishita, who kept firing rubber bands at me and asking if I was running away.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I took out my phone and checked the message from Ichinose one more time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>[&apos;It looks like Hoshinomiya-sensei wants to see you, Ayanokouji-kun...&apos;]</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After receiving that message, I headed for the special building she&apos;d designated.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At the time, just after school let out, that special building was always quiet.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Two years ago, there&apos;d been an incident in that very place where Sudou fell into Ryuuen&apos;s trap in a security camera blind spot, but since then, more cameras were installed there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With the exception of spaces where privacy was a consideration, such as the changing rooms and restrooms, it could be said that most of the school was now covered by them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While a small number of students felt suffocated by the extensive surveillance, the majority likely welcomed it as a means of protecting themselves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I arrived at the promised location, but Hoshinomiya-sensei was still nowhere in sight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So, I decided to kill some time by gazing out the window.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Considering I had to go see Hiyori, I didn&apos;t want to linger for too long today. The figures of students heading home or off to their club activities began to trickle into view.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;Club activities, huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the end, I never joined one, but my mindset changed enough that I thought if I could start over as a first-year, it might be good to try joining one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Enough that if someone were to ask if I was satisfied with my student life, I could give a hesitant nod.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I averted my gaze from the window and looked down the hall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Not long after, I heard the sound of high-pitched footsteps echoing all around.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As she came into view, our eyes met, and she approached with a light wave of her hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sorry for the wait, Ayanokouji-kun. Did you need something from me, calling me out to a place like this?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re the one who called me here, aren&apos;t you, Sensei?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whaaat are you talking about~? A teacher would never call a student out to a place like this, you know. I only came because Ichinose-san told me <em>you </em>wanted to see <em>me</em>, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was in the exact same situation, but I figured it wasn&apos;t even worth asking her about it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s eyes were clearly smiling.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I was trying to figure out her true intentions, she closed the distance even further, pressing herself against my body.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What do you think you&apos;re doing?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What am I doing? Whatever do you mean~?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She played dumb, refusing to answer my question and instead brought herself closer as if to whisper.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, without so much as asking for my permission, she entwined her slender arm with mine.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I need you to do your best this year, Ayanokouji-kun, so it&apos;s fine if I give you a little bit of service, right? Just like this.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">To call such a blatant act &apos;service&apos; was just like her, but for a teacher, it could only be called unbecoming conduct.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That said, I had no intention of lecturing her about it here, nor did I intend to forcibly shake off her slender arm. Either way, the important thing was not to take any action myself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You leave no openings, do you, Hoshinomiya-sensei.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I stopped her objective, not with action, but with conversation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Eh? What&apos;s that supposed to mean, &apos;leave no openings&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I already had my predictions about the upcoming special exam, but your advances just now have confirmed them. What the school will judge over the course of one week is our &apos;attitude in our daily school life.&apos; The number of tardies and absences, our general conduct. The factors that usually only cause minor monthly fluctuations will be the main evaluation criteria. The school hallways are lined with countless security cameras, so they can easily assess the students by checking the recordings later. If they see me in close contact with a teacher of the opposite sex, it will inevitably affect my assessment.&quot;</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/KGZwaOHTXhjgYMWagqXwo.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A little while ago, Hoshinomiya-sensei had been on the verge of running wild, determined to win even if it meant using her authority as a teacher to ensure her class&apos;s victory. To prevent that, I told her the details of my alliance with Ichinose and the arrangements that came with it, so she was now somewhat under control. However, what she truly desired was to return to the upper classes as soon as possible and to see Chabashira-sensei&apos;s class fall.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ayanokouji-kun, you&apos;re being a little too suspicious. We&apos;re in this together, aren&apos;t we? So there&apos;s no way I&apos;d do anything to drag you down, right? Besides, we don&apos;t even know if you&apos;re right about the exam.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, I don&apos;t have any concrete proof. It&apos;s just a feeling I have. But please, rest assured. Even if you were to intentionally act in a way that lowers my evaluation, Hoshinomiya-sensei, it won&apos;t affect our alliance.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I continued to speak in a calm, business-like tone, Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s smile deepened, perhaps because she understood.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, that&apos;s a relief. Well then, does this mean you&apos;ll let my class have the win this time? You&apos;ll let the class with fewer Class Points have the glory, won&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That would be the case if I could completely control the outcome. However, this is a competition between all four classes. If our class receives a negative assessment, we risk placing last. And there&apos;s no guarantee that Ichinose&apos;s class will be able to take first place.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But come on, that&apos;s where you get to show off your skills, isn&apos;t it, Ayanokouji-kun? You just have to arrange things so my class takes first and your class takes second.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Unfortunately, I have no intention of intervening to make adjustments.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...But why? Are you just going to ignore the risk of Class A and Class B winning?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Frankly, for this particular exam, I don&apos;t believe that will be an issue.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even if we get them, it&apos;s a mere fifty Class Points. Are you saying they&apos;re not even worth bothering with? That&apos;s&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s incorrect. It&apos;s because I&apos;ve judged that this isn&apos;t an exam that warrants demonstrating the true value of our alliance. To begin with, isn&apos;t it also necessary to believe in your own class?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For the past two years, the school saw Ichinose&apos;s class as being just as disciplined as Sakayanagi&apos;s, if not more so. However, unable to endure their current circumstances of having fallen to Class D, Hoshinomiya-sensei was always trying to forcibly change the situation by using every trick in the book.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Of course, it would&apos;ve been understandable if she were acting out of sheer desperation, but in a case like this, her actions could&apos;ve ended up nullifying her class&apos;s greatest strength.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Remember to watch over them. That&apos;s something even Chabashira-sensei is capable of.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hk...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I finally brought up the name of her contemporary rival, Chabashira-sensei.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The moment I did, Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s demeanor changed in an instant.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hoshinomiya-sensei stopped, and her arm slipped smoothly away from me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can trust you, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The meaning behind those words was simple. It included the threat that if betrayed, she would go on a rampage at any time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please consider our alliance from a long-term perspective, not a short-term one. That&apos;s all I can say for now.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see. Well, you certainly did come through for me during the last special exam, so I suppose I&apos;ll just wait and see for now.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a good thing that Hoshinomiya-sensei was wary and hadn&apos;t let her guard down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But in truth, it was also a fact that we didn&apos;t need to force a win in this exam. If our daily conduct was indeed being assessed, intervening carelessly to control it would be a troublesome and high-risk endeavor. For my class and Ichinose&apos;s, which had very few behavioral issues to begin with, employing unnecessary measures would only increase the danger. The best strategy was to let them act as they usually did.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 1</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">ONLY ABOUT THIRTY minutes had passed since school let out. I arrived at the library at a relatively early hour.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After many twists and turns, the time had finally come.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The start of a new special exam during Golden Week, repeated after-school invitations, and chance encounters. A considerable amount of time had passed since Ishizaki told me to meet with Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I stepped into the library, its distinctive scent gently drifted into my nostrils.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wondered if Hiyori was there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The library at ANHS was so vast that finding a person could be just as difficult as finding a book.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I happened to glance at the librarian at the entrance, she gave me a warm smile and then pointed a finger without a word.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That direction was the area where the mystery novels were shelved.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Apparently, the person I was looking for was there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I slowly approached the spot, which was still some distance away, Hiyori appeared from behind a bookshelf.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Before long, in the sparsely populated library, our eyes met.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But in the next instant, she averted her gaze and disappeared deeper into the shelves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I thought our eyes met, but could it be that she hadn&apos;t noticed me?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Calling out from a distance would be a breach of etiquette, so I headed for the bookshelves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was no longer where I&apos;d last seen her, so I began checking the aisles between the shelves.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One, two, three...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Where was she? I couldn&apos;t imagine she had gone that far&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I was thinking that, I finally found Hiyori at the end of the aisle, on the opposite side of the bookshelf where I stood.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And our eyes definitely met.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Or so I thought, but she immediately broke her gaze and vanished into another aisle.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She definitely noticed me. If so, does that mean she&apos;s avoiding me?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was it because I was late in coming to see her? Because I didn&apos;t transfer to Ryuuen&apos;s class?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I speculated on the reason, I closed the distance to talk to her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If she continued to run away from me inside the library, then I would have no choice but to give up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I couldn&apos;t bring myself to forcibly corner someone who was clearly reluctant.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hoping that wouldn&apos;t be the case, I walked toward the spot where I&apos;d last seen Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Where did she go from there...?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I was thinking that, Hiyori, perhaps wanting to see where I&apos;d gone, peeked out from behind a nearby bookshelf.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The distance was less than two meters. Close enough that if I stepped forward and reached out my arm, I could easily touch her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hiyo&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I tried to call her name, but she hid her face again.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But she didn&apos;t run away again; it seemed she&apos;d only hidden her face.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As proof, her hand rested on the bookshelf and a part of her uniform was visible.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Was I bothering you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I spoke in a quiet tone, her face slowly emerged after a brief silence.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Did you come here to look for a book to read...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At least she replied. For now, I was relieved about that.</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/LlMgAFLkskDfsqQkPbLJr.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, I came to see you today, Hiyori.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The meaning of my words must have gotten through, but Hiyori still wouldn&apos;t come out from behind the bookshelf, keeping half her body hidden. I was reminded of the words of Ishizaki from the other day.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>&apos;Make sure you see Shiina soon. She was pretty down, even if not as much as I was.&apos;</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then there was the somewhat awkward atmosphere from when we were in the elevator together a few days ago.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Why hadn&apos;t I come to see her sooner?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I now regretted that I&apos;d been running away from the guilt I felt over transferring classes.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you still bothered by the fact that I rejected your class&apos;s invitation and transferred to Class C?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I decided that we would not be able to have a satisfactory conversation without first eliminating any unnecessary distance between us, so I posed the question. Hiyori, who immediately showed her agitation, averted her eyes, which had been trying to meet mine and pressed her lips tightly together.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suppose I can&apos;t say... that I haven&apos;t been concerned. I kept imagining that maybe my invitation was the reason you were keeping your distance, Ayanokouji-kun...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s quite unlike you. You of all people should know that&apos;s not possible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If anything, the Hiyori in my mind was always unhurried and did things at her own pace.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I saw her as a person who could look at matters calmly, with a discerning eye, without ever growing flustered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I assumed she understood, without me needing to say a word, that my transfer from Class A to Class C had a purpose that could only be attained by joining that class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no way she&apos;d distance herself just because her invitation was rejected.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, it was an undeniable fact that I&apos;d delayed coming to see her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I apologize again for not being able to accept the transfer to Class B. And for taking so long to properly talk about it. That caused the misunderstanding.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In the silence of the library, Hiyori took in my words, then shook her head.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No... You&apos;ve done nothing wrong, Ayanokouji-kun. The class transfer... how should I put it... was just me getting my hopes up on my own... And even though I thought there might be a reason on my part for why you weren&apos;t visiting, I couldn&apos;t bring myself to come see you.
