Please allow me to apologize for that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After she said that, the rest of her body, which had been hidden, slowly came into view.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then she bowed her head, as deeply as she possibly could.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And yet, Hiyori didn&apos;t have a single thing to apologize for.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That being said, continuing this apology contest wouldn&apos;t lead to anything productive.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I thought I was prepared...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Prepared?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We&apos;re in different classes now, Ayanokouji-kun. I thought that our relationship, where we could chat so familiarly, had to come to an end... But talking with you like this... I suppose...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So she&apos;d been trying to sever our friendly relationship and resolve herself to see me as an enemy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Realizing that, I felt a sense of relief that I made it in time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you don&apos;t mind, Hiyori, why don&apos;t we talk about this past month we&apos;ve been apart? We have plenty of time today.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it... really okay?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course. I came here today because I wanted to see you and talk with you, Hiyori.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I told her my honest feelings, a smile appeared on Hiyori&apos;s face for the first time that day.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 2</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WE SAT FACING each other in the empty seats of the library.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then, as if to fill the one-month blank, we began to string together words little by little. At first, Hiyori, who had a somewhat distant atmosphere, gradually returned to her usual smooth and gentle tone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So, even after becoming a third-year student, the bookworm remains unchanged.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. In fact, I just finished reading another one last night.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She looked once at the bookshelf lined with mystery novels and replied that she&apos;d just returned it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, actually, I&apos;ve made one more friend who&apos;s a fellow reader.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hiyori clasped her own hands together and her eyes narrowed with joy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ever since I became a third-year, Kaneda-kun has been visiting me often at this time.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kaneda? Well, I can picture him reading a book, so I&apos;m not particularly surprised...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Still, until the end of the second year, I&apos;d never once seen Kaneda in the library.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If he was considering going to university, the library was also an excellent environment for studying.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, speak of the devil.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hiyori, whose back was to the library entrance, noticed Kaneda&apos;s arrival before I did and waved.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I also turned around and raised my hand in response to Kaneda, who was looking this way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda showed a slightly surprised face but immediately started walking and came to our table.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Hello, Shiina-shi. I&apos;ve come again today.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My guess was correct; along with the library books, he also had academic books in his hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kaneda-kun, you really do love books, don&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was clear from Hiyori&apos;s words that he was a frequent visitor. When I gestured with my eyes for him to sit in the seat next to me, he gave a slight nod and sat down.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes, well. I&apos;ve heard that you, Ayanokouji-shi, also used to frequent the library. It seems you haven&apos;t shown your face recently, though.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For the past month. It was precisely when Kaneda started coming to the library that I&apos;d been unable to come.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It seems like we&apos;ve been swapping places. I think we&apos;ll have more chances to meet from now on.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...I see. As a fellow reader, that&apos;s nothing but delightful.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He showed a welcoming stance, but his expression showed no joy. It was no surprise, since my presence had no direct bearing on him. The only one who would be truly happy about gaining another reading companion would probably be Hiyori right in front of me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Be that as it may, you seem to have been busy. The matter of your transfer from Class A to Class C surprised me a little&#x2014;no, quite a bit&#x2014;hmm?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A sudden shadow fell over us, its source being the librarian.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m sorry to interrupt your conversation. Shiina-san, this is sudden, but could you lend me a hand? I don&apos;t think it will take long&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If I&apos;m the one you need, of course I&apos;ll help. You two, if you don&apos;t mind, please wait just a little. I&apos;ll recommend some books to you later.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Leaving us with those words, Hiyori left her seat with the librarian.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Now, just the two of us, Kaneda and I, who were not particularly close, were left behind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was often the case that the atmosphere became a little awkward when a mutual acquaintance left. I learned such things through my school life.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I suppose I would try to take the lead in the conversation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What kind of books do you usually read, Kaneda?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m not very good at such meaningless small talk.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A clean rejection. It&apos;s like I threw him the conversational ball, and he just chucked it out into left field. I thought I started a natural topic since he liked books, but it seemed to be a question Kaneda disliked. Or perhaps my throw was clumsy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;My bad. I apologize if my wording offended you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was it an unbefitting overestimation to think that I could hold my own in a situation like this?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...No, my apologies as well. There&apos;s no need to be so down about it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I was just trying to connect the conversation smoothly. Really.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I got that sense, somehow.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you don&apos;t want to talk to me, you don&apos;t have to force yourself.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...This is rather unsettling. It&apos;s not that I dislike it, but I don&apos;t often have the opportunity to talk with you face-to-face like this, Ayanokouji-shi, so I couldn&apos;t help but think about various things. Until Shiina-shi comes back, shall we talk for a bit, if you&apos;d like?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was a little happy to see Kaneda showing a positive attitude toward conversing with me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If there&apos;s anything on your mind, you can ask me. Of course, I can&apos;t give unlimited answers regarding class problems.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I thought Kaneda would understand that, but I told him just in case.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Kaneda took off his glasses, breathed on the lenses, and wiped both of them with a cleaning cloth he took from his pocket. Then he slowly put his glasses back on.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;With Shiina-shi&#x2014;you seem to be on good terms.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hm? Oh, it&apos;s because she&apos;s a fellow bookworm, like you, Kaneda. I have her keep me company.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A fellow bookworm, you say? That may certainly be true, but the expression I saw on Shiina-shi&apos;s face from a distance just now was one I haven&apos;t been able to see this past month. When I saw you two from the entrance, I was frankly surprised. It was the first time in a long while I&apos;d seen her genuine smile.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That might have been my fault. I just apologized to Hiyori and she forgave me. So from now on, I imagine you&apos;ll be able to see her smiling in class and in the library, just as she did before.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I kept to myself the part about her inviting me to transfer classes. It wouldn&apos;t have been pleasant to tell Kaneda, who was not only from a rival class, but also close to Ryuuen and I had no idea how that information would be interpreted.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Do you realize just how amazing that is?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Amazing?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You don&apos;t understand? No... are you pretending that you don&apos;t? Ayanokouji-shi, have you ever felt like you&apos;re blessed?&quot; he asked me as if to test me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Blessed? That&apos;s far too broad a definition for me to answer.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;My apologies. It seems my words were insufficient. Even from my perspective as a man, your features are quite excellent, Ayanokouji-shi. You&apos;re also taller than average and well-built, yet you don&apos;t give off a needlessly intimidating air. More than anything, you have an impeccably clean-cut appearance. And on top of that, it seems you were dating Karuizawa-shi until recently and lately, I&apos;ve even been hearing rumors about you and Ichinose-shi. The more other men learn about you, the more they will look at you with admiration and, at the same time, feel a fierce jealousy at the immense gap between you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My interest was piqued by Kaneda&apos;s unexpected words and I listened intently.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not just your appearance. In terms of academics, you&apos;re increasingly being called the number one student in our year by those with high academic ability. I was once proud to think that my own academic ability was not inferior to yours, Ayanokouji-shi, but I&apos;m now ashamed of that and couldn&apos;t help but laugh at myself. Furthermore, concerning your physical abilities, it seems you&apos;ve been achieving excellent results in PE since April. As for me, someone who is poor at sports, I could stand on my head and still not come close to your potential&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So this was Kaneda Satoru, a student who could speak so eloquently and passionately.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He praised me while simultaneously disparaging himself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When looking at student values across the entire year, Kaneda was in the top thirty percent. I saw no reason for him to needlessly belittle himself, but...</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While forcefully heaping praise upon me, Kaneda continued.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And now, you&apos;ve unleashed the true abilities you&apos;d hidden for two years and have even taken up the position of leader to rebuild Class C now that Sakayanagi-shi left. Even that somewhat dangerous mind of yours must surely captivate many. You&apos;re the very picture of a hero, are you not? I envy you from the bottom of my heart.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Envy me? I&apos;m a traitor who abandoned Class A and objectively speaking, not using the power I possessed and hiding my true abilities would more likely be judged as villainous.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That just makes you an anti-hero, which is still a hero.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">With a hint of a dry, exasperated laugh, Kaneda glanced behind him.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">My eyes followed his and I saw Hiyori, who seemed to be working on a computer with the librarian. It looked like it would be a while longer before she returned.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">One thing I could tell for certain from Kaneda&apos;s attitude and words, was that he held feelings for me that were stronger than I&apos;d imagined. I was aware that transferring classes would lead to various opinions being directed at me, but his ranked high among them. At first, I thought his comments were out of consideration for his class leader, Ryuuen, but it didn&apos;t seem to be only that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And one thing was for certain: Kaneda didn&apos;t like me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah... my apologies. It seems I&apos;ve gotten carried away and talked too much.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Kaneda realized how his words must have sounded to me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s nothing to apologize for. People are free to think what they want of others.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even that part of you is so cool it&apos;s infuriating, Ayanokouji-shi. Even if you&apos;re prepared to be hated, I&apos;m sure it doesn&apos;t feel good to actually be hated by someone. Or is it simply because it&apos;s me, you don&apos;t care what I think of you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not it. I would say the same thing in the same way to anyone.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;d proceeded this far on the assumption that I would be hated.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;Then, what if that person were Shiina-shi...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, Kaneda brought up Hiyori&apos;s name.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was, indeed, one of the most appropriate students to use as an example in that situation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I intended to reply immediately just as before, but the words caught in the back of my throat.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t mind if Hiyori hated me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Yes, in essence, that was true.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I knew that was a possibility after I rejected Hiyori&apos;s invitation and transferred to Class C.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The reason I hadn&apos;t gone to apologize right away was because I needed to prioritize other matters.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">What she thought was a secondary, or even tertiary, concern that I placed low on my list of priorities.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Please, forget I asked. That was an indelicate question.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Without waiting for my reply, Kaneda quietly pulled back his chair and stood up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I think I will be leaving for today.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Are you sure? Hiyori looked like she wanted you to wait.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s quite all right. It makes no difference whether someone like me is here or not right now. I apologize for the one-sided conversation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, don&apos;t worry about it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is that so? Then I am grateful for your magnanimity.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Leaving behind an air of resolution, Kaneda departed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A short while later, Hiyori returned, looking around curiously.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh? Are you all alone, Ayanokouji-kun?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Apparently, he hadn&apos;t called out to Hiyori, who was near the entrance, before he left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. He left, apparently on urgent business. He asked me to tell you he&apos;ll be back again soon.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is that so? That&apos;s something to look forward to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Seeing her gentle smile, it seemed she was genuinely looking forward to his next visit.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I arbitrarily assumed that Hiyori spent the past month all alone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But in reality, she&apos;d been gradually expanding her circle of friends and found time to spend with Kaneda in the library.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I thought about it, there was nothing strange about it at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as I&apos;d changed classes and friends, the number of people I interacted with slightly increased, Hiyori, too, changed since we first met.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She&apos;d met someone new somewhere and was growing closer to that person.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A mere classmate. A mere underclassman, an upperclassman.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">From such relationships, they become friends and then best friends.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">People much closer to her than I was would appear one after another in Hiyori&apos;s future.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps someone else might be sitting in the empty seat next to her tomorrow&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Looking at her as she lowered her gaze to her book, I was surprised by my own arbitrary thoughts.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Who was getting close to whom was nothing more than a piece of information.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Something to be judged solely on whether or not it could be used in the future.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And yet, I was indulging in useless and arbitrary speculation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was trying to see an invisible someone next to Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t know why I&apos;d become unable to see her in the same way I saw other students.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Same sex, opposite sex. Friend, best friend.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I interacted with many students without being bound by things like gender or how close we were.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While there were varying degrees of compatibility, like whether our hobbies matched or if we seemed likely to get along, I basically never accepted or avoided someone for those reasons alone.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, with this much noise interfering with my thoughts, I had no choice but to admit it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That Hiyori was perhaps a little different from the frameworks I&apos;d used until then.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was different from willfully moving a category from right to left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Something that&apos;d been on the right side until yesterday moved to the left before I knew it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just reading books and sharing the same space together filled me with a sense of fulfillment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If I were to describe it in a word, it was &apos;happiness&apos;.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That expression was probably the closest fit.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t as if the time we&apos;d spent together was particularly long, nor had we exchanged many conversations in a short period.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But thinking back, I naturally felt a sense of familiarity with her from early on.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her name too&#x2014;it wasn&apos;t like I started calling her Hiyori because she asked me to.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In other words, it wasn&apos;t a name I used with a purpose.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Before I knew it, I&apos;d been staring intently at Hiyori&apos;s face as her gaze fell upon her book.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hiyori didn&apos;t notice my gaze for a while, but in a brief moment, she looked my way and our eyes met as if it were the most natural thing in the world.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is something the matter?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Looking at Hiyori&apos;s face calmed my heart.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no doubt that saying something like that would only make her uncomfortable.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Around just after 6:00 p.m., Hiyori and I left the library and then the school.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it okay if I stop by the library again tomorrow?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course it is. Besides, you don&apos;t need my permission, you know?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps finding my attempt to ask for confirmation amusing, she placed a hand to her mouth and chuckled softly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Maybe it&apos;s because I kind of see the library as your home.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s... not entirely wrong, I suppose. I&apos;m here every day unless I have other plans.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I felt like I would mistakenly assume she caught a cold if she wasn&apos;t there. After thinking that, I recalled a time when Hiyori had stopped appearing in the library for a while. It was around when the news that Karuizawa and I were dating spread around school. While it was true we were merely reading companions, Hiyori had been considerate by doing things like changing the times she visited.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">At the end of Hiyori&apos;s gaze, I found a small pull cart, wheels rattling, slowly making its way toward the main gate.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Many colorful flowers were lined up on its bed, beautiful even from a distance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As the two of us stood there watching it pass by, the middle-aged woman pulling the cart noticed us and stopped right in front of us.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I got special permission to sell here today. Would you like to take a look?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it alright?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After being spoken to so kindly, Hiyori happily narrowed her eyes and approached the cart&apos;s bed. I also stood beside Hiyori and looked down at the flowers.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Keyaki Mall didn&apos;t have a flower shop. If you wanted to give flowers for a birthday or the like, you had to get artificial flowers from the general store or order them online. So, it was refreshing to see them being sold like this. From cut flowers like baby&apos;s breath to potted hydrangeas. A small number of other assorted plants were also there.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;They&apos;re beautiful...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hiyori said to the flowers beside me, looking hesitant, as if deciding which one to buy. After waiting a little while, she reached out her hand as though she&apos;d found the one she wanted.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;May I have this one, please?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">What Hiyori pointed to as she spoke was a single, carefully wrapped corn poppy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Just one?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yes. I thought it was lovely, precisely because it&apos;s a single flower.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not about the number of flowers, young lady.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The clerk said so with a bright smile and explained that it was called a &apos;single wrap.&apos;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then I&apos;ll take one of these.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I took out my phone and asked to pay for it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, um, no, Ayanokouji-kun. I&apos;ll buy it myself&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s fine. I want to give this to you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I told her that, and just as Hiyori looked up at me, I continued.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s an apology for making you wait a month. Though this might not be much of one.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just a single corn poppy. Price-wise, it didn&apos;t even cost 400 points; it was a very cheap purchase.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not true at all. I&apos;m very happy.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After she bowed her head, it seemed to me that her face was slightly flushed when she looked up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The sky had been dyed by the sunset in an instant; perhaps that was the reason.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Then, I gratefully accept.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A voice of gratitude so quiet, one might have missed it if not close by.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After I paid, the clerk handed it to me instead of Hiyori, looking back and forth between us, and then said, &quot;Thank you very much.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">We just stood there and quietly watched the pull cart until it was out of sight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After we were alone again, I handed the single corn poppy I was holding to Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Thank you so much.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No need to thank me. It&apos;s just for my own satisfaction, a selfish apology.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That&apos;s how I answered, but the truth was a little different. I simply wanted to give Hiyori a present.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I genuinely want her to be happy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The corn poppy&#x2014;a gift given from such an impulsive action.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Clutching that single small flower to her chest, Hiyori stared intently at me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She was certainly smiling with joy the moment our eyes met.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But as we continued to gaze at each other, her expression gradually began to change.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She showed an unexpected expression.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why are you crying?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps Hiyori, whose eyes were faintly glistening with tears, hadn&apos;t noticed it herself.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She hurriedly wiped at the corners of her eyes with her slender, white fingertips.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For me, this was supposed to be just another ordinary day until I met you, Ayanokouji-kun. But it&apos;s now become such a happy and fulfilling time... it feels like a dream, I can&apos;t believe it...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her voice, which sounded as if it were squeezed from her very heart, continued.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m so happy... I&apos;m so very happy. That we didn&apos;t end up drifting apart, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Strangely enough, I was in the exact same state of mind as Hiyori.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s much the same for me. This was supposed to end as just another ordinary day. But it&apos;s turned into a very fulfilling one&#x2014;it&apos;s true.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If Hiyori&apos;s words weren&apos;t false, then it meant our feelings were mutual.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For some reason, this supposedly meaningless synchronization felt ticklish and it made me happy.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Beneath the sunset-dyed sky.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I deeply burned the image of her into my mind&#x2014;clutching the corn poppy, her eyes glistening.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">So that, from now on, I could remember it at any time&#x2014;</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 3</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">WHEN I RETURNED to the dorm lobby, Hashimoto, who&apos;d been sitting on a sofa, saw me and stood up.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sorry, Shiina. I&apos;m gonna borrow Ayanokouji.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Though he offered a light apology, Hashimoto gave her no room to refuse, but Hiyori showed no sign of displeasure and bowed cheerfully. Then she waved and got on the elevator first.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto watched her go with a broad grin, waving back.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We meet often. Were you waiting for me to come back?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah, something like that. Let&apos;s take a walk.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He half-forcibly led me out of the lobby and we walked down a path off the school route.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re really tolerant of Shiina, or rather, you&apos;re pretty close with her, aren&apos;t you? You&apos;re the one who gave her the flower, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Why do you think that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no one else around at the time, so there was no way Hashimoto could&apos;ve seen me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not a funny way to play dumb. There&apos;s no way it&apos;s nothing. Anyone could tell just by seeing how happy she looked holding that flower.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed Hashimoto felt that way after seeing Hiyori and I together.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re always going on about &apos;Shiina, Shiina,&apos; so there&apos;s been something on my mind. I figured I had to confirm it today, no matter what.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After a short pause, Hashimoto looked me straight in the eye.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;d probably be hard for anyone else to ask, so as the designated bad guy, I&apos;ll ask you straight up. Is Shiina someone especially close to you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">His expression clearly conveyed his seriousness.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is it a problem to have a specific person I&apos;m close to?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, I wouldn&apos;t go so far as to say it&apos;s a problem. But I&apos;m not exactly welcoming it, either.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto said, somewhat evasively. When I looked at him, he seemed a bit uncomfortable and averted his gaze.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not just me. Ichinose probably isn&apos;t too happy about it either, is she? I&apos;ve been hearing rumors that Ichinose likes you. The source of the rumor is Class D. In other words, she found out you broke up with Karuizawa and openly declared herself the next girlfriend candidate, right? I thought the way Ichinose looked at you wasn&apos;t exactly normal ever since we heard about the alliance together, but...anyway, I was sure you were going to get together with her&#x2014;or are you dating Ichinose?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;We&apos;re not dating.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This should&apos;ve been the answer Hashimoto wanted, but instead of being pleased to hear it, he scowled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...And why&apos;s that? If it&apos;s Ichinose, you couldn&apos;t ask for a better person to date, right? Honestly, even I, who&apos;ve barely had any contact with her, would say yes in a heartbeat if she confessed to me. She&apos;s on that level, you know? There&apos;s no reason to turn her down, is there?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Sorry to burst your bubble while you&apos;re getting all worked up, but I haven&apos;t been confessed to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even so, all you have to do is make a move, right? She already likes you, so you should just go for it without hesitation. Ah, I don&apos;t mean that in a weird way.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He hurriedly denied it, but that was a trivial distinction.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ichinose and I have an alliance. Nothing more, nothing less.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So you&apos;re saying you&apos;re just pragmatic allies? For the foreseeable future, too?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After asking that, he continued without waiting for my reply.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So then... does that mean you&apos;re going after Shiina right now?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m surprised. I didn&apos;t think you cared so much about other people&apos;s love lives.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;To be precise, I&apos;m just worried that those feelings might affect the outcome of the class competition.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was my business who I dated, but he was worried I might let my personal feelings get in the way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That seemed to be what he feared.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was a very Hashimoto-like way of thinking.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Nah, I don&apos;t even need to hear your answer; I already know. You cut ties with Horikita and the others you shared two years of hardship with to join us. I don&apos;t doubt for a second that your actions would change just because you got involved with one or two girls like Ichinose or Shiina.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And yet, you seem awfully hung up on it. Especially when it comes to Hiyori.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto was clearly placing more weight on it versus the situation with Ichinose.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;From an outsider&apos;s perspective, it&apos;s always felt to me like you treat Shiina differently.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto concluded by saying that if it was just a needless worry, then that was great, but it was already certain that I was, in fact, treating Hiyori in an indescribable way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">That said, I wouldn&apos;t let her existence affect the class&apos;s performance. But speaking purely from personal feelings, I didn&apos;t want to treat Hiyori carelessly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I see.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So that&apos;s what it is.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What&apos;s &apos;what it is&apos;?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Perhaps I&apos;m in love with Hiyori.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Huh?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can&apos;t say for sure what this feeling is just yet. But I might at least be harboring, or starting to harbor, feelings for Hiyori that I don&apos;t have for anyone else.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The unease and errors I&apos;d felt up to that point and their cause.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The reason it hadn&apos;t been easy to find was because it was something outside my empirical rules.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;W-Whoa, whoa, are you serious? Nah, what&apos;re you talking about, like this is your first time feeling something? You were just dating Karuizawa a little while ago, weren&apos;t you?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If the emotion I just identified is truly budding within me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I&apos;m even amazed by the strength of this thing called emotion and its unpredictable timing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A desire to know more began to overflow.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>If I traced it back, would I be able to see it?</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>When did I start to become conscious of Hiyori?</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>What was different about her compared to other people?</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>I want to try digging as deep as I can.</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>&#x2014;No, for now, I should probably stop these unnecessary delusions.</em></p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Coming up with an answer right now, as if deducing a strategy, would&apos;ve been far too hasty.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how it turned out, that emotion was extremely fascinating as a subject for experimentation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Then... depending on the situation, are you planning on dating Shiina?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Didn&apos;t you believe that my actions wouldn&apos;t change? So why are you sticking your nose so deep into someone else&apos;s love life?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, just this once, y&apos;know... It&apos;s my view on love, I guess, but it&apos;s screaming at me that I absolutely have to check.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Your view on love, huh? Do you have a corresponding amount of experience with it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I asked this while keeping the expelled Maezono in mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Now you&apos;re underestimatin&apos; me a bit, Ayanokouji. It&apos;s not like I can brag about it, but between middle and high school, I&apos;ve dated two... no, to be precise, three people.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I surmised that the reason he was cagey about the last one was because he was referring to Maezono.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So? Are you planning to date Shiina? If you can&apos;t answer, that&apos;s fine too&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whether we date or not involves the other person. It isn&apos;t something I can answer.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hah, well, yeah. But from the sound of it... if this is really your first time feeling this way, that&apos;d make it your first love, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Perhaps.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Right then, I possessed no way to deny it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No matter how many people you date, you only get one first love. And what&apos;s more, you almost never end up with that person. My own first love was a classmate in elementary school, but I couldn&apos;t even talk to her properly. If you treat your first love like that, it might feel romantic, or special, but in reality, it ain&apos;t all that. Guys are especially simple, y&apos;know, we&apos;ve got a tendency to unconditionally fall for any cute girl.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I fully understood that regardless of whether the object of affection was a man or a woman, the majority of people tended to be attracted to those with superior looks. Even looking at TV or magazines, the proportion of handsome men and beautiful women who caught the eye and gathered attention was overwhelmingly high.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So just go ahead and ditch that first love stuff. If a reasonably cute girl falls for you, your tenth love or your twentieth love can start just like that.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Despite his roundabout expressions, Hashimoto&apos;s way of thinking remained consistent.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Whether it&apos;s really your first love or whatever, stay away from Shiina. This is my strongest advice to you as your strategist, as your friend, and as a comrade aiming for Class A.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto was strongly wary of my relationship with Hiyori developing in an unexpected way.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not a difficult concept, is it? You can get back together with Karuizawa, you can date Ichinose. Heck, any other girl would be fine. Just, not Shiina.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was clear that by deliberately repeating himself, Hashimoto was watching my reaction. It was plain to see that he would take either excessive agreement or denial as an unfavorable sign.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I understand your concern very well. However, you&apos;re counting your chickens before they hatch.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;I can trust you, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You probably wouldn&apos;t believe me even if I answered, but you can trust me.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For me, Hiyori&apos;s existence was becoming indispensable.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">An existence that made me learn an unfamiliar emotion.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I couldn&apos;t help but want to find out whether or not I could be certain it was what Hashimoto called a first love.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to experience being swallowed by the irresistible waves of emotion and see how I would be carried away.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I wanted to take both myself and the other person apart and seek the answer to the very end.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was true that I would remember his warning as a warning, but I had no intention of stopping.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Because I was already getting ready to dive in as deep as I could.</p><h1 class="heading_1"><a class="heading-link" href="/read/ss-y3-v2/2">Chapter 7:<br/>The Observer</a></h1><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left"><em>A WEEK HAD finally passed since the exam announcement</em>&#xa0;and the Thursday of the reveal arrived.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The only slightly worrisome elements were the encounter with Ike and his friends at the cafe and Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s approach; I received no reports of trouble from any other classmates.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The matter with Hoshinomiya-sensei was unfortunate, as it only served as a disadvantage to the alliance, but it was also a reflection of just how hungry she was for victory.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The exam was truly a neck-and-neck race. In truth, it was completely unclear if any gap had formed at all. The students of Ryuuen&apos;s class also had an exemplary week, remaining calm and taking no action to entrap students from other classes. It seemed that the outcome would be decided by a truly trivial difference.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I waited in the classroom that morning for my classmates to arrive, a bewildered-looking Hashimoto came hurrying in.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;This is a real pain, Ayanokouji. At the eleventh hour, it&apos;s confirmed that Rokkaku&#xa0;and Yano&#xa0;will be absent.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ryuuen?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I suspected that right off the bat, too, but apparently there wasn&apos;t any real contact. They&apos;re saying they just got sick and have a fever.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">According to Hashimoto&apos;s explanation, the two of them had an idea what caused the fever; apparently, when Rokkaku and the others were shopping a few days ago, a sick-looking employee at the mall sneezed on them.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">From what I heard, it seemed like an unavoidable, unforeseen event.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Rokkaku and the others also understood the cause of their poor health and moreover, they hadn&apos;t done anything reckless like forcing themselves to come to school, so there was nothing to blame them for.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;d love it if today&apos;s tardiness and absences don&apos;t affect the exam... but things are never that simple, are they?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Probably.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Presumably, the scope of the exam extended right up to that morning&apos;s attendance, covering the entire week.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If that was the case, the absences might have dealt a fatal blow in such a neck-and-neck competition.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;At this point, all we can do is hope your 90% prediction is wrong, Ayanokouji, and that this exam has nothing to do with it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Hashimoto pinned his hopes on the few remaining unknown rules and details of the exam.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t think it&apos;s a concern with this class, but make sure to tell everyone not to slander the two who are absent under any circumstances. Even if we lose because of it, it won&apos;t affect the general state of the class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I figured you&apos;d say that, so I&apos;ve already sent out the notice.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Phone in hand, Hashimoto showed me the screen, indicating he just sent the message.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After that, the classmates who arrived in the classroom seemed to have learned of the situation; though they appeared slightly disheartened and shaken, they quickly composed themselves and went to their seats with expressions of acceptance.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">They understood very well that panicking wouldn&apos;t get the class points back.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Furthermore, not abandoning the hope Hashimoto had for an unrelated exam, I could see several students tackling their studies.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 1</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">ICHINOSE INVITED ME to have a little discussion about the exam results after school, so for the first time in a week, I headed to the front of the gym on the second floor of Keyaki Mall. There, I spotted Watanabe from behind, standing near the entrance and looking up at the gym. He then paced back and forth, crossing the gym&apos;s entrance over and over again. But he never went inside; I could see him repeat this behavior.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What are you doing?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Wh-whoa, Ayanokouji!? H-hey, long time no see. Wh-what a coincidence... haha.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Showing blatant agitation with slightly broken speech, Watanabe raised his hand and answered.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I was just thinkin&apos; about maybe joinin&apos; the gym, y&apos;know? Like, I&apos;ve got this newfound desire to work out and get lean? And I was wonderin&apos; if going to the gym with Amikura would mean we could spend more time together. Well, a mix of things like that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Neither was a bad thing, but it was clear that the latter objective was the bigger one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If that&apos;s your plan, you should just go ahead and join. It&apos;d be nice for me, too, since having you join would mean another person to talk to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;R-really? In that case, can I just say you were the one who invited me!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed he didn&apos;t so much want to take the final step himself as he wanted someone to give him a push.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I have no problem with that at all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">When I readily agreed, Watanabe seemed genuinely happy, his eyes sparkling with delight.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m meeting Ichinose after this, but do you want to join right now, maybe do a trial run? I&apos;m guessing Amikura will probably be there, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;A-a trial by fire right off the bat, huh...!?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For a moment, it crossed my mind that he might flinch, but Watanabe nodded as if he&apos;d made up his mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, as he faced the gym, his brave advance came to a halt.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Isn&apos;t this a little too obvious?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, I can&apos;t deny that. Girls especially seem to be perceptive when it comes to romance. If you, who&apos;s had nothing to do with the gym until now, suddenly joined the group, it&apos;s conceivable that Ichinose, or in some cases, Amikura herself, might get suspicious.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I explained this to Watanabe, mixing in my own analysis, which was gradually improving.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s no good! S-seriously, I can&apos;t have Amikura finding out at this stage!&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After letting his imagination run a little, he seemed to have concluded that he couldn&apos;t hide how suspicious he looked.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Letting her know you like her is also a valid strategy, you know.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;M-maybe, but I&apos;m not a veteran like you, Ayanokouji... I&apos;m like a hero in an RPG who hasn&apos;t even left the first village yet, you know? It&apos;s impossible at level one...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t quite understand his analogy, but it seemed he still wanted to avoid letting Amikura know his feelings.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Should you hold off on joining then?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ugh... If I can, I wanna have as many chances to talk to Amikura as possible... but still...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In that case, it&apos;s a bit roundabout, but there is a way to camouflage your objective.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Camouflage?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s an exaggeration to say &apos;hide a tree in a forest,&apos; but you could just get two or three other people besides yourself to join the gym at the same time. If you&apos;re just one of several people who were invited, it&apos;ll be harder for her to figure it out.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ooh, that&apos;s a great idea. Is there anyone from your class you could ask, Ayanokouji?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, it&apos;s better to avoid Class C. To join Ichinose and Amikura&apos;s circle naturally, it&apos;s best to select people from the same class.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Besides, involving Class C would make problems more likely to arise when the framework of the alliance was removed.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... It&apos;s true that it&apos;s more natural to get close to a classmate... but is there anyone who&apos;d join if I asked...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">This was the first time I&apos;d interacted with Watanabe since my class transfer, but he didn&apos;t mention my move from Class A to C at all. It seemed less like he was deliberately avoiding a sore subject and more like he wasn&apos;t that concerned about it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t know much about Class D, but what about Shibata? As a member of the soccer club, he probably doesn&apos;t mind physical activity and at the gym, he could further build up his body&#x2014;&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s absolutely impossible.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Watanabe&apos;s eyes flew open as he grabbed both my shoulders, cutting me off as if to say, &quot;Don&apos;t say another word.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s impossible?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s just cruel, Ayanokouji... The fact that you&apos;re sayin&apos; it without even knowing why just makes it more infuriatingly impossible... If Shibata saw you and Ichinose together, he&apos;d cry...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Hm...?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After saying it was impossible, he muttered something in a very quiet voice, but I couldn&apos;t quite catch it.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Look, he could become a rival for Amikura&apos;s affection, right? Shibata&apos;s surprisingly popular with the girls.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see. I hadn&apos;t considered that perspective.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I had next to no information on who in Class D was interested in whom.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But I can&apos;t think of anyone else who&apos;d do it. It costs money to join, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was true that joining out of inertia or just to be sociable, without any real interest, carried its own risks.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In that case, I have a clever idea. Provided he won&apos;t be a potential rival for you, of course.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Who is it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Kanzaki and Himeno. Then Hamaguchi. Should be okay with these three, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What a mismatched trio, huh? Himeno&apos;s a girl, and it&apos;s not like Kanzaki and Hamaguchi are the type to be rivals in love. But I can&apos;t picture them going to the gym at all.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not as surprising as you might think. Especially if Kanzaki&apos;s motivated, there&apos;s a chance the negotiation could work out.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Is that... so?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Watanabe tilted his head, not quite understanding.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If it&apos;s alright with you, Watanabe, I was thinking of talking to them. Is that okay?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Well, sure.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I intended to use Kanzaki and the others to bring about a change in Ichinose, but the fact that she evolved beyond his expectations would surely be a joyous thing for Kanzaki as well, though he probably strongly doubted how genuine that evolution was.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The fact that he hadn&apos;t approached me despite hearing about the alliance served as proof of that.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Up until now, we only exchanged occasional pleasantries, but from here on, it would be desirable to get closer and spend as much time as possible talking over the next few months.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">No matter how much Ichinose cast aside her hesitation, she couldn&apos;t win on her own.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was essential to raise the level of students like Kanzaki, Himeno, and of course, Watanabe and Amikura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Using the gym as a meeting place with Class D was an on-the-spot idea, but if it worked out well, it might not be a bad thing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;For now, hold off on joining today and give me a little time. I&apos;ll somehow get Kanzaki and the others to join the gym and try to create an opportunity for you to naturally get closer to Amikura.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;O-oh. Thanks, Ayanokouji. You&apos;re a good guy, you know that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">He tried to suppress his happiness, then went down the stairs from the second floor, where the gym was, to the first floor.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The fact that his steps seemed to have a little skip in them was probably proof of his anticipation.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was still a little while before Ichinose would arrive, but I decided to change and start my workout.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I was showing my membership card at the reception to check in, a student came in behind me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;...Hey...&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was Utomiya from Class 2-C. I&apos;d never seen him at the gym before, but was he perhaps a member? As if he thought he was being scrutinized, he spoke uncomfortably.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I was just a little curious, so I came to have a look. Are you... a member here, senpai?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was he hoping to join? Perhaps a new breeze was blowing into the sparsely populated gym.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Yeah. A friend invited me a little while ago and I started coming.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see... I get it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was painfully obvious from his attitude that he had no interest in the fact that I came here or what impression I had of the gym.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;They offer a free trial, so if you&apos;re interested, you should give it a try.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I told him anyway, even though I thought it might be meddling.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;ll think about it.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And, just as I&apos;d imagined, the reply came back with a cold expression, one that looked like he had no intention of thinking about it at all.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Perhaps he was displeased at being seen considering joining the gym, because he turned right back around and left.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That was Utomiya-kun, right? Are you two on bad terms? It looked pretty tense.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Akiyama-san, who happened to be passing by, asked me with a wry smile.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I don&apos;t mean for it to be, but I can&apos;t say we&apos;re on friendly terms, to be polite.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In most cases, when I talked with Utomiya, Tsubaki was often with him and we rarely ever spoke one-on-one.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But I&apos;m surprised you knew Utomiya&apos;s name.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;He&apos;s been coming here for the last two days straight, and I&apos;ve seen him at the reception desk or the entrance like just now, so I&apos;ve had a few chances to talk to him.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;So he&apos;s really considering joining?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I wonder. That might be part of it, but I think he&apos;s probably more concerned about his kouhai-chan.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;His kouhai?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;There&apos;s a first-year girl who was brought here by Utomiya-kun and joined recently. She just joined two days ago, but they seemed pretty close. Maybe he was worried about her?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Rather than having met after he became a second-year, perhaps they were acquaintances from before who ended up enrolling at ANHS?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Looks like she hasn&apos;t come yet today, though~&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, if he was acquainted with this kouhai, there was no need to come all the way to the gym to see her.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If he had some business, he could just contact her on his phone. Considering that, it could be that they had only just met and hadn&apos;t yet exchanged contact information directly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;By the way, that girl is an amazing athlete. The first day, a crowd gathered when she was on the treadmill, so I took a look too, and she was maintaining a 20-kilometer pace for several minutes. And she was perfectly fine training after that. Mashima-sensei got really into it, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Well, normally, you wouldn&apos;t come to a gym and run at 20 kilometers per hour.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">If your timing was off by even a single step, there was a risk of falling, and since it didn&apos;t stop immediately even when the stop button was pressed, the emergency stop couldn&apos;t keep up. It seemed to have limited applications, such as for those with experience in track and field, soccer, or martial arts to use for a moment of intense sprint training.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">For a first-year high school student, fifteen or sixteen years old&#x2014;and a girl at that&#x2014;to be able to handle it meant she was remarkably talented.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In fact, there&apos;d been children in the White Room who dropped out, unable to achieve that level.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In any case, it probably meant that an interesting talent joined the gym.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">But what was Utomiya&apos;s reason for taking an interest in that girl?</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I made some unkind speculations about it&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Perhaps a storm of love is brewing.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Akiyama-san said so and laughed, her eyes narrowing happily.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I see. It might have been another one of those things, like Watanabe&apos;s feelings or the boys&apos; maneuvering for Shiraishi.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Romance, something I would never have known about if I hadn&apos;t started dating Karuizawa.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And then, the image of Hiyori surfaced in my mind.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Through experience, I was becoming more sensitive to the romantic developments concerning myself and those around me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Ah, right... Um, there&apos;s just something I wanted to ask, if that&apos;s all right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What is it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Um, about Mashima-sensei... How should I put it... Is my impression of him the right one? It&apos;s not like he&apos;s actually a player, is it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;While I cannot definitively deny the possibility that he possesses such a side, I do believe he&apos;s a reliable teacher. I don&apos;t think he&apos;s so different from the impression you have of him.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I-Is that so? Thank you.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It wasn&apos;t as if there&apos;d been any major developments between the two. Rather, Akiyama-san&apos;s demeanor was such that it made me think there might be some progress between them in the future.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Inside the gym, there was no sign of Koenji or the notable first-year and the gym was deserted.</p><h2 class="heading_2">Part 2</h2><hr/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">AFTER WORKING UP a good sweat, I left the gym with Ichinose and Amikura.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Alright, I&apos;m gonna make a little detour on my way home,&quot; Amikura announced as soon as we stepped outside, then promptly put some distance between us, waving as she headed down the escalator.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;She was in quite a hurry. Is she meeting up with someone?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Mm, I think she was just being considerate. So that you and I could talk alone, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Was it out of concern for the leaders of allied classes to deepen their friendship? Or some other kind of consideration? It wasn&apos;t particularly difficult to imagine the real reason.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;If you&apos;d like, how about we walk home together? Ah... saying it like that makes it hard for you to say no, doesn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;No, it&apos;s fine. I was planning on heading straight home today anyway. Besides, we haven&apos;t had a chance to talk about the exam.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Really? That&apos;s great, I&apos;m glad.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I left Keyaki Mall with Ichinose, who looked up at me and smiled, her eyes narrowing slightly.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The time was past 6:30 p.m., but it wasn&apos;t dark outside.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was proof that winter&apos;s shadow had completely retreated and the daylight had begun to linger, one step at a time.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The outcome this time was unfortunate, wasn&apos;t it?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Even if you&apos;re thorough about managing your health, some things are just out of your control. It can&apos;t be helped.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Just as every class imagined, the recent exam took place over the course of one week.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The evaluation was based on how well we adhered to proper daily conduct and school rules, and although we weren&apos;t told the detailed point distribution, we were informed that all four classes were more or less neck and neck.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">The results were: Ichinose&apos;s class in first place with 50 Class Points. Horikita&apos;s and Ryuuen&apos;s classes were in second place, each receiving 10 Class Points. My class, with two absentees, came in last with a deduction of 25 points. It was a relief that Hoshinomiya-sensei&apos;s outburst didn&apos;t have a negative impact.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Horikita Class 1240 points</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ryuuen Class 1081 points</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ayanokouji Class 867 points</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ichinose Class 864 points</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">There was no change in the class rankings, and the gap from Class A to Class D remained mostly unchanged.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;It&apos;s not a problem in the grand scheme of things. It&apos;s a gap we can close if we aim to win the next special exam.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I see. That&apos;s very reassuring. I&apos;ll do my best to help out as much as I can, too.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Of course, I&apos;m counting on you. More importantly, I have something else I&apos;d like to discuss. I&apos;m thinking of getting more people to join the gym. Is it alright if I invite them?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I think that&apos;s a great idea! I was actually getting a little worried about things like sales, since the number of members is so low.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ichinose readily expressed her approval, agreeing it was a good plan.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;But you don&apos;t need my permission or anything.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s not quite right. The students I&apos;m thinking of inviting are from your class, Ichinose.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Oh, really? Who are they?&quot;</p><img src="/hola-lector-agua/images/books/ss-y3-v2/OEBPS/lVhZMayuhWwmBdFdcwpCW.jpg" loading="lazy" decoding="async" alt="image"/><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;The three I&apos;m thinking of asking are Watanabe, Kanzaki, and Himeno.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I consciously relayed the names in a natural flow, so as not to hint at any particular intention with the order or combination.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And I deliberately chose not to mention Hamaguchi&apos;s name.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, it was a petty trick at best. Upon hearing the three names, Ichinose smiled.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;That&apos;s an interesting trio. Kanzaki-kun has seemed a little distant from me lately, and on the other hand, it seems he&apos;s gotten closer with Himeno-san, as the two of them have apparently been seen talking together at times. I&apos;m aware they were trying hard to do something when I was being so unreliable. If we&apos;re at the gym together, we might be able to remove that unnecessary barrier, don&apos;t you think? But in that case, wouldn&apos;t it be better to invite Hamaguchi-kun as well?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It seemed she completely read my intentions. And she had a magnificent grasp on the state of her own class.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;And Watanabe-kun simply wants to get closer to Mako-chan&#x2014;I guess that&apos;s about it, right?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Impressive. I guess it was obvious.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">While I was sincere about wanting to liven up the gym, she saw through the reasons for my choices all too easily.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I can figure that much out. But, yes, I&apos;d totally welcome them. I think Watanabe-kun will be fine as long as we just pretend not to notice his true motives, but... I wonder if Kanzaki-kun and the others will agree to join?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;They&apos;ll join. No, I believe they have to.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;You&apos;re right. It doesn&apos;t have to be the gym later on, but I&apos;ve been feeling that we really do need to unite the class in the truest sense, and sooner rather than later.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;In that case, I&apos;ll go ahead and ask them without hesitation.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Okay, thank you. I&apos;m really happy for your support, Ayanokouji-kun.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">After Ichinose, standing to my right, gave me an unreserved smile, the back of her left hand brushed against the back of my right for an instant.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Then her hand slowly drew near again and our fingertips touched and intertwined.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">However, she reflexively realized it was wrong and hastily pulled away.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Her cheeks reddened, and she averted her gaze. But her eyes slowly returned to me.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I-I&apos;m sorry... I&apos;m sorry. I just... couldn&apos;t help wanting to touch you... even though we&apos;re just friends, aren&apos;t we.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even though we knew everything about each other, our relationship was that of friends.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Even though I knew it was only a difference in a single expression, a large, insurmountable wall stood between us.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">It was easy enough to take her wavering hand.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I was sure that Ichinose herself wouldn&apos;t have minded the action.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I didn&apos;t dislike her personality, her body, or her thoughts.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">In fact, it was true that she seemed far more charming than some half-assed person.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As I stared at her, Ichinose shyly met my gaze as well.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">She also possessed the great capacity to accept me, even knowing a part of my darkness.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">And yet&#x2014;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I would have denied that my heart was being swayed by romance at that very moment.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I recalled the other day: the library after school with Hiyori and the sight of her holding the flower.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">A space enveloped in a sense of bliss, a feeling I never experienced before.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">I clearly desired what I had felt on that day, at that time&#x2014;an emotion I had no memory of experiencing.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;&#x2014;Who are you thinking about?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">As if she slipped into my thoughts through our meeting gaze, her lips moved to form those words.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;What makes you think that?&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;Because you looked so happy, I suppose.&quot;</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">Ichinose had always been skilled at reading the atmosphere and understanding people, but since that night, her perception had sharpened dramatically.</p><p class="paragraph" style="text-align: left">&quot;I&apos;m sorry, you don&apos;t have to answer.
